# Chandogya-upanisad \[BS\]

## Invocation

*oṁ śaṁ no mitraḥ śaṁ varuṇaḥ*  
*śaṁ no bhavatv aryamā*  
*śaṁ na indro bṛhaspatṁḥ*  
*śaṁ no viñṇur urukramaḥ*  
*namo brahmaṇe namaste vāyo*  
*tvam eva pratyakñaṁ brahmāsi*  
*tvām eva pratyakñaṁ brahma vadiñyāmi*  
*ṛtaṁ vadiñyāmi satyaṁ vadiñyāmi*  
*tan mām avatu tad-vaktarām avatu*  
*avatu mām avatu vaktāram*  
*om śāntiḥ śāṇtiḥ śāntiḥ*

**Перевод:**

**May the sun give us auspiciousness! May Varuṇa give us auspiciousness! May Aryamā give us auspiciousness! May Indra and Bṛhaspati give us auspiciousness! May Viñṇu, endowed with great strides, give us auspiciousness! I offer respects to the Vedas. O Vāyu, I offer respects to you. You are directly Brahman. I will call you direct Brahman. I will speak proper words and the truth. Protect me and protect the speaker. Protect me and protect the speaker. Peace, peace, peace.**

*auṁ saha nāvavatu \| saha nau bhunaktu \| saha vīryaṁ karavāvahai \| tejasvi nāvadhītamastu mā vidviṣāvahai \| auṁ śāntiḥ śāntiḥ śāntiḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**May the mantra protect us. May it nourish us. May it give us power. May our studies have power. May we not develop hatred. Peace, peace, peace.**

*oṁ āpyāyantu mamāṅgāni vāk prāṇaś cakñuḥ śrotram atho balam indriyāṇi ca. sarvāṇi sarvaṁ brahmopaniñadaṁ māhaṁ brahma nirākuryāṁ mā mā brahma nirākarod anirākaraṇam astv anirākaraṇaṁ me 'stu tadātmani nirate ya upaniñatsu dharmās te mayi santu mayi santu. oṁ śāntiḥ śāntiḥ śāntiḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**May my limbs, speech, prāṇa, eyes, ears, strength and all senses thrive. The Upaniñads say that Brahman is everything. May I not reject Brahman (the Lord) and may Brahman not reject me. May there be no rejection by Brahman. May there be no rejection by me. May all the qualities taught in the Upaniñads manifest in me, who am engaged in the Lord. May those qualities manifest in me. Peace, peace, peace.**

## Part 1

### 1

### 1.1.1

*om ity etad akñaram udgītham upāsīta, om iti hy udgāyati tasyopavyākhyānam.*

**Перевод:**

**One should worship the syllable om, part of the Sāma Veda. It is called udgītha because one chants it loudly to begin a recitation. The syllable is now explained.**

**Комментарий:**

First worship related to karma is taught as a method of attaining brahma-vidyā. One should worship the syllable oṁ which is part of the udgītham (Sāma Veda). The śruti explains why om is called udgītha. Om iti hy udgāyati: in this world, one loudly sings the syllable om in beginning a recitation. Thus it is proper to explain utgītha to mean om since it is a part of the udgītha. The syllable will be explained in terms of its worship, power and results.

### 1.1.2

*eñaṁ bhūtānāṁ pṛthivī rasaḥ pṛthivyā āpo raso 'pām oñadhayo rasa oñadīnāṁ puruño rasaḥ puruñasya vāg raso vāca ṛg rasa ṛca sāma rasaḥ sāmna udgītho rasaḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**Earth is the essence of all beings. Water is the essence of earth. Herbs (food) are the essence of water. The body is the essence of food. Speech is the essence of the body. The verses of the Veda are the essence of speech. Sāma Veda is the essence of the Vedas. Om is the essence of the Sāma Veda.**

**Комментарий:**

Earth is the essence (rasaḥ) of all moving and non-moving beings since earth supports all entities, who take shelter of earth. Water is the essence of earth since water is cause of earth. Herbs are the essence of water since they are water’s transformations. The body is the essence of herbs since it is a transformation of food (herbs). Speech is the essence of the body since it assists the body. The verses of the Vedas are the essence of speech since they have deep meaning. The Sāma Veda is the essence of the Vedas since is the best singing. The udgītha is the essence of the Sāma Veda, since it is most attractive to hear. Udgītha here means om.

### 1.1.3

*sa eña rasāṇāṁ rasa-tamaḥ paramaḥ parārdhyo 'ñṭamo yad udgīthaḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**Om is the essence of all essences. It represents Brahman. It is the eighth essence.**

**Комментарий:**

Para means Brahman and ardhya means place. Om is to be worshipped like Brahman. Yat stands for yaḥ. It is the last in the series of essences just mentioned starting with earth as the essence.

### 1.1.4

*katamā katamark katamat katamat sāma katamaḥ katama udgītha iti vimṛñṭaṁ bhavati.*

**Перевод:**

**What is the verse of the Vedas? What is the Sāma Veda? What is the udgītha, om? This was the deliberation of the ancient sages.**

**Комментарий:**

The repetition of katama katama is to show respect. It means “what.” Vimṛñṭaṁ means that the ancient sages deliberated on this.

### 1.1.5

*vāg evark prāṇaḥ sāmom ity etad akñaram udgīthaḥ. tad vā etan mithunaṁ yad vāk ca prāṇaś cark ca sāma ca.*

**Перевод:**

**Speech is the Vedic verses. Prāṇa is the Sāma Veda. Om is the udgītha. Speech and prāṇa are a couple and the verses (ṛk) and the Sāma Veda are a couple.**

**Комментарий:**

The ancient sages deliberated as follows. Speech is the verse (ṛk) since the verses can only be pronounced by the sense organ of speech. Prāṇa is the sāman since the singing can only be accomplished by breathing in and out. Om is the udgītha, since it is part of the udgītha. Vak is feminine and prāṇa is masculine. Thus they are a couple (mithunam). Similarly ṛk and sāma are a couple, being nondifferent from vāk and sāma.

### 1.1.6

*tad etan mithunam om ity etasminn akñare saṁsṛjyate yadā vai mithunau samāgacchata āpayato vai tāv anyonyasya kāmam.*

**Перевод:**

**The couple is united with om. When the couple unites, they attain their desires mutually.**

**Комментарий:**

One should meditate on om as a combination with verses and sāma veda, superimposed with speech and prāṇa. Just as when a couple unites their desires are fulfilled, when the speech and prāṇa or verses and sāma unite with oṁ , there is success in attaining the goal.

### 1.1.7

*āpayitā ha vai kāmānāṁ bhavati ya etad evaṁ vidvān akñaram udgītham upāste.*

**Перевод:**

**The priest who meditates on oṁ has all his desires fulfilled.**

**Комментарий:**

The wise person who meditates on oṁ becomes an obtainer (āyayitā) of all desires. The sponsor of a sacrifice also has his desires fulfilled.

### 1.1.8

*tad vā etad anujñākñaraṁ ya dhi kiñcānujānāty om ity eva tadāhaiño eva samṛddhir yad anujñā samardhayitā ha vai kāmānāṁ bhavati ya etad evaṁ vidvān akñaram udgītham upāste.*

**Перевод:**

**Om is a syllable of permission. When someone requests, one assents using om, which is prosperity. The sponsor becomes an increaser of desired objects. Thus the sponsor meditates on om.**

**Комментарий:**

Om is a syllable indicating permission. In this world, (iha), on gives permission (anujānāti) to a person who requests one by chanting om. One gives permission chanting om, which is prosperity (samṛddhiḥ). The result is explained. The sponsor attains increase in his desired objects.

### 1.1.9

*teneyaṁ trayī vidyā vartata om ity āśrāvayaty om iti śaṁsaty om ity udgāyaty etasyaivākñarasyāpacityā mahimnā rasena.*

**Перевод:**

**By chanting om, the rituals of the three Vedas are performed The chanter makes a person recite by first chanting om. He praises by first chanting om. He chants om to give respect to that syllable, most powerful.**

**Комментарий:**

Om is praised in order that the hearer develops enthusiasm for chanting it. By this chanting of om, the rituals prescribed in the three Vedas (trayī vidyā) are undertaken. That is further explained. The chanter makes a person recite by first chanting using om and he praises first using om. Why? He utters om first in order to give respect to that syllable endowed with greatness which is most powerful (rasena).

### 1.1.10

*tenobhau kuruto yaś caitad evaṁ veda yaś ca na veda. nānā tu vidyā cāvidyā ca yad eva vidyayā karoti śraddhayopaniñadā tad eva vīrya-vattaraṁ bhavatīti khalv etasyaivākñarasyopavyākhyānaṁ bhavati.*

**Перевод:**

**He who know and he who does not know performs rites using om. They yield different results. The rite which a person performs with knowledge of Brahma and faith in scriptures produces unobstructed results. This is the explanation of the syllable om.**

**Комментарий:**

“This knowledge is useless since we see that people perform karmas using om even without knowing about om.” It is true that both persons who know and persons who do not know perform karmas using om but they obtain different results. That rite performed chanting om with knowledge becomes very powerful, and the rite performed without knowledge is not powerful. That rite performed with knowledge, with faith in scriptures (śraddayā), with knowledge of Brahma arising from the Upanisads (upaniñadā), produces results which are not obstructed by strong karmas (vīryavattaram).

## 2 - Prāṇa as Oṁkāra

### 1.2.1

*devāsurā ha vai yatra saṁyatira ubhaye prājāpatyās tad dha devā udgītham ājahrur anenainān abhibhaviñyāma iti.*

**Перевод:**

**When the devatās and demons, both offspring of Prajāpati, were waging war, the devatās accepted omkāra, thinking that by chanting om they would win.**

**Комментарий:**

A short story is narrated in order to show the role of the chief prāṇa in om. When the devatā and demons, two types of offspring of Prajāpati, Brahmā (prājāpatyāḥ) waged war (samyetire) the devatās accepted the chanting of om, part of the udgītha, thinking that by om (anena) they would conquer the demons.

### 1.2.2

*te ha nāsikyaṁ prāṇam udgītham upāsāṁ cakrire taṁ hāsurāḥ pāpmanā vividhus tasmāt tenobhayaṁ jighrati surabhi ca durgandhi ca pāpmanā hy eña viddhaḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**The devatās meditated on om as the nose with its prāṇa. The demons connected the nose with sin. Therefore man smells pleasant and bad smells by the nose. The nose became contaminated with sin.**

**Комментарий:**

The devatās meditated on om as the nose with prāṇa, the sense organ of smell. The demons combined the nose with sin. This means they made the nose function to cause sin. Because of the nose’s connection with sin, man smells both pleasant fragrances and bad smells by the nose. This means the nose’s prāṇa became contaminated with sin.

### 1.2.3

*atha ha vācam udgītham upāsāṁ cakrire tāṁ hāsurāḥ pāpmanā vividhus tasmāt tayobhayaṁ vadati satyaṁ cānṛtaṁ ca pāpmanā hy eñā viddhā.*

**Перевод:**

**The devatās meditated on om as speech. The demons connected speech with sin. Therefore, man speaks both truth and lies. Speech became contaminated with sin.**

### 1.2.4

*atha ha cakñur udgītham upāsāṁ cakrire tad dhāsurāḥ pāpmanā vividhus tasmāt tenobhayaṁ paśyati darśanīyaṁ cādarśanīyaṁ ca pāpmanā hy etad viddham.*

**Перевод:**

**The devatās meditated on om as the eye. The demons connected with eye with sin. Thus, man sees both pleasant and impure things. The eye became contaminated with sin.**

**Комментарий:**

Adarśanīyam means impure etc.

### 1.2.5

*atha ha śrotram udgītham upāsāṁ cakrire tad dhāsurāḥ pāpmanā vividhus tasmāt tenobhayaṁ+ śṛṇoti śravaṇīyaṁ cāśravaṇīyaṁ ca pāpmanā hy etad viddham.*

**Перевод:**

**The devatās meditated on om as the ear. The demons connected the ear with sin. Thus man hears both pleasant words and words filled with sin. The ear became contaminated with sin.**

### 1.2.6

*atha ha mana udgītham upāsāṁ cakrire tad dhāsurāḥ pāpmanā vividhus tasmāt tenobhayaṁ saṅkalpayate saṅkalpanīyaṁ cāsaṅkalpanīyaṁca pāpmanā hy etad viddham.*

**Перевод:**

**The devatās meditated on om as the mind. The demons connected the mind with sin. Thus man thinks of piety and sin. The mind became contaminated with sin.**

**Комментарий:**

Asaṁkalpanīyam means sin.

### 1.2.7

*atha ha ya evāyaṁ mukhyaḥ prāṇas tam udgītham upāsāṁ cakrire taṁ hāsurāḥ ṛtvā vidadhvaṁ+sur yathāśmānam ākhaṇam ṛtvā vidhvaṁsate.*

**Перевод:**

**The devatās meditated on om as the chief prāṇa. The demons, contacting it, were destroyed, just as a lump of mud is destroyed on contacting a stone.**

### 1.2.8

*evaṁ yathāśmānam ākhaṇam ṛtvā vidhvaṁsata evaṁ haiva sa vidhvaṁsate ya evaṁ-vidi pāpaṁ kāmayate yaś cainam abhidāsati sa eño 'śmākhaṇaḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**He who desire to commit sin against the person fixed in knowledge, he who commits violence to that person, becomes destroyed like a lump of mud contacting a stone.**

**Комментарий:**

The result of this knowledge is explained.

He who desire to do sin to the person fixed in knowledge (evaṁ vidhi), who commits violence to that person, becomes destroyed like a lump of mud contacting a stone (aśmākhanaḥ).

### 1.2.9

*naivaitena surabhi na durgandhi vijānāty apahata-pāpmā hy eña tena yad aśnāti yat pibati tenetarān prāṇān avati. etam u evātanto 'vittvotkrāmati vyādadāty evāntata iti.*

**Перевод:**

**Because of the chief prāṇa, the person does not know pleasant and unpleasant odors, since the chief prāṇa is devoid of sin. Because of this, the chief prāṇa, by eating and drinking, nourishes the other senses. At death, not attaining existence by the chief prāṇa, the senses leave the body. At the time of death the mouth opens wide.**

**Комментарий:**

The distinction of the chief prāṇa from the other senses is described.

By this chief prāṇa (etena), the person does not know pleasant or unpleasant smells since that prāṇa is free of sin. Because it is free of sin (tena), the chief prāṇa by eating and drinking nourishes the other senses, unlike the other senses which only maintain themselves. All the senses like smelling gain their existence from the eating and drinking of the chief prāṇa. Thus at the time of dying (antataḥ), not gaining their existence (avittvā) because the chief prāṇa no longer eats or drinks, they leave the body. Because all the senses leave the body at the time of death (antataḥ), the mouth opens wide (vyādadāti).

### 1.2.10

*taṁ hāṅgirā udgītham upāsāṁ cakra etam anu evāṅgirasaṁ manyante 'ṅgānāṁ yad rasaḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**The sage Aṅgirā meditated on om as the chief prāṇa. Some call the chief prāṇa aṅgirasam since it is the essence of all the limbs.**

**Комментарий:**

Aṅgirā meditated on om as the chief prāṇa. Some call the chief prāṇa āṅgirāsam since it was worshipped by Aṅgirā. Since the limbs are all dependent on prāṇa, the prāṇa is called aṅgirasa, the essence of the limbs (changed from aṅgarasa). The devatās are fond of speaking indirectly (changing aṅgarasa to aṅgirasa).

### 1.2.11

*tena taṁ+ ha bṛhaspatir udgītham upāsāṁ cakra etam u eva bṛhaspatiṁ manyante vāg ghi bṛhatī tasyā eña patiḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**Because the prāṇa is the essence of the limbs, Bṛhaspati meditated on om as the chief prāṇa. Some consider the chief prāṇa to be Bṛhaspati, since speech is great, and prāṇa is its master.**

**Комментарий:**

Because it is the essence of the limbs, Bṛhastpati meditated on om as the chief prāṇa. Some consider the chief prāṇa to be Bṛhaspati. Speech is great (bṛhat) since it has the power to reveal all meaning. Since speech is dependent on the chief prāṇa the chief prāṇa is called the lord (pati) of speech which is great (bṛhat). Thus they worship the chief prāṇa as Bṛhaspati.

### 1.2.12

*tena taṁ+ hāyāsya udgītham upāsāṁ cakra etam u evāyāsyaṁ manyanta āsyād yad ayate.*

**Перевод:**

**Ayāsya meditated on om as Bṛhaspati (the chief prāṇa). Some consider the chief prāṇa to be Ayāsya since it comes out of (ayate) the mouth (āsya).**

### 1.2.13

*tena taṁ ha bako dālbhyo vidāṁ cakāra. sa ha nimiñīyānām udgātā babhūva sa ha smaibhyaḥ kāmān āgāyati.*

**Перевод:**

**Baka, the son of Dalbha, meditated on om as Ayāsya, Bṛhaspati and Aṅgirasa. He became the priest of the people of Naimiña. By singing, he fulfilled their desires.**

**Комментарий:**

Baka. the son of Dalbha, meditated on om as Ayāsya, Bṛhaspati and Aṅgirasa. He became the priest of the people of Naimiña. By singing, through the power of knowledge (om as prāṇa), he fulfilled (āgāyati) their desires for conquering enemies etc. (kāmān).

### 1.2.14

*āgātā ha vai kāmānāṁ bhavati ya etad evaṁ vidvān akñaram udgītham upāsta ity adhyātmam.*

**Перевод:**

**He who meditates on om in this way becomes the fulfiller of desires of the sponsors.**

**The meditator becomes the fulfiller of desires for the sponsors. This is the end of the mediation on om related to the senses, mind and prāṇa.**

**Комментарий:**

Adhyātma here means related to the senses, mind and prāṇa.

## 3 - Worship of the sun etc. as oṁkara

### 1.3.1

*athādhidaivataṁ ya evāsau tapati tam udgītham upāsītodyan vā eña prajābhya udgāyati udyaṁs tamo-bhayam apahanty apahantā ha vai bhayasya tamaso bhavati ya evaṁ veda.*

**Перевод:**

**Worship of Om related to the devatās is called adhidaivatam. One should meditate on Om which shines in the sky. The sun on rising seems to sing as it produces light for the people. Rising, the sun destroys fear of darkness. One who knows this becomes the destroyer of fear of darkness.**

**Комментарий:**

Worship of Om related to the devatās is called adhidaivatam. One should meditate one Om, seeing it as the sun. One should distinguish between inferior and superior forms. Worship relating Om to the sun who is the object of worship for people following karma is superior to worship the sun using Om which is simply related to karma.

The sun which is the object of meditation while chanting is similar to the chanting of Om.

Vai indicates emphasis. The sun (eñaḥ) rising (udyan), illuminating for the people, seems to sing. The syllable Om also seems to sing. Having risen, the sun removes fear produces by darkness for the people. The result of worshipping the sun while chanting Om, with the quality of removing fear of darkness is described. The person who knows this becomes the remover of fear of darkness.

### 1.3.2

*samāna u evāyaṁ cāsau coñṇo 'yam uñṇo 'sau svara itīmam ācakñate svara iti pratyāsvara ity amuṁ tasmād vā etam imam amuṁ codgītham upāsīta.*

**Перевод:**

**Prāṇa and the sun are similar. Prāṇa is warm and the sun is warm. They say that prāṇa moves. And that the sun moves and again moves. Therefore one should worship Om as prāṇa or the sun.**

**Комментарий:**

Om as prāṇa (ayam) and Om as the sun (asau) are the same. That is illustrated. Prāṇa (ayam) is warm when one breathes, and the sun (asau) is warm. They say that prāṇa is svara or continuous movement of air and that the sun is pratyasvara, going and coming, setting and rising. Prāṇa and the sun are similar in being warm and mobile. Thus one should worship Om as prāṇa or the sun since they have similar qualities.

### 1.3.3

*atha khalu vyānam evodgītham upāsīta yad vai prāṇiti sa prāṇo yad apāniti so 'pānaḥ. atha yaḥ prāṇāpānayoḥ sandhiḥ sa vyāno yo vyānaḥ sa vāk tasmād apṛāṇann anapānan vācam abhivyāharati.*

**Перевод:**

**One should meditate on Om as vyāna. Prāna is breathing out and apāna is breathing in. Vyāna is the juncture of prāṇa and apāna. Speech is vyāna since speech takes place without breathing out or breathing in.**

**Комментарий:**

Another type of worship of Om is described. One should worship Om as vyāna. What is vyāna? Prāṇa refers to air leaving through the mouth and nose. It is a function of air. Apāna is breathing in air through the mouth and nose. Vyāna is the special function between these two functions. The cause of meditating on Om as vyāna is explained. Since speech is accomplished by vyāna, speech is said to be vyāna. Speech takes place without breathing out (aprāṇan) or breathing in (anapānan), by the function of vyāna.

### 1.3.4

*yā vāk sark tasmād aprāṇann anapānann ṛcam abhivyāharati yark tat sāma tasmād aprāṇann anapānan sāma gāyati yat sāma sa udgīthas tasmād aprāṇann anapānann udgāyati.*

**Перевод:**

**Speech is the ṛk or verses of the Vedas. Therefore one utters the verses without breathing out or in. The verses are the sāma. Therefore one sings the sāma without breathing out or in.**

**Sāma is Om. Therefore one chants Om without breathing out or in.**

**Комментарий:**

Speech, the verses (ṛk), sāma and Om are one with vyāna since men perform these actions without breathing in or out.

### 1.3.5

*ato yāny anyāni vīrya-vanti karmāṇi yathāgner manthanam ājeḥ saraṇaṁ dṛḍhasya dhanuña āyamanam aprāṇann anapānaṁs tāni karoty etasya hetor vyānam evodgītham upāsīta.*

**Перевод:**

**In this world there are many actions requiring strength such as kindling fire, running a race or bending a strong bow. They are accomplished without breathing out or breathing in. For this reason one should meditate on Om as vyāna.**

**Комментарий:**

In this world, kindling fire, running a race or bending a strong bow, acts of great strength, are accomplished by vyāna. Because of this, one should worship Om as vyāna, since the act requires strength.

### 1.3.6

*atha khalūdgīthākñarāṇy upāsītodgītha iti prāṇa evot prāṇena hy uttiñṭhati vāg gīr vāco ha gira ity ācakñate 'nnaṁ tham anne hīdaṁ sarvaṁ sthitam.*

**Перевод:**

**One should worship om in its three syllables. Ut means prāṇa. One stands up because of prāṇa. Gī means speech. Thus they call speech giraḥ or words. Tha means food. Everything is sustained by food.**

**Комментарий:**

One should meditate on the three syllables of udgītha. The syllable ut is prāṇa. By prāṇa one stands up. The syllable gī means speech. Thus speech is also called giraḥ (words). Tham means food. Everything is situated in food.

### 1.3.7

*dyaur evod antarikñaṁ gīḥ pṛthivī tham āditya evod vāyur gīr agnis thaṁ+ sāma-veda evody yajur-vedo gīr ṛg-vedas thaṁ dugdhe 'smai vāg doḥaṁ yo vāco doho 'nna-vān annādo bhavati ya etāny evaṁ vidvān udgīthākñarāṇy upāsta udgītha iti.*

**Перевод:**

**Ut is the heavens, gī is the antarikña and tha is earth. Ut is the sun, gī is air and tha is fire. Ut is Sāma Veda, gī is Yajur Veda and tha is Ṛg Veda. Speech yields results to the worshipper in the form of speech. This wise man who worships Om with the syllables of udgītha attains speech, possesses food and enjoys food.**

**Комментарий:**

One can meditate in other ways on the syllables of udgītha. The syllable ut stands for heaven, the sun and the Sāma Veda. Gī stands for the antarikña, air and Yajur Veda. The syllable tha stands for earth, fire and Rg Veda. The result of this worship of the syllables of udgītha is stated. Speech yields results (doham) to the worshipper who has desires. What is the result? He attains a result in the form of speech. Another result is described. He possesses plenty of food (annavān) and can digest food well (annādaḥ)

### 1.3.8

*atha khalv āśīḥ-samṛddhir upasaraṇānīty upāsīta yena sāmnā stoñyan syāt tat sāmopadhāvet.*

**Перевод:**

**One should meditate, thinking of meditations which will cause prosperity. One should meditate on the Sāma by which one will utter praises.**

**Комментарий:**

One should meditate thinking of meditations (upasaraṇāni) which will bring many blessings. One should meditate (upadhāvet) on the sāma by which he will utter praise.

### 1.3.9

*yasyām ṛci tām ṛcaṁ yad ārñeyaṁ tam ṛñiṁ yāṁ devatām abhiñṭoñyan syāt tāṁ devatām upadhāvet.*

**Перевод:**

**One should meditate on the verse in which one will praise using a sāman. One should meditate on the sage of the mantra. One should meditate on the devatā whom one will praise.**

**Комментарий:**

One should meditate on that verse (ṛcam) in which (yasām ṛci) he will praise using a sāman. One should meditate on the respectable (ārñeyam) sage related to the sāman.

### 1.3.10

*yena cchandasā stoñyan syāt tac chanda upadhāved yena stomena stoñyamāṇaḥ syāt taṁ+ stomam upadhāvet.*

**Перевод:**

**One should meditate on the meter such as gāyatrī by which he will praise. One should meditate on the collection of verses (stoma) by which he will praise.**

**Комментарий:**

Stomas are sets of nine verses, of fifteen types.

### 1.3.11

*yāṁ diśam abhiñṭoñyan syāt tāṁ diśam upadhāvet.*

**Перевод:**

**He should meditate on the direction in which he will utter praise.**

### 1.3.12

*ātmānam antata upasṛtya stuvati kāmaṁ dhyāyann apramatto 'bhyāśo ha yad asmai sa kāmaḥ samṛddhyate yat-kāmaḥ stuvīteti.*

**Перевод:**

**After meditating as described above one should meditate on Brahman with great attention, while meditating on one’s desires. The desires with which one uttered praises will quickly be awarded to that person.**

**Комментарий:**

After meditating as described above (antataḥ) one must meditate on Brahman, while meditating on one’s desires with attention (apramattaḥ) to pronunciation and intonation.

One quickly (abhyāsaḥ) attains fulfilment of one’s desires because of praising with a certain desire (yat kāmaḥ stuvīta).

## 4 - Meditation on omkāra

### 1.4.1

*om ity etad akñaram udgītham upāsītom it hy udgāyati tasyopākhyānam.*

**Перевод:**

**One should meditate on the syllable Om. The priest sings Om. The explanation follows.**

**Комментарий:**

Explanation of meditation on omkāra is resumed after having explained the syllables of udgīṭha.

### 1.4.2

*devā vai mṛtyor bibhyatas trayīṁ vidyāṁ prāviśaṁs te chandobhir acchādayan yad ebhir ācchādayaṁ+s tac chandasāṁ chandastvam.*

**Перевод:**

**Fearing the death, the devatās resorted to the rituals of the three Vedas. They covered themselves with the Vedic mantras. The mantras are called chandas since the devatās used the mantras for covering.**

**Комментарий:**

The devatās, fearing death, performed Vedic karmas. Being afraid of death, they covered themselves with Vedic mantras (chandobhiḥ). The Vedic mantras are called cchandas since they devatās used the mantras to cover themselves (cchādaka).

### 1.4.3

*tān u tatra mṛtyur yathā matsyam udake paripaśyad evaṁ paryapaśyad ṛci sāmni yajuñi. te nu viditvordhvā ṛcaḥ sāmno yajuñaḥ svaram eva prāviśan.*

**Перевод:**

**Death watched them as they engaged in rituals of the Ṛg, sāma and Yajur Vedas, just as a person watches fish in water. Understanding death’s intentions, being purified by the three Vedas, they meditated on Om.**

**Комментарий:**

U has no meaning. Just as one sees fish in a lake, death watched the devatās engaged in karmas, and desired to catch them when they committed a mistake. Understanding what death desired to do, the devatās became purified (ūrdhvā) by the karmas of the three Vedas and began to meditate on Om (svaram praviñan).

### 1.4.4

*yadā vā ṛcam āpnoty om ity evātisvaraty evaṁ sāmaivaṁ yajur eña u svaro yad etad akñaram etad amṛtam abhayaṁ tat praviśya devā amṛtā abhayā abhavan.*

**Перевод:**

**When one recites the Rg, Sāma and Yajur verses, one chants Om. Thus Om is svara. Meditating on Om which prevents death and fear, the devatās became deathless and fearless.**

**Комментарий:**

When one recites the Rg verses one chants (atisvarati) Om. Thus Om (eñaḥ) is called svara.

Meditating on Om which prevents death and fear, the devatās became deathless and fearless.

### 1.4.5

*sa ya etad evaṁ vidvān akñaraṁ praṇauty etad evākñaraṁ svaram amṛtam abhayaṁ praviśati tat praviśya yad amṛtā devās tad amṛto bhavati.*

**Перевод:**

**Knowing this, the person praises the syllable Om and meditates on Om which is deathless. Meditating on Om, one becomes deathless like the deathless devatās.**

### 5

### 1.5.1

*atha khalu ya udgīthaḥ sa praṇavo yaḥ praṇavaḥ sa udgītha ity asau vā āditya udgītha eña praṇava om iti hy eña svarann eti.*

**Перевод:**

**What the udgātā chants is what the hotā chants. What the hotā chants is what the udgātā chants. The sun is both the udgītha and praṇava, since the sun rises while chanting Om.**

**Комментарий:**

Knowing that udgīta and Om are the same gives a result: many sons. The Om which the udgāta chants is the Om which the hotā chants. The Om chanted by the hotā is the Om chanted by the udgātā. These should be seen as one entity. Having done that one should see udgītta (that udgātā chants) and praṇava (Om) as the sun. Why? Giving permission to perform obligatory karmas by saying Om (svaran), the sun rises (eti). This means the sun is both praṇava and udgītha.

### 1.5.2

*etam u evāham abhyāgamiñaṁ tasmān mama tvam eko 'sīti ha kauñītakiḥ putram uvāca raśmīṁs tvaṁ paryāvartayād bahavo vai te bhaviñyantīty adhidaivatam.*

**Перевод:**

**“I understood Om as the sun and from that obtained only one son, you.” Kauñītaki said this to his son. You should therefore meditate on Om as the sun with many rays. You will have many sons. This is the teaching concerning Om as the sun.**

**Комментарий:**

I understood (abhyāgamiñam) Om as the sun but did not meditate on Om as the sun endowed with many rays. Because of that fault, I had only one son, you. Kauñītaki said this to his son. Therefore you should meditate with knowledge of the sun endowed with many rays (paryāvartayāt). And you will have many sons, by the power of that knowledge of the sun with many rays. This was taught as the meditation on Om as the sun deity.

### 1.5.3

*athādhyātmaṁ ya evayaṁ mukhyaḥ prāṇas tad ugītham upāsītom iti hy eña svarann eti.*

**Перевод:**

**Now the meditation on Om as prāṇa is taught. One should meditate on the chief prāṇa as udgītha or Om, since the chief prāṇa functions, using Om to permit the senses to function.**

**Комментарий:**

Now the meditation concerning adhyātma (body) will be taught (athyādhyātmam). One should worship udgītha (om) as the chief prāṇa since this prāṇa (eñaḥ) moves about (eti), giving permission for the functions of speech and other senses (svaran) with the word Om. Thus prāṇa is the udgītha or Om.

### 1.5.4

*etam u evāham abhyāgamiñaṁ tasmān mama tvam eko 'sīti ha kauñītakiḥ putram uvāca prāṇāṁs tvaṁ bhūmānam abhigāyatād bahavo vai me bhaviñyantīti.*

**Перевод:**

**I understood Om as the chief prāṇa and from that obtained only one son, you.” Kauñītaki said this to his son. Thinking “I will have many sons” you should meditate on Om as the chief prāṇa along with the many senses.**

**Комментарий:**

Thinking “I will have many sons” you should chant Om while meditating the many senses such as voice (prāṇān bhūmānam) along with the chief prāṇa.

### 1.5.5

*atha khalu ya udgīthaḥ sa praṇavo yaḥ praṇavaḥ sa udgītha iti hotṛ-sadanād dhaivāpi durudgītam anusamāharatīty anusamāharatīti.*

**Перевод:**

**The udgīta is praṇava. Praṇava is udgītha. The udgātā corrects the fault in his chanting by the correct knowledge of the hotā.**

**Комментарий:**

The result of seeing udgītha and prāṇa as one is described. By the hotā’s knowledge of ones of udgītha and praṇava, the hotā removes the fault of udgītha chanted with lack of knowledge.

### 6

### 1.6.1

*iyam evark agnis sāma tad etad etasyām ṛcy adhyūḍhaṁ sāma tasmād ṛcy adhyūḍhaṁ sāma gīyata iyam eva sāgnir amas tat sāma.*

**Перевод:**

**Earth is Ṛk and fire is Sāma. Sāma is situated in Ṛg. Therefore sāma is sung as being placed on Ṛg. Earth is sā and fire is ama. This forms sāma.**

**Комментарий:**

Worship of udgītha in another way is praised. One should see Ṛg as earth and Sāma as fire.

Sāma (tat sāma) in the form of fire (etat) is situated on (adhyuḍham) Rg in the form of earth (etasyām). Thus sāma is sung as being situated on Ṛk even today. Seeing ṛg and sāma as earth and fire, one should see earth and fire in the word sāma: sa and ama (iyam eva sā agniḥ amaḥ). Thus word sāma is used in this world (tat sāma), joining earth and fire in the syllables sā and ama.

### 1.6.2

*antarikñam evarg vāyuḥ sāma tad etad etasyām ṛcy adhyūḍhaṁ sāma tasmād ṛcy adhyūḍhaṁ+ sāma gīyate 'ntarikñam eva sā vāyur amas tat sāma.*

**Перевод:**

**Antarikña is Ṛk and air is sāma. Sāma is situated in Ṛg. Therefore sāma is sung as situated in Ṛk. Antarikña is sā and air is ama. This forms sāma.**

### 1.6.3

*dyaur evarg ādityaḥ sāma tad etad etasyām ṛcy adhyūḍhaṁ sāma tasmād ṛcy adhyūḍhaṁ+ sāma gīyate dyaur eva sādityo 'mas tat sāma.*

**Перевод:**

**Heaven is Ṛk the sun is sāma. Sāma is situated in Ṛg. Sāma is sung as situated in Ṛk. Heaven is sā and the sun is ama. This forms sāma.**

### 1.6.4

*nakñatrāṇy evark candramāḥ sāma tad etad etasyām ṛcy adhyūḍhaṁ sāma tasmād ṛcy adhyūḍhaṁ+ sāma gīyate nakñatrāṇy eva sā candramā amas tat sāma.*

**Перевод:**

**The constellations are Ṛk and the moon is sāma. Sāma is situated in Ṛg. Thus sāma is sung as situated in Ṛg. The constellations are sā and the moon is ama. This makes sāma.**

### 1.6.5

*atha yad etad ādityasya śuklaṁ bhāḥ saivarg atha yan nīlaṁ paraḥ kṛñṇaṁ tat sāma tad etad etasyām ṛcy adhyūḍhaṁ sāma tasmād ṛcy adhyūḍhaṁ+ sāma gīyate.*  
*atha yad etad ādityasya śuklaṁ bhāḥ saiva sātha yan nīlaṁ paraḥ kṛñṇaṁ tad amas tat sāmātha*

**Перевод:**

**The white effulgence of the sun is Ṛk. The great darkness which is blue-black is Sāma. The sāma is situated in the Ṛg. Therefore sāma is sung as being situated in the Ṛg. The white light of the sun is sā and the great darkness which is blue-black is ama. This forms sāma.**

**Комментарий:**

Paras means extreme.

### 1.6.6

*ya eño 'ntar-āditye hiraṇmayaḥ puruño dṛśyate hiraṇya-śmaśrur hiraṇya-keśa a-praṇakhāt sarva eva suvarṇaḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**He who is within the sun, the radiant puruña is seen by the yogīs. He has radiant beard, radiant hair and is resplendent in all parts of his form up to his toe nails.**

**Комментарий:**

Seeing Ṛg as earth, antarikña, heaven, constellations, the sun’s white effulgence, and seeing the sāman as fire, air, sun, moon, and the sun’s blue effulgence, the yogīs see the attractive Lord (puruñaḥ). The word hiraṇmayaḥ means effulgent rather than gold, to conform to other śruti statements describing the Lord such as nīla-toyada: he is blue like a rain cloud (Nārāyaṇa-sukta).

### 1.6.7

*tasya yathā kapyāsaṁ puṇḍarīkam evam akñiṇī tasyod iti nāma sa eña sarvebhyaḥ pāpmabhya uditaḥ udeti ha vai sarvebhyaḥ pāpmabhyo ya evaṁ veda.*

**Перевод:**

**He has eyes like a lotus which rises from the water on its stem and blossoms. His name is ut since he is free of all sins and karmas. He who knows this becomes free of all sins.**

**Комментарий:**

Kapi means “one who drinks water” and refers to the sun. āsa means to make blossom. Thus kayāsam means that which is made to blossom by the sun and thus possesses beauty. Or kapi means a stem which drinks water. Kapyāsam means that which is situated (asa) on the stalk. This interpretation indicates that the lotus is honored for arising from the mud and blossoming without being picked. Or kapyāsam means situated in the water. Thus the Lord has eyes wide and pure like the petal of a lotus which, arising from deep in the water, situated on a stem, blossoms in the sun. The vulgar explanation that the Lord’s lotus eyes are like the back side of a monkey is rejected.

He is called ut. The reason is given. He is called ut because he free of all sins. This means that the Lord is free of any trace of being controlled by karma. The Lord performs actions but does not accrue karma for those actions. The result of this knowledge is shown. He who knows this becomes free of all sins.

### 1.6.8

*tasyark ca sāma ca geñṇau tasmād udgīthas tasmāt tv evodgātaitasya hi gātā sa eña ye cāmuñmāt parāñco lokās teñāṁ ceñṭe deva-kāmānāṁ cety adhidaivatam.*

**Перевод:**

**The ṛks and sāmans are forms of song concerning the Lord. Thus ṛk is called utgītha (song about the Lord), and the chanter is called udgāthā, he who sings about the Lord. The supreme Lord is above the sun planet where the devatās have enjoyable objects. The meditation on the Lord has been taught.**

**Комментарий:**

The ṛks and sāmans, as earth, fire etc. are forms of song (geñnau). Actually the Ṛks (Ṛg Veda mantras) are not sung, but being nondifferent from sāmans (Sāma Veda mantras), which are sung, they are here said to be songs. Having stated that they are earth, fire etc. they are said to be everything. Therefore ut (the Lord), taking the form of song, is the udgītha. One who sings about Ut, the Lord is called the udgātā. Or udgītha means the Lord, since ut means the Lord and he is the subject of song in the Ṛg and Sāma (gītha). The radiant Lord (hiraṇmayaḥ puruñaḥ, sa eñaḥ) is the controller of the planets above the sun and the places of enjoyment and means of enjoyment of the devatās.

## 7 - Meditation on parts of the body

### 1.7.1

*athādhyātmaṁ vāg evark prāṇaḥ sāma tad etad etasyām ṛcy adhyūḍhaṁ sāma tasmād ṛcy adhyūḍhaṁ sāma gīyate. vāg eva sā prāṇo 'mas tat sāma.*

**Перевод:**

**Now meditation concerning the self is taught. Speech is the Ṛk. Prāṇa is the sāma. The sāma is situated in the Ṛk. Therefore the Sāma is sung situated in the Ṛk. Speech is sā and prāṇa is ama. Their combination is sāma.**

### 1.7.2

*cakñur evarg ātmā sāma tad etad etasyām ṛcy adhyūḍhaṁ sāma tasmād ṛcy adhyūḍhaṁ sāma gīyate. cakñur eva sātmāmas tat sāma.*

**Перевод:**

**The eye is the Ṛk. The reflection of the self (not the soul) is the sāma. The sāma is situated in the Ṛk the sāma is sung situated in the Ṛk. The eye is sā and the self is ama. Their combination is sāma**

### 1.7.3

*śrotram evarṅ manaḥ sāma tad etad asyām ṛcy adhyūḍhaṁ+ sāma tasmād ṛcy adhyūḍhaṁ+ sāma gīyate. śrotram eva sā mano 'mas tat sāma.*

**Перевод:**

**The ear is the Ṛk. Mind is the sāma. Therefore the sāma is situated in the Ṛk the sāma is sung situated in the Ṛk. The ear is sā and the mind is ama. Their combination is sāma.**

### 1.7.4

*atha yad etad akñṇaḥ śuklaṁ bhāḥ saivarg atha yan nīlaṁ paraḥ kṛñṇaṁ tat sāma*  
*tad etad asyām ṛcy adhyūḍhaṁ+ sāma tasmād ṛcy adhyūḍhaṁ+ sāma gīyate. atha yad etaitad akñṇaḥ śuklaṁ bhāḥ saiva sātha yan nīlaṁ paraḥ kṛñṇaṁ tad amas tat sāma.*

**Перевод:**

**White light in the eye is the Ṛk. The black darkness is sāma. The sāma is situated in the Ṛk. The white light of the eye is sā. The very dark blue is ama. Their combination is sāma.**

**The sāma is sung situated in the Ṛk.**

### 1.7.5

*atha ya eño 'ntar-akñiṇi puruño dṛśyate saivark tat sāma tad ukthaṁ tad yajus tad brahma tasyaitasya tad eva rūpaṁ yad amuñya rūpaṁ yāv amuñya geñṇau tau geñṇau yan nāma tan nāma.*

**Перевод:**

**The person seen by the yogī within the eye is Ṛk. He is sāma. He is uktha. He is the Yajur Veda. He is Brahman. The form in the sun is this form in the eye. The songs for the person in the sun are the songs for the person in the eye. The name of that person is the name of this person.**

### 1.7.6

*sa eña ye caitasmād arvāñco lokās teñāṁ ceñṭe manuñya-kāmānāṁ ceti tad ya ime vīṇāyāṁ gāyanty etaṁ te gāyanti tasmāt te dhana-sanayaḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**The Lord in the eye rules over the lower planets and the enjoyments of humans. Those who sing on the vīṇā sing about this Lord. Because of that, they attain all wealth.**

**Комментарий:**

Since the Lord is the ātmā of all beings, devatā and humans sing about him alone.

### 1.7.7

*atha ya etad evaṁ vidvān sāma gāyaty ubhau sa gāyati so 'munaiva sa eña ye cāmuñmāt parāñco lokās tāṁś cāpnoti deva-kāmāṁś ca.*

**Перевод:**

**He who knowing this sings a sāman, sings to the Lord in the sun and the Lord in the eye. By this worship which includes of the Lord in the sun, he attains all the worlds above and the enjoyment of the devatās.**

**Комментарий:**

He knows that the Lord in the sun is the same as the Lord in the eye. The result is described.

### 1.7.8

*athānenaiva ye caitasmād arvāñco lokās tāṁ+ś cāpnoti manuñya-kāmā+ś ca tasmād u haivaṁ-vid udgātā brūyāt.*

**Перевод:**

**By this worship which includes worship of the Lord in the eye, he attains all the lower worlds and the enjoyment of humans. The udgātā endowed with this knowledge should speak to the sponsor.**

**Комментарий:**

By worship of the Lord situated in the sun and the eye, one attains all worlds.

### 1.7.9

*kaṁ te kāmam āgāyānīty eña hy eva kāmāgānasyeñṭe ya evaṁ vidvān sāma gāyati sāma gāyati.*

**Перевод:**

**“Shall I supply you the enjoyments of the devatās or humans by singing?” Knowing this, he is capable of fulfilling the desires of the sponsor through his singing.**

### 8

### 1.8.1

*trayo hodgīthe kuśalā babhūvuḥ śilakaḥ śālavatyaś caikitāyano dālbhyaḥ pravāhaṇo jaivalir iti, te hocur udgīthe vai kuśalāḥ smo hantodgīthe kathāṁ vadāma iti.*

**Перевод:**

**Śilāka, son of Śālāva, a person of the Dālbha gotra, who was the son of Cikitāyana, and Pravāhaṇa, son of Jīvala, were skillful in knowledge of udgītha. They said, “We are skillful at udgītha. Let us discuss together about udgītha.”**

**Комментарий:**

A story is told to introduce another type of worship. Let us discuss for gaining expertise in knowledge.

### 1.8.2

*tatheti ha samupaviviśuḥ, sa ha pravāhaṇo jaivalir uvāca, bhagavan tāv agre vadatāṁ brāhmaṇyor vadator vācaṁ+ śroñyāmīti.*

**Перевод:**

**Agreeing they sat down. Pravāhana, son of Javali, said, “O worshippable ones! First you two speak. I will hear the discussion of two brāhmaṇas.”**

**Комментарий:**

Pravāhana was a kñatriya. He thus spoke with respect to the two brāhmaṇas.

### 1.8.3

*sa ha śilakaḥ śālāvatyaś caikitāyanaṁ dālbhyam uvāca hanta tvā pṛcchāni, pṛccheti hovāca.*

**Перевод:**

**Śilaka son of Śālāva said to Dālbha, son of Cikitāyana, “Let me ask you.” Dālbhya said, “Please ask.”**

### 1.8.4

*kā sāmno gatir iti, svara iti hovāca, svarasya kā gatir iti, prāṇa iti hovāca, prāṇasya kā gatir ity annam iti hovācānnasya kā gatir ity āpa iti hovāca.*

**Перевод:**

**What is the shelter of sāma?**

**Svara is the shelter.**

**What is the shelter of svara?**

**Prāṇa is the shelter.**

**What is the shelter of prāṇa?**

**Food is the shelter.**

**What is the shelter of food?**

**Water is the shelter.**

### 1.8.5

*apāṁ kā gatir ity asau loka iti hovācāmuñya lokasya kā gatir iti na svargaṁ lokam atinayed iti hovāca, svargaṁ vayaṁ lokaṁ sāmābhisaṁsthāpayāmaḥ svarga-saṁsthāvaṁ hi sāmeti.*

**Перевод:**

**What is the shelter of water?**

**That planet is the shelter. Thus he spoke.**

**What is the shelter of that planet?**

**He said, “Sāma does not lead one beyond Svarga. We firmly establish Svarga through the sāma. Sāma is praise of Svarga.”**

**Комментарий:**

The heavens shower rain. Thus that planet is the shelter of water. Other than Svarga there is nothing higher. One should not speak of anything beyond this. Svargo vai lokaḥ sāma-vedaḥ: Sāma Veda is verily Svarga-loka. (Ṣad-viṁśa-brāhmaṇa)

### 1.8.6

*taṁ ha śilakaḥ śālāvatyaś caikitāyanaṁ dālbhyam uvācāpratiñṭhitaṁ vai kila te dālbhya sāma yas tv etarhi brūyān mūrdhā te vipatiñyatīti mūrdhā te vipated iti.*

**Перевод:**

**Śilaka, son of Śālava said to Dālbhya, son of Cikitāyana, “O Dālbhya! Your idea is not well founded. If someone were to say “Your head will fall off” your head would fall off.**

**Комментарий:**

Dālbhya thought that the final goal of the sāma was Svarga. Your head would fall off, but I will not say that. You should live.

### 1.8.7

*hantāham etad bhagavato vedānīti, viddhīti hovācāmuñya lokasya kā gatir ity ayaṁ loka iti hovācāsya lokasya kā gatir iti pratiñṭhāṁ lokam atinayed iti hovāca, pratiñṭhāṁ vayaṁ lokaṁ sāmābhisaṁsthāpayāmaḥ pratiñṭhā-saṁstāvaṁ hi sāmeti.*

**Перевод:**

**Let me know from you.**

**Sīlaka said, “Please understand.”**

**What is the shelter of Svarga?**

**This world is the shelter of Svarga.**

**What is the shelter of this world?**

**The sāman cannot lead you to a shelter other than this world. We establish this world through the sāma. The sāman is praised as this world.”**

### 1.8.8

*taṁ+ ha pravāhaṇo jaivalir uvācānta-vad vai kila te śālāvatya sāma yas tv etarhi brūyān mūrdhā te vipatiñyatīti mūrdhā te vipated iti hantāham etad bhagavato vedānīti, viddhīti hovāca.*

**Перевод:**

**Pravāhana, son of Javali said to Śīlaka, “O Śīlaka! Your knowledge is limited. If a person says that your head will fall off, your head will fall off.”**

**Śilaka said, “Let me know from you, respected person!”**

**Pravāhana said, “Please understand.”**

### 9

### 1.9.1

*asya lokasya kā gatir ity ākāśa iti hovāca sarvāṇi ha vā imāni bhūtāny ākāśād eva samutpadyanta ākāśaṁ praty astaṁ yanty ākāśo hy evaibhyo jyāyān ākāśaḥ parāyaṇam.*

**Перевод:**

**Śilaka said, “What is the shelter of this world?”**

**Pravāhana said, “Ākāña is the shelter of this world. All beings arise from ākāśa and disappear in ākāśa. Ākāśa is superior to all else. Ākāśa is the ultimate shelter.”**

**Комментарий:**

What is the shelter of earth? Ākāsa means Brahman, the supreme Lord. He is superior to all else because of having all auspicious qualities.

### 1.9.2

*sa eña paro-varāyān udgīthaḥ sa eño 'nantaḥ paro-varīyo hāsya bhavati paro-varīyaso ha lokāñ jayati ya etad evaṁ vidvān paro-varīyāṁ samudgītham āste.*

**Перевод:**

**The udgītha concerning the Lord (ākāśa) is most praiseworthy among the praiseworthy.**

**That ākāśa is unlimited. The person who knows the excellent Lord and meditates on the udgītha in this way becomes most praiseworthy among the praiseworthy. He attains most praiseworthy planets.**

**Комментарий:**

The sāma finally takes shelter of the Lord. The earth was pointed out to be antavat: temporary. The Lord does not have this fault.

### 1.9.3

*taṁ haitam atidhanvā śaunaka udara-śāṇḍilyāyoktvovāca yāvat ta enaṁ prajāyām udgīthaṁ vediñyante paro-varīyo haibhyas tāvad asmil loke jīvanaṁ bhaviñyati.*

**Перевод:**

**Atidhanva, son of Śunaka, spoke this knowledge to Udara-śāṇḍilya and then said, “Those in your lineage who learn this udgītha will have life on earth while being more praiseworthy than the praiseworthy.”**

### 1.9.4

*tathāmuñmil loke loke iti sa etad evaṁ vidvān upāste paro-varīya eva hāsyāsmil+ loke jīvanaṁ bhavati tathāmuñmil+ loke loka iti loke loka iti.*

**Перевод:**

**They will also be praiseworthy in the next life. He who knows this and meditates in this way, even at the present time will have prosperity in this life and in the next life.**

### 10

### 1.10.1

*maṭacī-hateñu kuruñv āṭikyā saha jāya-yoñastir hi cākrāyaṇa ibhya-grāme pradāṇaka uvāsa.*

**Перевод:**

**Uñasti, the son of Cakra, lived in a miserable condition without food with his wife, who had not yet matured in Kuru, which was afflict with famine because of hail storms, in a village of elephant keepers.**

**Комментарий:**

A story is told to explain worship related to the prastāva, udgītha and pratihāra. A sage named Uñasti, the son of Cakra, lived with his wife, who had not yet matured (āṭikyā) in Kuru, which was afflict with famine because of hail storms, in a village of elephant keepers(ibhya-grame), in a miserable condition without food (pradrāṇakaḥ). He desired to live only attain brahma-vidyā.

### 1.10.2

*sa hebhyaṁ kulmāñān khādantaṁ bibhikñe taṁ hovāca. neto 'nye vidyante yac ca ye ma ima upanihitā iti.*

**Перевод:**

**He begged from an elephant driver who was eating low grade grains. “There is nothing left except the remnants of what I have eaten which I have placed here.”**

### 1.10.3

*eteñāṁ me dehīti hovāca, tān asmai pradadau hantānupānam ity ucchiñṭaṁ vai me pītaṁ syād iti hovāca.*

**Перевод:**

**Uñasti said, “Give me some of the leftovers.” The elephant driver gave him the leftovers. When the driver offered water, Uñasti said, “This will mean that I am drinking leftovers.”**

### 1.10.4

*na svid ete 'py ucchiñṭā iti na vā ajīviñyā imān akhādann iti hovāca kāmo ma udaka-pānam iti.*

**Перевод:**

**The driver said, “Are these grains not leftovers?” Uñasti said, “Without eating the grains I would not be able to live. Accepting drinking water would be motivated by desire.”**

### 1.10.5

*sa ha khāditvātiśeñāñ jāyāyā ājahāra sāgra eva su-bhikñā babhūva tān pratigṛhya nidadhau.*

**Перевод:**

**Having eaten, he gave the remnants to his wife. She had previously eaten. She accepted the grains and stored them.**

### 1.10.6

*sa ha prātaḥ sañjihāna uvāca yad batānnasya labhemahi labhemahi dhana-mātrāṁ+ rājāsau yakñyate sa mā sarvair ārtvijyair vṛṇīteti.*

**Перевод:**

**Waking in the morning he said to his wife, “If I could obtain a little food, then maintaining my life, I should be able to obtain some wealth. The kinging is performing a sacrifice. He should choose me for performing the duties of the priest.”**

### 1.10.7

*taṁ jāyovāca hanta pate ima eva kulmāñā iti tān khāditvāmuṁ yajñaṁ vitatam eyāya.*

**Перевод:**

**His wife said, “Dear husband! Here are the grains.” Eating them, he went to the great sacrifice.**

**Комментарий:**

Here are the grains that you put in my hands. Please eat them. Eating the stale, leftover grains, he went to the great sacrifice.

### 1.10.8

*tatrodgātṝn āstāve stoñyamāṇān upopaviveśa, sa ha prastotāram uvāca.*

**Перевод:**

**He sat near the udgātās who were about to sing at the āstāva (place for chanting). He spoke to the prastotāra (assistant of the udgātā, who chants the prastāva).**

**Комментарий:**

The udgātās consisted of the udgātās, prastotās and the pratihartas. He sat near them as they were about to start chanting. He spoke to the prastotā.

### 1.10.9

*prastotaryā devatā prastāvamanv āyattā tāṁ ced avidvān prastoñyasi mūrdhā te vipatiñyatīti.*

**Перевод:**

**O prastotā! If you chant without knowing the worshippable devatā to be superimposed when chanting the prastāva, your head will fall off.**

**Комментарий:**

Your head will fall off while chanting in my presences, since I know the devatā to be worshipped. Though person in ignorance may be qualified as a priest, in the presence of a person who knows, he loses his qualification.

### 1.10.10

*evam evodgātāram uvācodgātaryā devatodgītha-manvāyattā tāṁ ced avidvān udgāsyasi mūrdhā te vipatiñyatīti.*

**Перевод:**

**In the same way he spoke to the udgātā. “O udgātā! If you chant the udgītha without knowing the worshippable deity, your head will fall off.”**

### 1.10.11

*evam eva pratihartāram uvāca, pratihartar yā devatā pratihāram anvāyattā tāṁ ced avidvān pratihariñyasi mūrdhā te vipatiñyatīti, te ha samāratās tūñṇīm āsāṁ cakrire.*

**Перевод:**

**In the same way he spoke to the pratihartā. “O pratihartā! If you chant the pratihara without knowing the worshippable deity, your head will fall off.”**

**Комментарий:**

All of them gave up the attempt to chant and remained silent, afraid of their heads falling off.

### 11

### 1.11.1

*atha hainaṁ yajamāna uvāca, bhagavantaṁ vā ahaṁ vividiñāṇīty uñastir asmi cākrāyaṇa iti hovāca.*

**Перевод:**

**The sponsor said to Uñasti, “I desire to know your honored self.” He said, “I am Uñasti, son of Cakra.”**

### 1.11.2

*sa hovāca, bhagavantaṁ vā aham ebhiḥ sarvair ārtvijyaiḥ paryaiñiñaṁ bhagavato vā aham avidyā ‘n ān avṛñi.*

**Перевод:**

**The sponsor said, “I searched for you, for performing all the rites. Not attaining you, I accepted others.”**

### 1.11.3

*bhagavāṁs tv eva me sarvair ārtvijyair iti tathety atha tarhy eta eva samatisṛñṭāḥ stuvatāṁ yāvat tebhyo dhanaṁ dadyās tāvan mama dadyā iti, tatheti ha yajamāna uvāca.*

**Перевод:**

**The sponsor said, “I accept you for performing all the rites.” Uñasti said, “I agree. Let these priests, being well instructed sing praises. You should give me as much wealth as you give to them.” The sponsor agreed.**

**Комментарий:**

The priests chosen by you and permitted by me after I am satisfied, having taught them about the devatās, should sing the praises.

### 1.11.4

*atha hainaṁ prastotopasasāda, prastotar yā devatā prastāvam anvāyattā tāṁ ced avidvān prastoñyasi mūrdhā te vipatiñyatīti, mā bhagavān avocat katamā sā devateti.*

**Перевод:**

**The prastotā approached Uñasti. “Dear sir, you said that if I chanted while not knowing the devatā of the prastāva, my head would fall off. Who is that devatā?”**

### 1.11.5

*prāṇa iti hovāca, sarvāṇi ha vā imāni bhūtāni prāṇam evābhisaṁviśanti prāṇam abhyujjihate saiñā devatā prastāvam anvāyattā tāṁ ced avidvān prāstoñyo mūrdhā te vyapatiñyat tathoktasya mayeti.*

**Перевод:**

**Uñasti said, “The devatā is prāṇa. All beings enter into prāṇa and emerge from prāṇa. If you chant the prastāva without knowing this deity of the prastāva, your head will fall off. This I have said.”**

**Комментарий:**

Prāṇa means the supreme Lord since he alone gives life to all beings. That is made clear by the statement that all beings enter into prāṇa. All beings merge (saṁviśanti) into (abhi) prāṇa and emerge from prāṇa. The Lord is famous in Vedānta for this quality. If you had not shown respect and chanted without knowing, your head would have fallen off. But you did well to approach me and ask with humility.

### 1.11.6

*atha hainam udgātopasasādodgātar yā devatodgītham anvāyattā tāṁ ced avidvān udgāsyasi mūrdhā te vipatiñyatīti, mā bhagavān avocat katamā sā devateti.*

**Перевод:**

**The udgātā approached Uñasti. “Dear sir, you said that if I chanted while not knowing the devatā of the udgītha, my head would fall off. Who is that devatā?”**

### 1.11.7

*āditya iti hovāca, sarvāṇi ha vā imāni bhūtāny ādityam uccaiḥ santaṁ gāyanti saiñā devatodgītham anvāyattā tāṁ ced avidvān udagāsyo mūrdhā te vyapatiñyat tathoktasya mayeti.*

**Перевод:**

**The sun is the deity to be imposed on the udgītha. All beings sing about the sun situated high above. If you chant the udgītha without knowing this deity of the udgītha, your head will fall off. This I have said.**

**Комментарий:**

Pra is the syllable common to prastāva and prāṇa. Similarly the syllable ut is common to udgītha and uccaiḥ santam.

### 1.11.8

*atha hainaṁ pratihartopasasāda, partihartar yā devatā pratihāram anvāyattā tāṁ ced avidvān pratihariñyasi mūrdhā te vipatiñyatīti, mā bhagavān avocat katamā sā devateti.*

**Перевод:**

**The pratihartā approached Uñasti. “Dear sir, you said that if I chanted while not knowing the devatā of the pratihāra, my head would fall off. Who is that devatā?”**

### 1.11.9

*annam iti hovāca, sarvāṇi ha vā imāni bhūtāny annam eva pratiharamāṇāni jīvanti saiñā devatā pratihāram anvāyattā tāṁ ced avidvān pratyahariñyo mūrdhā te vyapatiñyat tathoktasya mayeti tathoktasya mayeti.*

**Перевод:**

**Food is the devatā for the pratihāra. All beings live by eating food. If you chant the pratihāra without knowing this deity of the pratihāra your head will fall off. This I have said.**

**Комментарий:**

Pratiharamānāni means “being eaten.” The word prati is common to pratiharamānāni and pratihāra. One should impose the deity of food on the pratihāra.

## 12 - Dog’s Ugītha

### 1.12.1

*athātaḥ śauva udgīthas tad dha bako dālbhyo glāvo vā maitreyaḥ svādhyāyam udvavrāja.*

**Перевод:**

**Now the topic of udgītha seen by dogs is introduced. The son of Dalbhya and Mitrā, who was named Baka or Glāva, left the village to study.**

**Комментарий:**

Now the topic of udgītha seen by dogs is introduced. Vā means and. The son of Dalbhya and Mitrā, who was named Baka or Glāva, left the village (udvavrāja) to study.

### 1.12.2

*tasmai śvā śvetaḥ prādurbabhūva, tam anye śvāna upasametyocur annaṁ no bhagavān āgāyatv aśanāyāma vā iti.*

**Перевод:**

**A white dog appeared in front of Baka. Other doges approached the white dog and said, “Dear sir! Please sing to supply us with food. We are hungry.”**

**Комментарий:**

A sage, pleased with Baka’s study, appeared in the form of a white dog. Other miserable dogs approached the white dog. Please supply us with food by singing.

### 1.12.3

*tān hovācehaiva mā prātar upasamīyāteti, tad dha bako dālbhyo glāvo vā maitreyaḥ pratipālayaṁ cakāra.*

**Перевод:**

**The white dog said, “Meet me at this place in the morning.” Baka or Glāva stayed, waiting for the white dog and the other dogs to come.**

### 1.12.4

*te ha yathaivedaṁ bahiñ-pavamānena stoñyamāṇāḥ saṁrabdhāḥ sarpantīty evam āsasṛpus te ha samupaviśya hiṁ cakruḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**Just as the priests chanting the bahiñ-pavamāna verses move about holding each other’s cloth, so the dogs move around holding each other’s tail. They then sat down and began chanting.**

**Комментарий:**

The white dog and the others came in the morning. Just as the udgātā and others chanting the bahiñpavamāna verses move about while touching each other, the dogs, taking the tail of another dog in their mouths, moved about. They sat down and began chanting (hiṁ cakruḥ).

### 1.12.5

*oṁ adam oṁ pibāma oṁ devo varuṇaḥ prajāpatiḥ savitā nnam ihāharad anna-pate ‘nnam ihā ‘ahara ‘haro ‘m iti.*

**Перевод:**

**Om. May we eat. Om. May we drink. May the shining Lord, the Lord who is hidden, the Lord who protects, who is the sun, bring food, here. O lord of food (sun)! Bring food here, bring food.**

**Комментарий:**

The dogs sang in this manner. The last phrase is repeated as a prayer. While meditating on the sun through the udgītha one attains food as a result.

## 13 - Explanation of Stobhas

### 1.13.1

*ayaṁ vāva loko hā-u-kāro vāyur hā-i-kāraś candramā atha-kāra ātmeha-kāro 'gnir ī-kāraḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**The stobha of hāu should be worshipped as the world. The syllable hāu in the stobha should be worshipped as air. The word atha in the stobha should be worshipped as the moon. The word iha should be worshipped as the ātmā. The word ī should be worshipped as fire.**

**Комментарий:**

The different types of worship related to the stobha (interjections included in Sāma Veda) which part of the sāma are described. The stobha hāu should be worshipped as the world. This is well known in the rathantara sāman. Hāu is well known in the vāmadeva sāman.

### 1.13.2

*āditya u-kāro nihava e-kāro viśve-devā auho-i-kāraḥ prajāpatir hiṁ-kāraḥ prāṇaḥ svaro 'nnaṁ yā vāg virāṭ.*

**Перевод:**

**The stobha u is the sun. The stobha e is calling out. Auhoi is the viśvadevas. Him is Prajāpati. The stobha svara is prāṇa. The stobha yā is food. The stobha vāk is the universal form.**

**Комментарий:**

Virāṭ means universal form or food.

### 1.13.3

*Anirukta trayodaśaḥ stobhāh sañcaro huḥkāraḥ*

**Перевод:**

**The thirteenth stobha hum which moves about is indescribable.**

**Комментарий:**

Because it cannot be defined clearly huṁ is said to waver. One should meditate on its wavering nature while chanting hum. One should meditate on the thirteen items while chanting the thirteen stobhas.

### 1.13.4

*dugdhe 'smai vāg dohaṁ yo vāco doho 'nna-vān annādo bhavati ya etām evaṁ sāmnām upaniñadaṁ vedopaniñadaṁ veda iti.*

**Перевод:**

**Speech will grant him results in the form of speech. He who knows this secret meaning of the stobhas, will be the possessor of food and the enjoyer of food.**

## Part 2

## 1 - Meditation on the whole Sāma

### 2.1.1

*oṁ samastasya khalu sāmna upāsanaṁ sādhu yat khalu sādhu tat sāmety ācakñate yad asādhu yad asāmeti.*

**Перевод:**

**One should meditate on Om as the whole sāma. Proper actions are called sāma and improper actions are called asāma.**

**Комментарий:**

Having explained meditation of limbs of the sāma, now meditation on the whole of the sāman is praised. One should perform proper meditation on the whole of the sāma—sāma with five types of knowledge or seven types of knowledge. Evidence is given. Proper actions are called sāma and improper actions are called asāma.

### 2.1.2

*tad utāpy āhuḥ sāmnainam upāgād iti sādhunainam upāgād ity eva tad āhur asāmnainam upāgād ity asādhunainam upāgād ity eva tad āhuḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**This also is said. They say “He came with sāma” which means “He came auspiciously.” They say “He came with asāma” which means “He came inauspiciously.”**

**Комментарий:**

On this subject they also say as follows (tat utāpy āhuḥ). They say “He came with sāma” which means “He came auspiciously.” They say “He came with asāma” which means “He came inauspiciously.”

### 2.1.3

*athotāpy āhuḥ sāma no bateti yat sādhu bhavati sādhu batety eva tad āhur asāma no bateti yad asādhu bhavaty asādhu batety eva tad āhuḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**This also is said. Joyfully people say “This is sāma for us”, meaning that the action was good.**

**They mean it was beneficial. They say “This is asama for us” meaning it is not beneficial for us. They mean that it is unbeneficial.**

**Комментарий:**

Thus one should meditate on the whole sāma as being most beneficial.

### 2.1.4

*sa ya etad evaṁ vidvān sādhu sāmety upāste 'bhyāśo ha yad enaṁ+ sādhavo dharmā ā ca gaccheyur upa ca nameyuḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**All good qualities come and attend upon the person who, as practice, meditates on the whole sāma as being most beneficial**

**Комментарий:**

He is able to enjoy (upanameyuḥ) those qualities.

## 2 - Meditation on Sāma as Five types of Knowledge

### 2.2.1

*lokeñu pañca-vidhaṁ sāmopāsīta pṛthivī hiṁ-kāro 'gniḥ prastāvo 'ntarikñam udgītha ādityaḥ*  
*pratihāro dyaur nidhanam ity ūrdhveñu.*

**Перевод:**

**One should meditate on sāma related to the worlds in five ways: in hiṁkāra one sees the earth, in prasthava one sees fire, in udgītha one sees antarikña in pratihara one sees the sun and nidhana one sees the heavens.**

**Комментарий:**

One should perform meditation related to the world using sāma with five types according to hiṁkāra, prastāva, udgītha, pratihāra and nidhāna (parts of the Sāman to be chanted).

### 2.2.2

*athāvṛtteñu dyaur hiṁ-kāra ādityaḥ prastāvo 'ntarikñam udgītho 'gniḥ pratihāraḥ pṛthivī nidhanam.*

**Перевод:**

**In reverse order, one should meditate one on heavens in hiṁkāra, the sun in prastāva, antarikña in the udgītha, fire in the pratihāra and earth in the nidhāna.**

### 2.2.3

*kalpante hāsmai lokā ūrdhvāś cāvṛttāś ca ya etad evaṁ vidvāl+ lokeñu pañca-vidhaṁ+ sāmopāste.*

**Перевод:**

**For the person who knows this and worships the five elements in the worlds, the planets in ascending and descending order make arrangements of the worshipper to enjoy.**

### 3

### 2.3.1

*vṛñṭau pañca-vidhaṁ+ sāmopāsīta puro-vāto hiṁ-kāro megho jāyate sa prastāvo vapati sa udgītho vidyotate stanayati sa pratihāraḥ udgṛhṇāti tan nidhanaṁ*

**Перевод:**

**One should meditate on the sāma of five parts as rain. Hiṁkāra is the eastern wind. The prastāva produces clouds. The udgītha rains. The pratihara flashes lightning and roars with thunder. The nidhana stops the rain.**

### 2.3.2

*varñati hāsya vaṛsayati ha ya etad evaṁ vidvān vṛñṭau pañca-vidhaṁ+ sāmopāste.*

**Перевод:**

**For he who meditates on the same of five parts as rain it rains when he desires. Even today, such a person causes rain.**

### 4

### 2.4.1

*sarvāsv apsu pañca-vidhaṁ+ sāmopāsīta megho yat samplavate sa hiṁ-kāro yad varñati sa prastāvo yāḥ prācyaḥ syandante sa udgītho yāḥ pratīcyaḥ sa pratihāraḥ samudro nidhanam.*

**Перевод:**

**One should meditate on the five parts of the sāman as all types of water. When clouds mass together it is himkāra. When the clouds shower rain it is prastāva. The rivers flowing east are the udgītha. The rivers flowing west are the pratihāra. The ocean is nidhanam.**

### 2.4.2

*na hāpsu praity apsu-mān bhavati ya etad evaṁ vidvān sarvāsv apsu pañca-vidhaṁ+ sāmopāste.*

**Перевод:**

**He who knows this and meditates on the five parts of the sāma as all types of water will not die in water and will possess sufficient water.**

### 5

### 2.5.1

*ṛtuñu pañca-vidhaṁ+ sāmopāsīta vasanto hiṁ-kāro grīñmaḥ prastāvo varñā udgīthaḥ śarat pratihāro hemanto nidhanam.*

**Перевод:**

**One should meditate on the five parts of the sāman as the seasons. Spring is hiṁkāra. Summer is prastāva. Monsoon is udgītha. Autumn is pratihāra. Winter is nidhana.**

### 2.5.2

*kalpante hāsmā ṛtava ṛtu-mān bhavati ya etad evaṁ vidvān ṛtuñu pañca-vidhaṁ+ sāmop*  
*āste.*

**Перевод:**

**The seasons are capable of supply him with enjoyable objects. He who knows thus and meditates on the sāma of five parts as the seasons enjoys the results of all seasons.**

### 6

### 2.6.1

*paśuśu pañca-vidhaṁ+ sāmopāsītājā hiṁ-kāro 'vayaḥ prastāvo gāva udgītho 'śvāḥ pratihāraḥ puruño nidhanam.*

**Перевод:**

**One should meditate on the sāma of five parts as animals. Goats are hiṁkāra. Sheep are prastāva. Cows are udgītha. Horses are pratihāra. Man is nidhana.**

### 2.6.2

*bhavanti hāsya paśavaḥ paśumān bhavati ya etad evaṁ vidvān paśuñu pañca-vidhaṁ sāmopāste .*

**Перевод:**

**He who meditates on sāma of five parts as animals attains animals. He possesses many animals.**

### 7

### 2.7.1

*prāṇeñu pañca-vidhaṁ paro-varīyaḥ sāmopāsīta prāṇo hiṁ-kāro vāk prastāvaś cakñur udgīthaḥ śrotraṁ pratihāro mano nidhanaṁ paro-varīyāṁ+si vaitāni.*

**Перевод:**

**One should meditate on the most excellent sāman of five parts as the senses. The nose is hiṁkāra. Voice is the prastava. The eye is the udgītha. The ear is the pratihāra. The mind is the nidhana. The senses are the best of the best.**

### 2.7.2

*paro-varīyo hāsya bhavati paro-varīyaso ha lokāñ jayati yad etad evaṁ vidvān prāṇeñu pañca-vidhaṁ paro-varīyaḥ sāmopāsta iti tu pañca-vidhasya.*

**Перевод:**

**He who knowing this meditates on the most excellent sāman of five parts as the senses possesses the best life and attains the most excellent planets. The meditation on the sāma of five parts has been explained.**

## 8 - Meditation on the seven parts of the Sāman

### 2.8.1

*atha sapta-vidhasya vāci sapta-vidhaṁ+ sāmopāsīta yat kiñca vāco him iti sa hiṁ-kāro yat preti sa prastāvo yad eti sa ādiḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**One should meditate on the sāma of seven parts as speech. The syllable “him” in speech is hiṁkāra. The syllable pra in speech is prastāva. The syllable a is ādi or Om, the first word of the udgītha.**

### 2.8.2

*yad uditi sa udgītho yat pratīti sa pratihāro yad upeti sa upadravo yan nīti tan nidhanam.*

**Перевод:**

**The syllable ut is the udgītha. The word prati is the pratihāra. The phrase upa is the upadrava. The syllable ni is the nidhana.**

### 2.8.3

*dugdhe 'smai vāg dohaṁ yo vāco doho 'nna-vān annādo bhavati, ya etad evaṁ vidvān vāci sapta-vidhaṁ+ sāmopāste.*

**Перевод:**

**He who knows this and meditates on the sāma as the seven parts of speech, attains the results of speech. He possess all food and enjoys all food.**

### 9

### 2.9.1

*atha khalv amum ādityaṁ+ sapta-vidhaṁ+ sāmopāsīta sarvadā samas tena sāma māṁ prati māṁ pratīti sarveṇa samastena sāma.*

**Перевод:**

**One should worship sāma in seven parts as the sun. The sun is always the same dimension (sama) and thus is called sāma. The sun is equal to all since everyone thinks that the sun is facing himself.**

### 2.9.2

*tasminn imāni sarvāṇi bhūtāny anvāyattānīti vidyāt tasya yat purodayāt sa hiṁ-kāras tad asya paśavo 'nvāyattās tasmāt te hiṁ-kurvanti hiṁ-kāra-bhājino hy etasya sāmnaḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**One should know that all beings depend on the sun. The sun before rising is hiṁkāra. The animals are dependent on hiṁkāra of the sāma and thus say “him.” They are dependent on this form of himkāra related to the sun in the sāma.**

**Комментарий:**

One should meditate on the sun as giving shelter to all beings. Dependence of all beings on the sun is shown at various times. One should think of the sun before it rises as hiṁkāra. All animals depend on that form of hiṁkāra.

### 2.9.3

*atha yat prathamodite sa prastāvas tasya manuñyā avāyattās tasmāt te prastuti-kāmāḥ praśaṁ+sā-kāmāḥ prastāva-bhājino hy etasya sāmnaḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**When the sun first rises it is prastāva. Humans are dependent on the sun at this time. Thus they desire to desire praise (prastuti-kāmaḥ). They are related to the form of the sun at this time in the prastāva of the sāma.**

**Комментарий:**

Prastuti-kāma means praśaṁña-kāmaḥ.

### 2.9.4

*atha yat saṅgava-velāyāṁ+ sa ādis tad asya vayāṁsy anvāyattāni tasmāt tāny antarikñe 'nārambaṇāny ādāyātmānaṁ paripatanty ādi-bhājīni hy etasya sāmnaḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**The sun at forenoon is the ādi. The birds are dependent on this form of the sun. Without support of anything except themselves, they fly in the sky. They are related to ādi of the sāman.**

**Комментарий:**

The birds take support (ādāya) of themselves. This is similar to ādi.

### 2.9.5

*atha yat samprati madhyan-dine sa udgīthas tasya devā anvāyattās tasmāt te sat-tamāḥ prājāpatyānām udgītha-bhājino hy etasya sāmnaḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**Now at midday, the sun is the udgīta. The devatās are dependent on this form of the sun. Therefore they are the greatest among the Prajāpatis. They are related to the udgītha of the sāma.**

**Комментарий:**

The devtās are sattama (excellent) and udgītha is similarly sattama.

### 2.9.6

*atha yad ūrdhvaṁ madhyān-dināt prāg aparāhṇāt sa pratihāras tad asya garbhā anvāyattās tasmāt te pratihṛtā nāvapadyante pratihāra-bhājino hy etasya sāmnaḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**The sun after midday and before afternoon is the pratihāra. The embryos are dependent on this form of the sun. Thus, being held up (pratihṛtā) they do not fall down. They are related to the pratihāra of the sāman.**

### 2.9.7

*atha yad ūrdhvam aparāhṇāt prāg asta-mayāt sa upadravas tad asyāraṇyā anvāyattās tasmāt te puruñaṁ dṛñṭvā kakñaṁ+ śvabhram ity upadravanty upadrava-bhājino hy etasya sāmnaḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**The sun in the afternoon before sunset is upadrava. The forest animals are dependent on this form of the sun. Seeing a human, the animals flee (upadrava) into forest caves to hide. They are related to the upadrava of the sāman.**

### 2.9.8

*atha yat prathamāstamite tan nidhanaṁ tad asya pitaro 'nvayāttās tasmāt tān nidadhāti nidhana-bhājino hy etasya sāmna evaṁ khalv amum ādityaṁ sapta-vidhaṁ sāmopāste.*

**Перевод:**

**The sun as it starts to set is nidhana. The Pitṛs are dependent on this form of the sun. Thus people offer (nidadhāti) piṇḍas on kuśa to the Pitṛs. The Pitṛs are related to the nidhana of the sāman. Thus one should meditate on the sun in the sāma of seven parts.**

### 10

### 2.10.1

*atha khalv ātma-sammitam atimṛtyu sapta-vidhaṁ sāmopāsīta hiṁ-kāra iti try-akñaraṁ prastāva iti try-akñaraṁ tat samam.*

**Перевод:**

**One should meditate upon the sāman of seven parts composed of groups of pairs with equal number of syllables, and which surpasses the sun. Himkāra consists of three syllables. Prastāva consists of three syllables. They are equal.**

**Комментарий:**

Having taught the meditation on sāma with seven parts as the sun, now meditation on the sāma with seven forms to go beyond the sun is described. Mṛtyu here means the sun since it causes destruction of the universe by passage of day and night. The seven parts consists of a total of twenty-two syllables, with three syllables each. These constitute the sun. The remaining syllable is beyond the sun (atimṛtyu).

### 2.10.2

*ādir iti dvy-akñaraṁ pratihāra iti catur-akñaraṁ tat ihaikaṁ tat samam.*

**Перевод:**

**Ādi has two syllables. Pratihāra has four syllables. Taking one syllable and adding that to the two syllables. They become equal with two syllables**

### 2.10.3

*udgītha iti try-akñaram upadrava iti catur-akñaraṁ tribhis tribhiḥ samaṁ bhavaty akñaram atiśiñyate try-akñaraṁ tat samam.*

**Перевод:**

**Udgītha has three syllables and upadrava has four syllables. Making them both three syllables, they are equal. One syllable remains separate.**

### 2.10.4

*nidhanam iti try-akñaraṁ tat samam eva bhavati tāni ha vā etāni dvā-viṁ+śati rākñāṇi.*

**Перевод:**

**Nidhana has three syllables. It is equal to the others. They are twenty two syllables in total.**

### 2.10.5

*Ekaviṁśatyādityam āpnoti ekaviṁśo vā itho ‘sāv adityaḥ dvāviṁśena param ādityāj jayati tannākaṁ tadi viśokam,*

**Перевод:**

**By meditating on the twenty-one syllables one attains the sun planet. The twenty-first syllable is the sun. By the twenty-second syllable one attains the planet without sorrow beyond the sun.**

**Комментарий:**

The twelve months, the five seasons and the three worlds makes twenty components. The twenty-first component is the sun.

### 2.10.6

*Āpnoti hādityasya jayam paro hāsyādityāj jayo bhavati ya etad evaṁ vidvān ātma-sammitam atimṛtyu sapta-vidhaṁ+ sāmopāste sāmopāste.*

**Перевод:**

**He who knows this and meditates on sama of seven parts consisting of pairs of equal syllables, with one syllable beyond that, conquers the sun and attains excellence beyond the sun.**

### 11

### 2.11.1

*mano hiṁ-kāro vāk prastāvaś cakñur udgīthaḥ śrotraṁ pratihāraḥ prāṇo nidhanam etad gāyatraṁ prāṇeñu protam.*

**Перевод:**

**Mind is hiṁkāra, speech is prastāva, the eye is udgītha, the ear is pratihāra and prāṇa is nidhana. This is the gāyatrī related to the senses.**

**Комментарий:**

One should project the mind etc. on the five parts of the gāyatrī of the sāma.

### 2.11.2

*sa ya evam etad gāyatraṁ prāṇeñu protaṁ veda prāṇī-bhavati sarvam āyur eti jyog jīvati mahān prajayā paśubhir bhavati mahān kīrtyā mahā-manāḥ syāt tad vratam.*

**Перевод:**

**He who knows the gāyatrī of the sāman intertwined with the senses, will have strong senses, good health, becomes free of disease, and has many children and animals. He will have great fame. He should be generous. This is the vow.**

**Комментарий:**

Jyog means effulgent: he is free of disease. Mahāmanaḥ means generous.

### 12

### 2.12.1

*abhimanthati sa hiṁ-kāro dhūmo jāyate sa prastāvo jvalati sa udgītho 'ṅgārā bhavanti sa pratihāra upaśāmyati tan nidhanaṁ+ saṁ+śāmyati tan nidhanam etad rathantaram agnau protam.*

**Перевод:**

**Kindling the fire is hiṁkāra. Production of smoke is the prastāva. The blazing fire is udgītha. The coals are pratihāra. The calming of the fire is nidhana. The extinction of the fire is nidhana. This is the rathantara (Sāma verses) intertwined with fire.**

### 2.12.2

*sa ya evam etad rathantaram agnau protaṁ veda brahma-varcasy annādo bhavati sarvam āyur eti jyog jīvati mahān prajayā paśubhir bhavati mahān kīrtyā na pratyaṅṅ agnirm ācāmen na niñṭhīvet tad vratam.*

**Перевод:**

**He who knows the rathantara of the sāman intertwined with fire will be brilliant with knowledge of the Vedas, will eat well, have healthy life, free of disease, with plenty of progeny and animals. He will have great fame. He should not eat in front of the fire and should not spit in front of the fire. This is the vow.**

### 13

### 2.13.1

*upamantrayate sa hiṁ-kāro jñapayate sa prastāvaḥ striyā saha śete sa udgīthaḥ prati strīṁ saha śete sa pratihāraḥ kālaṁ gacchati tan nidhanaṁ pāraṁ gacchati tan nidhanam etad vāmadevyaṁ mithune protam.*

**Перевод:**

**Inviting is hiṁkāra. Conveying intention is the prasvāta. Lying down with his wife is udgītha. Facing his wife is the pratihāra. Ending the intercourse is nidhana. Finishing is nidhana. This is the vāmadeva sāman intertwined with intercourse.**

### 2.13.2

*sa ya evam etad vāmadevyaṁ mithune protaṁ veda mithunī-bhavati mithunān mithunāt prajāyate sarvam āyur eti jyog jīvati mahān prajayā paśubhir bhavati mahān kīrtyā na kāñcana pariharet tad vratam.*

**Перевод:**

**He who knows the vāmadeva sāman intertwined with intercourse will always be with his wife. He will remain potent. He will have healthy life, will be free of disease, and will have many children and animals. He will have great fame. He should not reject any woman who approaches him. This is the vow.**

**Комментарий:**

This rule of not rejecting any woman is only for the worshipper of the vāmadeva verses.

### 14

### 2.14.1

*udyan hiṁ-kāra uditaḥ prastāvo madhyan-dina udgītho 'parāhṇaḥ pratihāro 'staṁ yan nidhanam etad bṛhad āditye protam.*

**Перевод:**

**The rising sun is hiṁkāra. The sun after rising is prastāva. The sun at midday is udgītha. The afternoon sun is pratihāra. The setting sun is nidhana. This is the meditation for bṛhad-sāman intertwined with the sun.**

### 2.14.2

*sa ya etam etad bṛhad āditye protaṁ veda tejasvy annādo bhavati sarvam āyur eti jyog jīvati mahān prajayā paśubhir bhavati mahān kīrtyā tapantaṁ na nindet tad vratam.*

**Перевод:**

**He who knows the bṛhad sāman intertwined with the sun becomes strong, enjoys food, has good health, is free of disease, has many children and animals and has great fame. He should not criticize the sun. That is the vow.**

### 15

### 2.15.1

*abhrāṇi samplavante sa hiṁ-kāro megho jāyate sa prastāvo varñati sa udgītho vidyotate stanayati sa pratihāra udgṛhṇāti tan nidhanam etad vairūpaṁ parjanye protam.*

**Перевод:**

**The gathering clouds are hiṁkāra. The fully formed cloud is prastāva. The rain is udgītha. The thunder and lightning is the pratihāra. The withdrawal of rain is nidhana. This is the vairūpa sāma intertwined with rain.**

### 2.15.2

*sa ya evam etad vairūpaṁ parjanye protaṁ veda virūpāṁś ca paśūn avarundhe sarvam āyur eti jyog jīvati mahān prajayā paśubhir bhavati mahān kīrtyā varñantaṁ no nindet tad vratam.*

**Перевод:**

**He who knows the vairūpa sāman intertwined with clouds attains various types of animals, has good health, is free of disease, and has many children and animals and great fame. He should not criticize the rain. That is the vow.**

### 16

### 2.16.1

*vasanto hiṁ-kāro grīñmaḥ prastāvo varñā udgīthaḥ śarat pratihāro hemanto nidhanam etad vairājam ṛtuñu protam.*

**Перевод:**

**Spring is the hiṁkāra. Summer is the prastāva. Monsoon is the udgītha. Autumn is the pratihaṛa. Winter is the nidhana. This is the vairāja sāman intertwined with the seasons.**

### 2.16.2

*sa ya evam etad vairājam ṛtuñu protaṁ veda virājati prajayā paśubhir brahma-varcasena sarvam āyur eti jyog jīvati mahān prajayā paśubhir bhavati mahān kīrtyartūn na nindet tad vratam.*

**Перевод:**

**He who knows the vairāja sāman intertwined with the seasons becomes resplendent with progeny, animals, knowledge of the Vedas. He attains good health, free of disease and has many children and great fame. He should not criticize the seasons. This is the vow.**

### 17

### 2.17.1

*pṛthivī hiṁ-kāro 'ntarikñaṁ prastāvo dyaur udgītho diśaḥ pratihāraḥ samudro nidhanam etāḥ śakvaryo lokeñu protāḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**Earth is hiṁkāra. Antarikña is the prastāva. Heaven is the udgītha. The directions are the pratihāra. The ocean is the nidhana. This is the śakvari sāman intertwined with the worlds.**

### 2.17.2

*sa ya evam etāḥ śakvaryo lokeñu protā veda lokī bhavati sarvam āyur eti jyog jīvati mahān prajayā paśubhir bhavati mahān kīrtyā lokān na nindet tad vratam.*

**Перевод:**

**He who knows the śakvarī sāman intertwined with the worlds attains the excellent worlds, has good health, is free of disease, has many children and animals and has great fame. He should not criticize the worlds. This is the vow.**

### 18

### 2.18.1

*ajā hiṁ-kāro 'vayaḥ prastāvo gāva udgītho 'śvāḥ pratihāraḥ puruño nidhanam etā revatyaḥ paśuñu protāḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**Goats are the hiṁkāra. Sheep are the prastāva. Cows are the udgītha. Horses are the pratihāra. Humans are the nidhana. This is the revatī sāman intertwined with animals.**

### 2.18.2

*sa ya evam etā revatyaḥ paśuñu protā veda paśu-mān bhavati sarvam āyur eti jyog jīvati mahān prajayā paśubhir bhavati mahān kīrtyā paśūn na nindet tad vratam.*

**Перевод:**

**He who knows the revatī sāman intertwined with animals becomes the possessor of animals, has good health, is free of disease, has many children and animals and has great fame. He should not criticize animals. That is the vow.**

### 19

### 2.19.1

*loma hiṁ-kāras tvak prastāvo māṁ+sam udgītho 'sthi pratihāro majjā nidhanam etad yajñājñīyam aṅgeñu protam.*

**Перевод:**

**Hair is the hiṁkāra. Skin is the prastāva. Flesh is the udgītha. Bone is the pratihāra. Marrow is the nidhana. This is the yajñājñīya sāman intertwined with limbs of the body.**

### 2.19.2

*sa ya evam etad yajñāyajñīyam aṅgeñu protaṁ vedāṅgī bhavati nāṅgena vihūrcchati sarvam āyur eti jyog jīvati mahān prajayā paśubhir bhavati mahān kīrtyā saṁvatsaraṁ majjño nāśnīyāt tad vrataṁ majjño nāśnīyād iti vā.*

**Перевод:**

**He who knows the yajñāyajñīya sāman intertwined with the limbs becomes endowed with all limbs, with no deformed limbs, has good health, is free of disease, has many children and animals and has great fame. He should not eat meat with marrow for a year or should not eat at all. That is the vow.**

### 20

### 2.20.1

*agnir hiṁ-kāro vāyuḥ prastāva āditya udgītho nakñatrāṇi pratihāraś candramā nidhanam etad rājanaṁ devatāsu protam.*

**Перевод:**

**Fire is the hiṁkāra. Air is the prastāva. The sun is the udgītha. The constellations are the pratihāra. The moon is the nidhana. This is the rājana sāman intertwined with the devatās.**

### 2.20.2
