*agniñ te pādaṁ vakteti sa ha śvo-bhūte gā abhiprasthāpayāṁ cakāra tā yatrābhi sāyaṁ babhūvus tatrāgnim upasamādhāya gā uparudhya samidham ādhāya paścād agneḥ prāḍupopaviveśa.*

**Перевод:**

**Agni will teach you one pāda of Brahman. The next day, herding the cows, in the evening he stopped at a place, and kindling a fire there, confining the cows, holding firewood in his hands, he sat facing east with the fire behind him.**

**Комментарий:**

Having taught one pāda of Brahman the bull said that Agni would teach another pāda and became silent. The next day, Satyakāma (saḥ), after performing nitya and naimittika karmas, herded the cows towards his guru’s residence. At the place where he stopped in the evening, surrounded by all the cows, confining them, making a fire, performing sandhyā rites, taking wood in his hand, meditating on the words of the bull, he sat facing east, behind the fire.

### 4.6.2

*tam agnir abhyuvāda satyakāma3 iti bhagava iti ha pratiśuśrāva.*

**Перевод:**

**Agni said to him, “O Satyakāma!” Satyakāma answered, “Dear sir!”**

### 4.6.3

*brahmaṇaḥ somya te pādaṁ bravāṇiti bravītu me bhagavān iti tasmai hovāca pṛthivī kalāntarikñaṁ kalā dyauḥ kalā samudraḥ kalaiña vai somya catuñ-kalaḥ pādo brahmaṇo 'nanta-vān nāma.*

**Перевод:**

**“O gentle boy! I will teach you a pāda of Brahman.” “Dear sir! Teach me.” Agni said to him, “Earth is one kalā. Antarikña is one kalā. Heaven is one kalā. The ocean is one kalā. Gentle one! This pāda of Brahman consisting of four kalās is called Ananta-vān.”**

### 4.6.4

*sa ya etam evaṁ vidvāṁś catuñ-kalaṁ pādaṁ brahmaṇo 'nanta-vān ity upāste 'nanta-vān asmil+ loke bhavaty ananta-vato ha lokāñ jayati ya etam evaṁ vidvāṁś catuñ-kalaṁ pādaṁ brahmaṇo 'nanta-vān ity upāste.*

**Перевод:**

**He who, knowing this, meditates upon the pāda of Brahman consisting of four kalās, and called Anantavān becomes unlimited in this world and conquers unlimited worlds.**

### 7

### 4.7.1

*haṁ+sas te pādaṁ vakteti sa ha śvo-bhūte gā abhiprasthāpayāṁ cakāra tā yatrābhi sāyaṁ babhūvus tatrāgnim upasamādhāya gā uparudhya samidham ādhāya paścād agneḥ prāḍupopaviveśa.*

**Перевод:**

**“A swan will teach you one pāda of Brahman.” The next day Satyakāma herded the cows. Where he stayed in the evening, after lighting a fire, confining the cows and holding wood in his hands, he sat facing east with the fire behind him.**

### 4.7.2

*taṁ haṁsa upanipatyābhyuvāda satyakāma3 iti bhagava iti ha pratiśuśrāva.*

**Перевод:**

**A swan flew down and said, “O Satyakāma!” Satyakāma answered, “Dear sir!”**

### 4.7.3

*brahmaṇaḥ somya te pādaṁ bravāṇiti bravītu me bhagavān iti tasmai hovācāgniḥ kalā sūryaḥ kalā candraḥ kalā vidyut kalaiña vai somya catuñ-kalaḥ pādo brahmaṇo jyotiñ-mān nāma.*

**Перевод:**

**“O gentle one! I will teach you one pāda of Brahman.” “Please teach me, sir.” The swan said to him, “Agni is one kalā, the sun is one kalā, the moon is one kalā and lightning is one kalā. Gentle one! This pāda of Brahman with four kalās is called Jyotiñmān.”**

### 4.7.4

*sa ya etam evaṁ vidvāṁ+ś catuñ-kalaṁ pādaṁ brahmaṇo jyotiñ-mān ity upāste jyotiñ-mān asmil+ loke bhavaty jyotiñ-mato ha lokāñ jayati ya etam evaṁ vidvāṁ+ś catuñ-kalaṁ pādaṁ brahmaṇo jyotiñ-mān ity upāste.*

**Перевод:**

**He who, knowing this, meditates upon the pāda of with four kalās becomes radiant in this world and conquers radiant planets.**

### 8

### 4.8.1

*madguñ ṭepādaṁ vakteti sa ha śvo-bhūte gā abhiprasthāpayāṁ cakāra tā yatrābhi sāyaṁ babhūvus tatrāgnim upasamādhāya gā uparudhya samidham ādhāya paścād agneḥ prāḍupopaviveśa.*

**Перевод:**

**“A cormorant will teach you one pāda of Brahman.” The next day Satyakāma herded the cows. Where he stayed in the evening, after lighting a fire, confining the cows and holding wood in his hands, he sat facing east with the fire behind him.**

### 4.8.2

*taṁ madgur upanipatyābhyuvāda satyakāma3 iti bhagava iti ha pratiśuśrāva.*

**Перевод:**

**A cormorant flew down and said to him, “O Satyakāma!” Satyakāma replied, “Dear sir!”**

### 4.8.3

*brahmaṇaḥ somya te pādaṁ bravāṇiti bravītu me bhagavān iti tasmai hovāca prāṇaḥ kalā cakñuḥ kalā śrotraṁ kalā manaḥ kalaiña vai somya catuñ-kalaḥ pādo brahmaṇa āyatana-vān nāma.*

**Перевод:**

**“O gentle one! I will teach you one pāda of Brahman.” “Please teach me, sir.” “Prāṇa is one kalā, the eye is one kalā, the ear is one kalā and mind is one kalā. Gentle one! This pāda of with four kalās is called Āyatana-vān.”**

### 4.8.4

*sa ya etam evaṁ vidvāṁ+ś catuñ-kalaṁ pādaṁ brahmaṇa āyatana-vān ity upāsta āyatana-vān asmil+ loke bhavaty āyatana-vato ha lokāñ jayati ya etam evaṁ vidvāṁ+ś catuñ-kalaṁ pādaṁ brahmaṇa āyatana-vān ity upāste.*

**Перевод:**

**He who, knowing this, meditates upon the pāda of with four kalās attains abodes in this world and conquers planets with abodes.**

### 9

### 4.9.1

*prāpa hācārya-kulaṁ tam ācāryo 'bhyuvāda satyakāma3 iti bhagava iti ha pratiśuśrāva.*

**Перевод:**

**He arrived at the house of his teacher. The teacher said, “O Satyakāma!” Satyakāma said, “Dear sir!”**

### 4.9.2

*brahma-vid iva vai somya bhāsi ko nu tvānuśacāsety anye manuñyebhya iti ha pratijajñe bhagavāṁ+s tv eva me kāme brūyāt.*

**Перевод:**

**“Gentle one! You shine as if you have realized Brahman. Who taught you?” He answered, “Persons other than humans taught me. When the teacher alone desires, the teacher will instruct me.”**

**Комментарий:**

Who taught you about Brahman! “No human was enthusiastic to teach me, your student. Devatās taught me. Your lordship will teach me, according to your desire. Why should I ask anyone else for instruction?”

### 4.9.3

*śrutaṁ hy eva me bhagavad-dṛśebhya ācāryād dhaiva vidyā viditā sādhiñṭhaṁ prāpatīti tasmai haitad evovācātra ha na kiñcana vīyāyeti vīyāyeti.*

**Перевод:**

**I have heard from persons like you that knowledge learned from teachers are most effective. The teacher instructed him in this knowledge. Nothing was lacking.**

**Комментарий:**

I do not desire to learn from anyone except you. After he said this, the teacher spoke to Satyakāma the knowledge having sixteen kalās. He taught him nothing less and nothing more than what the devatās taught him. Nothing was different in the teaching since that teaching was complete in itself.

## 10 - Upakosala-vidyā

### 4.10.1

*upakosalo ha va kāmalāyanaḥ satyakāme jābāle brahma-caryam uvāsa tasya ha dvādaśa-varñāṇy agnīn paricacāra sa ha smānyān ante-vāsinaḥ samāvartayaṁ+s taṁ+ ha smaiva na samāvartayati.*

**Перевод:**

**Upakosala, the son of Kamala, served as a brahmacārī under Satyakāma Jabāla. He served the fires for twelve years. Satyakāma sent the others home after completion of studies, but did not send Upakosala home.**

**Комментарий:**

Upakosala-vidyā is now taught. Upakosala, son of Kamala studied as brahmacārī under Jabāla Satyakāma to gain knowledge. He served the fires for twelve years. Satyakāma taught the other the Vedas, since they desired that. Knowing that Upakosala desired brahma-vidyā he brahmacārīs did not have him leave, thinking that he should not be taught brahmavidyā without performing long service.

### 4.10.2

*taṁ jāyovāca tapto brahma-cārī kuśalam agnīn paricacārīn mā tvāgnayaḥ paripravocan prabrūhy asmā iti tasmai hāprocyaiva pravāsāṁ cakre.*

**Перевод:**

**Satyakāma’s wife spoke to him. “This brahmacārī, undergoing austerities, has served the fires well. The fires should not scold you. Please teach him that knowledge.” Without teaching him, Satyakāma departed.**

**Комментарий:**

His wife spoke. Performing austerities, this brahmacārī has served the fires perfectly. The fires, pleased with his service, will scold you for not teaching him. Teach him that knowledge so that the fires do not scold you. Though his wife said this, Satyakāma departed for another province without teaching him.

### 4.10.3

*sa ha vyādhinānaniśituṁ dadhre tam ācārya-jāyovāca brahma-cārīnn aśāna kiṁ nu nāśnāstīti sa hovāca bahava ime 'smin puruñe kāmā nānātyayā vyādhibhiḥ pratipūrṇo 'smi nāśiñyāmīti.*

**Перевод:**

**Upakosola, pained in his mind, remained without eating. The wife of the teacher said, “O brahmacārī! Please eat. Why do you not eat?” He said, “There are many desires in this person. I am full of pain, fearing these things. Thus I do not eat.”**

**Комментарий:**

There are many desires, other than the attainment of Brahman, which produce birth, old age, and death, and which cause affliction in this person. I am full of pain from fear of these things. Thus I do not eat.

### 4.10.4

*atha hāgnayaḥ samūdire tapto brahma-cārī kuśalaṁ naḥ paryacārīd dhantāsmai prabravāmeti tasmai hocuḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**The fires said, “This brahmacārī, performing austerities, has served us well. We should appear in front of him.” They spoke to him.**

**Комментарий:**

Hearing those words, the fires such as gārhapatya, pleased with his service, out of compassion, appearing before him, spoke.

### 4.10.5

*prāṇo brahma kaṁ brahma khaṁ brahmeti sa hovāca vijānāmy ahaṁ yat prāṇo brahma kaṁ ca tu khaṁ ca na vijānāmīti te hocur yad vāva kaṁ tad eva khaṁ yad eva khaṁ tad eva kam iti prāṇaṁ ca hāsmai tad ākāśaṁ cocuḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**“Prāṇa is Brahman. Ka is Brahman. Kha is Brahman.” He said, “I understand that prāṇa is Brahman. I do not understand about ka and kha.” They said, “Ka is kha and kha is ka.” They taught him that prānā is ākāśa.**

**Комментарий:**

The fires did not advise some contrary worship. Desiring liberation and fearing birth, old age and death, he wanted instructions on liberation. Thus they taught Brahman as the object of worship. The words mentioned must be equated with Brahman.

Upakosola understood that prāṇa is Brahman since the Lord gives life to the whole universe. He did not understand how ka and kha were Brahman. Ka means happiness and kha means ākāśa or ether. The Lord has material happiness and material ether as his body (he gives life to them). Moreover, ākāśa can modify ka or happiness or ka can modify ākāśa. Thus the meaning is unlimited happiness. That is Brahman.

Understanding the intention of his question, the fires spoke. ka and kha act as modifiers of each other. But is it not enough to say that Brahman is unlimited happiness (ākāśa modifing ka)? Why be repetitious and say that ka modifies ākāśa? No, this strengthens the meaning. One can meditate on unlimited happiness and on the joyful unlimited nature of Brahman. Or the statement is spoken in order to perfect worship, since the teaching becomes successful with exchange of modifier and modified. Thus the fires taught that Brahman is prāṇa because he gives life to the universe, and is unlimited bliss (ākāśa).

## 11 - Agni-vidyā

### 4.11.1

*atha hainaṁ gārhapatyo 'nuśaśāsa pṛthivy agnir annam āditya iti ya eña āditye puruño dṛśyate so 'ham asmi sa evāham asmīti.*

**Перевод:**

**Then the gārhapatya fire taught him. “Earth, fire, food and the sun are my bodies. I am that person seen in the sun. I am he.”**

**Комментарий:**

The fires teach Agni-vidyā as an aṅga of their teachings about Brahman. Having taught Upakosala about Brahman as giver of life to the universe and as unlimited bliss, the gārhaptya fire teach Upakosala (enam) about himself. Earth, fire, food and the sun are my bodies. Fire and sun are considered one since they both cook and illuminate. There is no oneness of fire with earth and food. Thus the fire speaking says “I (fire) am the sun.” The sun is a form of fire.

### 4.11.2

*sa ya etam evaṁ vidvān upāste 'pahate pāpa-kṛtyāṁ lokī bhavati sarvam āyur eti jyog jīvati nāsyāvara-puruñāḥ kñīyanta upa vayaṁ taṁ bhuñjāmo 'smiṁś ca loke 'muñmiṁ+ś ca ya etam evaṁ vidvān upāste.*

**Перевод:**

**He who, knowing this, worships the fire, destroys all sins. He attains Brahmaloka. He attains good health and freedom from disease until attaining Brahman. His dependents do not perish. We protect in this world and the next the person who, knowing this, worships fire**

**Комментарий:**

He who knowing this worships the fire, destroys all sins which are contrary to Brahma-vidyā. Though he attains Brahman with destruction of sins contrary to Brahman, and though his main result is brahma-vidyā, the secondary results are praised. Pāpa can also mean the sins which prevent brahma-vidyā. Lokī can mean attaining the planet of fire on the path to attaining Brahmaloka (spiritual world). Dependents such as disciples and their disciples as well as offspring and grandchildren will not perish. They become knowers of Brahman.

*brahmaiva bhavati nāsyābrahmavit kule bhavati*

**Those born in his family become knowers of Brahman. Muṇḍaka Upaniñad**

We protect (upabhuñjāmaḥ) him till he attains Brahman.

### 12

### 4.12.1

*atha hainam anvāhārya-pacano 'nuśaśāsāpo diśo nakñatrāṇi candramā iti ya eña candramasi puruño dṛśyate so 'ham asmi sa evāham asmīti.*

**Перевод:**

**The southern fire (anvāhārya) gave teachings. “Water, the directions, the constellations and the moon are my forms. I am the person who is seen in the moon. I am he.”**

**Комментарий:**

Fire and the moon are similar in giving light.

### 4.12.2

*sa ya etam evaṁ vidvān upāste 'pahate pāpa-kṛtyāṁ lokī bhavati sarvam āyur eti jyog jīvati nāsyāvara-puruñaḥ kñīyanta upa vayaṁ taṁ bhuñjāmo 'smiṁś ca loke 'muñmiṁ+ś ca ya etam evaṁ vidvān upāste.*

**Перевод:**

**He who, knowing this, worships the fire, destroys all sins. He attains Brahmaloka. He attains good health and freedom from disease until attaining Brahman. His dependents do not perish. We protect in this world and the next the person who, knowing this, worships fire**

### 13

### 4.13.1

*atha hainam āhavanīyo 'nuśaśāsa prāṇa ākāśo dyaur vidyud iti ya eña vidyuti puruño dṛśyate so 'ham asmi sa evāham asmīti.*

**Перевод:**

**The āhavanīya fire gave teachings. “Prāṇa, ākāśa, heaven and lightning are my forms. I am he who is the person seen in lightning. I am he.”**

**Комментарий:**

Fire and lightning share the quality of illumination.

### 4.13.2

*sa ya etam evaṁ vidvān upāste 'pahate pāpa-kṛtyāṁ lokī bhavati sarvam āyur eti jyog jīvati nāsyāvara-puruñaḥ kñīyanta upa vayaṁ taṁ bhuñjāmo 'smiṁś ca loke 'muñmiṁ+ś ca ya etam evaṁ vidvān upāste.*

**Перевод:**

**He who, knowing this, worships the fire, destroys all sins. He attains Brahmaloka. He attains good health and freedom from disease until attaining Brahman. His dependents do not perish. We protect in this world and the next the person who, knowing this, worships fire.**

### 14

### 4.14.1

*te hocur upakosalaiñā somya te 'smad vidyātma-vidyā cācāryas te gatiṁ vaktety ājagāma hāsyācāryas tam ācāryo 'bhyuvādopakosala3 iti.*

**Перевод:**

**Together they spoke to Upakosala. O gentle Upakosala! This is the knowledge of fire, the knowledge of the Lord, along with its aṅgas. Your guru will teach you the rest. His guru returned and said, “O Upakosala!”**

**Комментарий:**

After giving individual teachings, together the fires spoke. This is that agni-vidyā, which is paramātma-vidyā (asmād vidyā), including aṅgas as well. The intention of the fires is made clear. O Upakosala. We have taught you this agni-vidyā with its aṅgas, which is knowledge of Brahman, in order to enliven you, who were depressed at not attaining that knowledge from your guru, who went away without teaching you. Since knowledge learned from guru is most effective, he should teach the method (gati) of going by the path of the sun, the place for meditation, and the attractive form (saṁyad-vāma) of the Lord. He should teach the remaining necessary knowledge, not just the path (gatim).

### 4.14.2

*bhagava iti ha pratiśruśrāva brahma-vida iva somya te mukhaṁ bhāti ko nu tvānuśaśāseti ko nu mānuśiñyād bho itīhopeva nihnuta ime nūnam īdṛśā anyādṛśā itīhāgnīn abhyūde kiṁ nu somya kila te 'vocann iti.*

**Перевод:**

**“Dear sir!” he answered. His guru said, “Gentle one! Your face shines like that of a person who knows Brahman. Who taught you?” “Who should teach me?” He seemed to hide the issue, but then said, going to the fires, “These fires are like this now but were different previously.” “Gentle on! What did the fires teach you?”**

**Комментарий:**

He seemed to evade the issue. After some time, pointing to the fires he said, “They taught me. These fires now appear to be blazing but had a different form previously. They taught me.”

### 4.14.3

*idam iti ha pratijajñe lokān vāva kila somya te 'vocann ahaṁ tu te tad vakñyāmi yathā puñkara-palāśa āpo na śliñyanta evam evaṁ-vidi pāpa karma na śliñyata iti bravītu me bhagavān iti tasmai hovāca.*

**Перевод:**

**He answered, “They taught me this much.”**

**“Gentle one! They taught you about the worlds. I will teach you about Brahman so that sin will not cling to you, just as water does not cling to a lotus leaf. Upakosala said to him, “Sir, you should teach me.”**

**Комментарий:**

The fires taught completely about earth etc. (lokān) They taught complexly about agni-vidyā with its aṅgas, but not completely about Brahman which should be known. I will speak about Brahman, with knowledge so that sin will not cling to you, just as water does not cling to a lotus leaf, if you desire to hear. Clinging of sin means actions which cause sinful reactions. This knowledge has the power to counteract the creation of those reactions. This refers to actions committed through inattention rather than deliberation. It includes sukṛti as well as duskṛtis.

### 15

### 4.15.1

*ya eño 'kñiṇi puruño dṛśyate eña ātmeti hovācaitad amṛtam abhayam etad brahmeti yad yady apy asmin sarpir vodakaṁ vā siñcati vartmanīhy eva gacchati.*

**Перевод:**

**He said, “The person seen in the eye is ātmā. He is greatest, sweetest and destructive of fear. If ghee or water is sprinkled in the eye, it leaves on both sides.**

**Комментарий:**

The person seen in the eye by the yogīs is ātmā, the controller. He is most sweet (amṛtam) and destroys all suffering (abhayam). He is the greatest (brahma). If one sprinkles water on ghee on the eye, it flows away on both sides (vartmani). It does not stick to the eye, because of the power of the Lord residing there, who cannot be contaminated.

### 4.15.2

*etaṁ saṁyad vāma ity ācakñata etaṁ hi sarvāṇi vāmāny abhisaṁyanti sarvāṇy eva vāmāny abhisaṁyanti ya evaṁ veda.*

**Перевод:**

**That person is called Samyad-vāma. All attractive objects gather in him. All attractive objects are attained by he who knows this person.**

**Комментарий:**

Saṁyad-vāma means he in whom desirable objects (vāma) gather (saṁyat). Or he is the shelter of all auspicious qualities or has his every desire fulfilled. The worshipper of the Lord develops similar qualities (tat-kratu-nyāya).

### 4.15.3

*eña u eva vāmanīr eña hi sarvāṇi vāmāni nayati sarvāṇi vāmāni nayati ya evaṁ veda.*

**Перевод:**

**He alone is vāmanī (bringer of splendor). He produces attractive qualities. One who knows him attains all attractive qualities.**

**Комментарий:**

He produces splendor (vāmani) in those who take shelter of him. His worshipper becomes like him.

### 4.15.4

*eña u eva bhāmanīr eña hi sarveñu lokeñu bhāti sarveñu lokeñu bhāti ya evaṁ veda.*

**Перевод:**

**He alone is most beautiful. He illuminates all worlds. He who knows him illuminates all worlds.**

**Комментарий:**

The Lord has a form whose splendor spreads to all planets (bhāmanī). The devotee develops a similar quality if he so desires. Saṁyad-vāma, vāmanī and bhāmani are results. By explaining real results for the devotees, the real nature of those qualities in the Lord are indicated. It is not exaggerated praise, like ghee and water rolling from the eye.

### 4.15.5

*atha yad u caivāsmiñ chavyaṁ kurvanti yadi ca nārciñam evābhisaṁyanty arciño 'har ahna āpūryamāṇa-pakñam āpūryamāṇa-pakñād yān ñaḍ udaṅṅ eti māsāṁ+s tān māsebhyaḥ saṁvatsaraṁ+ saṁvatsarād ādityam ādityāc candramasaṁ candramaso vidyutaṁ tat puruño 'mānavaḥ sa enān brahma gamayaty eña deva-patho brahma-patha etena pratipadyamānā imaṁ mānavam āvartaṁ nāvartante nāvartante.*

**Перевод:**

**Whether the person has funeral rites performed or not, he goes by the light, from light to the day, from day to the waxing fortnight, from the waxing fortnight to the six months of uttarāyana, from the uttarāyana to the year, from the year to the sun, from the sun to the moon, from the moon to lightning. The spiritual guide will lead him from lightning to the Lord. This is the path of the presiding deities, the path for attaining Brahman. Those who go by this path do not return to the whirlpool of human birth.**

**Комментарий:**

Atha indicates the main subject matter. Whether the sons etc. perform funeral rites or not for this knower of Brahman (asmin), the knowers go by the path of light. The different steps refer to the devatās situated at various places according to Vedānta-sutra [4.3.4](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vs/4.3.4). Ṣaḍ udan means the six months of uttarāyana. Deva-pathaḥ means the path having the presiding deities as guides. Brahma-patha means the path which leads to Brahman. Those who go by this path do not return to the terrible wheel of death (mānavam āvartam) where one takes bodies of humans etc.

### 16

### 4.16.1

*eña ha vai yajño yo 'yaṁ pavata eña ha yann idaṁ+ sarvaṁ punāti yad eña yann idaṁ sarvaṁ punāti tasmād eña eva yajñas tasya manaś ca vāk ca vartanī.*

**Перевод:**

**The wind that blows is sacrifice. The wind, going everywhere, purifies everything. Going everywhere and purifying everything, it is sacrifice. Mind and speech are its two paths.**

**Комментарий:**

It is well known in smṛtis that objects are purified by rain, wind and sun. Like the wind sacrifice goes everywhere and purifies. Its quality of going is explained by the paths of speech and mind.

### 4.16.2

*tayor anyatarāṁ manasā saṁ+skaroti brahmā vācā hotādhvaryur udgātānyatarāṁ+ sa yatropākṛte prātar anuvāke purā paridhānīyāyā brahmā vyavavadati.*

**Перевод:**

**The Brahma priest purifies one of these paths with his mind. The hotā, adhvaryu and udgatha priests purify the other path with speech. The brahma priest, beginning the prātar anuvāka, may speak before the recitation of the paridhānīya hymn.**

**Комментарий:**

Vyavadati means he speaks various words which are then criticized. He has given up his silence.

### 4.16.3

*anyatarām eva vartanī saṁ+skaroti hīyate 'nyatarā sa yathaika-pād vrajan ratho vaikena cakreṇa vartamāno riñyaty evam asya yajño riñyati yajñaṁ+ riñyantaṁ yajamāno 'nuriñyati sa iñṭvā pāpīyān bhavati.*

**Перевод:**

**Speaking, he purifies one path and destroys the other. Just as man walking on one foot or a chariot moving on one wheel gets injured, the sacrifice is injured. The sacrificer is injured. Having sacrificed, he becomes sinful.**

**Комментарий:**

By loss of mental concentration, the path of mind of the brahma gets destroyed. The path of speech of the hotā, adhvaryu and udgatha remains. The fault is explained. As the man walking on one foot or the chariot moving on one wheel is destroyed, the sacrifice gets destroyed since it has faults. When the sacrifice is destroyed, the results are destroyed. He becomes a sinner.

### 4.16.4

*atha yatropākṛte prātar anuvāke na purā paridhānīyāyā brahmā vyavavadaty ubhe eva vartanī saṁ+skurvanti ta hīyate 'nyatarā.*

**Перевод:**

**When the brahmā starts the prārat anuvāka and does not speak before the paridhānīya hymn, both paths purify. Neither path is destroyed.**

### 4.16.5

*sa yathobhaya-pād vrajan ratho vobhābhyāṁ cakrābhyāṁ vartamānaḥ praitiñṭhaty evam asya yajñaḥ pratitiñṭhati yajñaṁ pratitiñṭhantaṁ yajamāno 'nupratiñṭhati sa iñṭvā śreyān bhavati.*

**Перевод:**

**Just as a man walking on two legs or a chariot moving on two wheels stands firm, the sacrifice stands firm. When the sacrifice is firm, the sacrificer is firm. Sacrificing, he gains all results.**

### 17

### 4.17.1

*prajāpatir lokān abhyatapat teñāṁ tapyamānānāṁ+ rasān prāvṛhad agniṁ pṛthvyā vāyum antarikñād ādityaṁ divaḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**Prajāpati contemplated the worlds and extracted the essence of those worlds of his meditation. He extracted fire from the earth, air from the antarikña and the sun from the heavens.**

**Комментарий:**

Vyāhṛti-homa (bhūr bhuvaḥ svaḥ) is prescribed as atonement for a break in the sacrifice. Prajāpati contemplated the worlds, desiring to extract the essence of the worlds.

### 4.17.2

*sa etās tisro devatā abhyatapat tāsāṁ tapyamānānāṁ+ rasān prāvṛhad agner ṛco vāyor yajūṁ+ñi sāmāny ādityāt.*

**Перевод:**

**He contemplated the three devatās and extracted their essence: the Ṛg hymns from fire, the Yajur hymns from air and the sāma hymns from the sun.**

### 4.17.3

*sa etāṁ trayīṁ vidyām abhyatapat tasyās tapyamānāyā rasān prāvṛhad bhūr ity ṛgbhyo bhuvar iti yajurbhyaḥ svar iti sāmabhyaḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**He contemplated the three Vedas with their knowledge and extracted from them their essence: bhūh; from the Ṛg mantras, bhuvaḥ from the Yajur mantras and svaḥ from the sāma mantras.**

### 4.17.4

*tad yady ṛkto riñyed bhūh svāheti gārhapatye juhuyād ṛcām eva tad-rasenarcāṁ vīryeṇarcāṁ yajñasya viriñṭaṁ sandadhāti.*

**Перевод:**

**If the sacrifice becomes injured because of the Ṛg mantras, for the atonement one should offer oblations in the gārhapatya fire with the mantra bhūḥ svāhā. By the strength of the essence of the Ṛg mantras, he corrects the injury of the sacrifice caused by Rg mantras.**

### 4.17.5

*atha yadi yajuñṭo riñyed bhuvaḥ svāheti dakñiṇāgnau juhuyād yajuñām eva tad-rasena yajuñāṁ vīryeṇa yajuñāṁ yajñasya variñṭaṁ+ sandadhāti.*

**Перевод:**

**If the sacrifice becomes injured because of the Yajur mantras, for the atonement one should offer oblations in the dakñiṇa fire with the mantra bhuvaḥ svāhā. By the strength of the essence of the Yajur mantras, he corrects the injury of the sacrifice caused by Yajur mantras.**

### 4.17.6

*atha yadi sāmato riñyed svaḥ svāhety āhavanīye juhuyāt sāmnām eva tad-rasena sāmnāṁ vīryeṇa sāmnāṁ yajñasya variñṭaṁ+ sandadhāti.*

**Перевод:**

**If the sacrifice becomes injured because of the Sāma mantras, for the atonement one should offer oblations in the āhavanīya fire with the mantra svaḥ svāhā. By the strength of the essence of the Sāma mantras, he corrects the injury of the sacrifice caused by sāma mantras.**

### 4.17.7

*tad yathā lavaṇena suvarṇaṁ+ sandadhyāt suvarṇena rajataṁ+ rajatena trapu trapuṇā sīsaṁ+ sīsena lohaṁ lohena dāru dāru carmaṇā.*

**Перевод:**

**Gold should be combined with borax (for softening). Silver should be combined with gold. Tin should be combined with silver. Lead should be combined with tin. Iron should be combined with lead. Wood should be combined with iron. Leather should be combined with wood.**

### 4.17.8

*evam eñāṁ lokānām āsāṁ devatānām asyāstrayyā vidyayā vīryeṇa yajñasya viriñṭaṁ+ sandadhāti bheñaja-kṛto ha vā eña yajño yatraivaṁ-vid brahmā bhavati.*

**Перевод:**

**By the power of the worlds, devatās and Vedas, one mends the injury to the sacrifice.**

**Where there is a Brahmā priest with this knowledge, the sacrifice is cured as if by medicine.**

### 4.17.9

*eña ha vā udak-pravaṇo yajño yatraivaṁ-vid brahmā bhavaty evaṁ-vidaṁ ha vā eñā brahmāṇam anugāthā yato yata āvartate tat tad gacchati.*

**Перевод:**

**Where the brahma priest has this knowledge, the sacrifice results in elevation. There is a verse concerning this brahma priest.**

**Wherever there is fault in a sacrifice, the knowledgeable brahma priest goes and remedies everything.**

**Комментарий:**

Mānavaḥ (having intelligence) of the next verse is taken as the subject of the last phrase of verse 9.

### 4.17.10

*mānavo brahmaivaika ṛtvik kurūn aśvābhirakñaty evaṁ-vid dha vai brahmā yajñaṁ yajamānaṁ+ sarvāṁś cartvijo 'bhirakñati tasmād evaṁ-vid eva brahmāṇaṁ kurvīta nānevaṁ-vidaṁ nānevaṁ-vidam.*

**Перевод:**

**As a mare protects the warriors, the one brahma priest protects the other priests. The brahma priest in knowledge protects the sacrifice, the sponsor and all the priests. Thus one should choose a brahma priest with this knowledge, not one without this knowledge.**

## Part 5

## 1 - Prāṇa-vidyā

### 5.1.1

*oṁ. yo ha vai jyeñṭhaṁ ca śreñṭhaṁ ca veda jyeñṭhaś ca ha vai śreñṭhaś ca bhavati prāṇo vāva jyeñṭhaś ca śreñṭhaś ca.*

**Перевод:**

**He who knows prāṇa as the oldest and the best becomes the oldest and the best. Prāṇa is the oldest and best.**

**Комментарий:**

Prāṇa-vidyā is explained. Vāva is for emphasis. In the womb prāṇa operates before the senses. Thus it is the oldest. Because the sense depend on it, it is the best.

### 5.1.2

*yo ha vai vasiñṭhaṁ veda vasiñṭho ha svānāṁ bhavati vāg vāva vasiñṭhaḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**He who knows variñṭha (wealth) becomes the wealthiest among the jñānīs. Voice is wealth (vasiñṭha).**

**Комментарий:**

Svānāṁ means jñānīs. Vasisṭhaḥ means very wealthy. An eloquent person becomes very wealthy. Since it is the cause of proper behavior which is the cause of wealth, speech is said to be wealth.

### 5.1.3

*yo ha vai pratiñṭhāṁ veda prati ha tiñṭhaty asmiṁ+ś ca loke 'muñmiṁ+ś ca cakñur vāva pratiñṭhā.*

**Перевод:**

**He who knows pratiñṭha (firm position) gains position in this world and the next. The eye is position.**

**Комментарий:**

Ha means well known. Cakñuñā hi same durge ca pratitiñṭhati: one remains firm on even or uneven ground by the eye. (Bṛhad-ārāṇyaka Upaniñad) The eye is firmness because it reveals to the person even or uneven ground, and this causes steadiness.

### 5.1.4

*yo ha vai sampadaṁ veda saṁhāsmai kāmāḥ padyante daivāś ca mānuñāś ca śrotraṁ vāva sampat.*

**Перевод:**

**The desires both divine and human are supplied to the person who knows wealth (sampada). The ear is wealth.**

**Комментарий:**

Desirable objects are supplied (sampadyante) to the person who knows sampada (wealth).

Śrotre hīme sarve vedā abhisaṁpannāḥ: all the Vedas are supplied to the ear. (Bṛhad-ārāṇyaka Upaniñad). The ear is wealth because it allows one to hear the meaning of the Vedic scriptures, which causes wealth.

### 5.1.5

*yo ha vā āyatanaṁ vedāyatanaṁ+ ha svānāṁ bhavati mano ha vā āyatanam.*

**Перевод:**

**He who knows āyatanam (abode) will be the abode for his dependents. The mind is the abode.**

**Комментарий:**

The mind is the abode since it is the abode of enjoyment by being aware of objects like garlands and sandalwood.

### 5.1.6

*atha ha prāṇā ahaṁ+ śreyasi vyūdire 'haṁ+ śreyān asmy ahaṁ+ śreyān asmīti.*

**Перевод:**

**Once, the senses argued about who is best. “I am the better.” “I am the better.”**

**Комментарий:**

Atha indicates the beginning of a story. The story is introduced to show that mukhya-prāṇa is the best.

### 5.1.7

*te ha prāṇāḥ prajāpatiṁ pitaram etyocur bhagavan ko naḥ śreñṭha iti tān hovāca yasmin va utkrānte śarīraṁ pāpiñṭha-taram iva dṛśyate sa vaḥ śreñṭha iti.*

**Перевод:**

**The prāṇas said to their father Prajāpati, “O lord! Who among us is the best?” He said to them, “He is the best among you who on leaving the body makes the body look detestable.”**

### 5.1.8

*sā ha vāg uccakrāma sā saṁvatsaraṁ proñya paretyovāca katham aśakatarte maj jīvitum iti yathā kalā avadantaḥ prāṇantaḥ prāṇena paśyantaś cakñuñā śṛṇvantaḥ śrotreṇa dhyāyanto manasaivam iti praviveśa ha vāk.*

**Перевод:**

**Famous voice left the body. Staying away for one year, voice returned and said, “How could you live without me?” “Just as mute persons without speaking, breathing by prāṇa, seeing by the eye, hearing by the ear, thinking by the mind, carry on living, we lived.” The voice reentered the body.**

**Комментарий:**

Understanding that he was not the best, the voice reentered the body.

### 5.1.9

*cakñur hoccakrāma tat saṁvatsaraṁ proñya paryetyovāca katham aśakatarte maj jīvitum iti yathāndhā apaśyantaḥ prāṇantaḥ prāṇena vadanto vācā śṛṇvantaḥ śrotreṇa dhyāyanto manasaivam iti praviveśa ha cakñuḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**Famous eye left the body. Staying away for one year, the eye returned and said, “How could you live without me?” Just as blind persons without seeing, breathing by prāṇa, speaking by the voice, hearing by the ear, thinking by the mind, carry on living, we lived.” The eye reentered the body.**

### 5.1.10

*śrotraṁ hoccakrāma tat saṁvatsaraṁ proñya paryetyovāca katham aśakatarte maj jīvitum iti yathā badhirā aśṛṇvantaḥ prāṇantaḥ prāṇena vadanto vācā paśyantaś cakñuñā dhyāyanto manasaivam iti praviveśa ha śrotram.*

**Перевод:**

**Famous ear left the body. Staying away for one year, voice returned and said, “How could you live without me?” “Just as deaf persons without hearing, breathing by prāṇa, speaking by the voice, seeing by the eye, thinking by the mind, carry on living, we lived.” The ear reentered the body.**

### 5.1.11

*mano hoccakrāma tat saṁvatsaraṁ proñya paryetyovāca katham aśakatarte maj jīvitum iti yathā bālā amanasaḥ prāṇantaḥ prāṇena vadanto vācā paśyantaś cakñuñā śṛṇvantaḥ śrotreṇaivam iti praviveśa ha manaḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**The mind left the body. Staying away for one year, voice returned and said, “How could you live without me?” “Just as children without deep thinking, breathing by prāṇa, speaking by the voice, seeing by the eye, and hearing by the ear, carry on living, we lived.” The mind reentered the body.**

### 5.1.12

*atha ha prāṇa uccikramiñan sa yathā suhayaḥ paḍvīśa-śaṅkūn saṅkhided evam itarān prāṇān samakhidat taṁ+ hābhisametyocur bhagavann edhi tvaṁ naḥ śreñṭho 'si motkrāmīr iti.*

**Перевод:**

**As a horse pulls out the pegs tying its feet, prāṇa, when it desired to leave, began pulling the other senses. The senses came and said to prāṇa, “O lord! You should be our master. You are the best among us. Do not leave.”**

**Комментарий:**

Mukhya-prāṇa desiring to leave uprooted the other senses from their locations, like an excellent horse which pulls out the pegs binding his feet when whipped by the horseman to test the horse. O worshippable one (bhagavan)! You should be (edhi) our master. You are the best among us. Do not leave.

### 5.1.13

*atha hainaṁ vāg uvāca yad ahaṁ vasiñṭho 'smi tvaṁ tad vasiñṭho 'sīty atha hainaṁ cakñur uvāca yad ahaṁ pratiñṭhāsmi tvaṁ tat pratiñṭhāsīti.*

**Перевод:**

**Voice spoke to prāṇa, “Though I am vasiñtha, you are that vasiñṭha.” The eye spoke to him. “Though I am pratiñṭha, you are that pratiñṭha.”**

**Комментарий:**

As citizens offer taxes to the king the senses offered their respective qualities to prāṇa.

I am variñṭha because of the quality of producing wealth from eloquence. But you are variñṭha by that quality. That quality in me is actually yours since it is dependent on you.

### 5.1.14

*atha hainaṁ śrotram uvāca yad ahaṁ+ sampad asmi tvaṁ tat sampad asīty atha hainaṁ mana uvāca yad aham āyatanam asmi tvaṁ tad āyatanam asīti.*

**Перевод:**

**The ear said to him, “Though I am sampada you are that sampada.” The mind said to him, “Though I am āyatanam you are that āyatanam.”**

### 5.1.15

*na vai vāco na cakñūṁñi na śrotrāṇi an manāṁsīty ācakñate prāṇā ity ācakñate prāṇo hy evaitāni sarvāṇi bhavati.*

**Перевод:**

**They do not call them voice, eye, ear or mind. They call them prāṇa. Prāṇa is all these.**

**Комментарий:**

Common people and knows of scripture do not call them voice, eye, ear or mind. They refer to them as prāṇa. Why is that? Their existence depends on prāṇa.

*Yad adhīnā yasya sattā tat tad ity eva bhaṇyate*

**That whose existence is dependent on another is called by that same name.**

## 2 - Bhaviñya Purāṇa

### 5.2.1

*sa hovāca kiṁ me 'nnaṁ bhaviñyatīti yat kiñcid idam ā-śvabhya ā-śakunibhya iti hocus tad vā etad anasyānnam ano ha vai nāma pratyakñaṁ na ha vā evaṁ-vidi kiñcinānannaṁ bhavati.*

**Перевод:**

**Prāṇa said, “What will be my food?” The senses said, “What is food for beings down to dogs and vultures is your food.” All this is food for prāṇa. Its direct name is ana. He who knows this will not be without food.**

**Комментарий:**

The śruti said, “Whatever is eaten by beings in whatever world is the food of prāṇa (anasya). It is eaten by prāṇa alone. Ana is the name of prāṇa directly because it is related to various activities such as eating. Since devatās are fond of indirect expression instead of the direct name ana, the word prāṇa is used. All food eaten by other beings is food eaten by prāṇa since activities like eating are his activities. Or ana becomes anna, directly related to the life air. The practitioner of prāṇa-vidyā does not have to eat the food of all beings, since that is impossible. Rather he meditates that the food of all beings is the food of prāṇa.

### 5.2.2

*sa hovāca kiṁ me vāso bhaviñyatīty āpa iti hocus tasmād vā etad aśiñyanta purastāc copariñṭāc cādbhiḥ paridadhati lambhuko ha vāso bhavaty anagno ha bhavati.*

**Перевод:**

**Prāṇa said, “What will be my garment?” The senses said, “Water.” Therefore they cover prāṇa with water before and after eating. A person who thinks in this way, receiving a garment, is not naked.**

**Комментарий:**

Water should be contemplated as a garment. One makes prāṇa covered with a garment, not naked, by sipping water before and after eating. Thinking in this way a person, receiving (lambhukaḥ) a garment (vasaḥ), is not naked.

### 5.2.3

*tad dhaitat satyakāma jābālo gośrutaye viayāghrapadyāyoktvovāca yady apy enac chuñkāya sthāṇave brūyāj jāyerann evāsmiñ chākhāḥ praroheyuḥ palāśānīti.*

**Перевод:**

**Satyakāma Jābāla, saying this teaching of prāṇa to Gośruti, son of Vyaghrapat, then said, “If one says this to the dried up trunk of a tree, branches will grow and leaves will sprout on it.”**

### 5.2.4

*atha yadi mahaj jigamiñed amāvāsyāyāṁ dīkñitvā paurṇamāsyāṁ raṭrau sarvauñadhasya manthaṁ dadhi-madhunor upamathya jyeñṭhāya śreñṭhāya svāhety agnāv ājyasya hutvā manthe sampātam avanayet.*

**Перевод:**

**If one desires to attain greatness, after taking initiation on the dark moon, on the night of the full moon one should place all types of herbs in pot, mix in yogurt and honey, and after offering oblations of ghee into the fire while saying jyeñṭhāya, śreñṭhāya svāhā, one should place the residue (sampāt) in the pot.**

**Комментарий:**

The ritual of mantha (pot) is now explained. If one desires to attain greatness in the form of being elder and best, at the proper time, placing herbs from the forest and village, ground up into a pot shaped like a ladle or a cup made of copper, and throwing in honey and yogurt, mixing it, one should place it in front. Offering oblations with ghee in the fire while saying jyeñṭhāya śreñṭhāya svāhā, one should keep the residue in the pot.

### 5.2.5

*vasiñṭhāya svāhety agnāv ājyasya hutvā manthe sampātam avanayet pratiñṭhāyai svāhety agnāv ājyasya hutvā manthe sampātam avanayet sampade svāhety agnāv ājyasya hutvā manthe sampātam avanayed āyatanāya svāhety agnāv ājyasya hutvā manthe sampātam avanayet.*

**Перевод:**

**Offering oblations of ghee into the fire while saying vasiñṭhāya svāhā, one should place the residue in the pot. Offering oblations of ghee into the fire, while saying pratiñṭhāyai svāhā one should place the residue in the pot. Offering oblations of ghee into the fire, while saying sampade svāhā one should place the residue in the pot. Offering oblations of ghee into the fire, while saying āyatanāya svāhā one should place the residue in the pot.**

### 5.2.6

*atha pratisṛpyāñjalau mantham ādhāya japaty amo nāmāsy amā hi te sarvam idaṁ+ sa hi jyeñṭhaḥ śreñṭho rājādhipatiḥ sa mā jyaiñṭhyaṁ+ śraiñṭhyaṁ+ rājyādhipatyaṁ gamayaty aham evedaṁ+ sarvam asānīti.*

**Перевод:**

**Approaching and taking the pot in one’s hands, one utters, “Your name is ama. You are prāṇa. The prāṇa is the oldest, the best, the most radiant, and the ruler. May seniority, excellence, radiance and rulership not leave me. May I be the controller of all this.**

**Комментарий:**

Approaching or circling around (pratisṛpya), taking the pot in his hands, he utters “amo nāmāsi, your name is ama (strength). Ama is a name of prāṇa. You are prānā (amā). You are the whole world (te sarvam idam). The whole world is dependent on you, since the world is dependent on food in the form of the pot. This prāṇa, in the form of the pot (saḥ) is the oldest, the best, the radiant (rājā), the ruler. May they not leave me! May I become the controller of all this (aham eva sarvam idam asāni.)

### 5.2.7

*atha khalv etayarcā paccha ācāmati tat savitur vṛṇīmaha ity ācāmati vayaṁ devasya bhojanam ity ācāmati śreñṭhaṁ+ sarva-dhātamam ity ācāmati turaṁ bhagasya dhīmahīti*  
*sarvaṁ pibati nirṇijya kaṁ+saṁ camasaṁ vā paścād agneḥ saṁviśati carmaṇi vā sthaṇḍile vā vācaṁ yamo 'prasāhaḥ sa yadi striyaṁ paśyet samṛddhaṁ karmeti vidyāt.*

**Перевод:**

**He eats the residue in the pot while uttering the following mantra, portion by portion. Uttering tat savituḥ he eats the first portion. Uttering vayam devasya bhojaman, he eats the second portion. Uttering śreñṭhaṁ sarva-dhātamam, he eats the third portion. Uttering turaṁ bhagasya dhīmahi, he drinks everything.**

**Washing the copper cup or ladle, he should lie down behind the fire on an animal skin or on the ground, without speaking and without effort. If he sees a woman in his dream, he should know that his actions will be fruitful.**

**Комментарий:**

He eats the residue in the pot while uttering the following mantra, portion by portion.

The full mantra is tat savitur vṛṇīmahe vayaṁ devasya bhojanam śreñṭaṁ sarvadhātamaṁ turaṁ bhagasya dhīmahi. We pray (vṛṇīmahe) for food which is excellent (śreñṭḥam) and supports everything (sarva-dhātamam) from the sun who is nondifferent from prāṇa (savituḥ). Being satisfied with the food, we immediately (turam) meditate on the sun (bhagasya).

He should have a controlled mind so that he will not be overwhelmed by seeing bad dreams.

### 5.2.8

*tad eña ślokaḥ.*  
*yadā karmasu kāmyeñu*  
*striyaṁ+ svapneñu paśyati*  
*samṛddhiṁ tatra jānīyāt*  
*tasmin svapna-nidarśane tasmin svapna-nidarśana iti*

**Перевод:**

**There is a verse concerning this.**

**When one sees a woman in a dream when performing rituals for fulfilling desires, he should know that, on seeing that dream, his ritual will produce prosperity.**

## 3 - Pañcāgni-vidyā

### 5.3.1

*śvetaketur āruṇeyaḥ pañcālānāṁ+ samitim eyāya taṁ+ ha pravāhaṇo jaivalir uvāca kumārānu tvāśiñat pitety anu hi bhagava iti.*

**Перевод:**

**Śvetaketu, grandson of Aruṇa, came to the assembly of the Pañcālas. Pravahaṇa, son of Jivala said to him, “O young boy! Did your father instruct you?” “Yes sir, he did.”**

### 5.3.2

*vettha yad ito 'dhi prajāḥ prayantīti na bhagava iti vettha yathā punar āvartanta3 iti na bhagava iti vettha pathor deva-yānasya pitṛ-yāṇasya ca vyāvartanā3 iti na bhagava iti.*

**Перевод:**

**Do you know where people go on departing from here? No, I do not know, sir.**

**Do you know how they return? No, sir.**

**Do you know how the deva-yāna and pitṛ-yāna differ? No, sir.**

**Комментарий:**

The first question concerns the destination of those performing kāmya-karmas.

The second question concerns the method of returning by those influenced by karma.

The third question concerns the difference between the two paths.

### 5.3.3

*vettha yathāsau loko na sampūryata3 iti na bhagava iti vettha yathā pañcamyām āhutāv apaḥ puruña-vacaso bhavantīti naiva bhagava iti.*

**Перевод:**

**Do you not why that world does not get filled? No, sir.**

**Do why are the waters of the fifth oblation called puruña? No, sir.**

**Комментарий:**

Why do the heavens not become full by people going there constantly from this planet?

Water represents the other elements also in subtle form.

### 5.3.4

*athānu kim anuśiñṭo 'vocatha yo hīmāni na vidyāt kathaṁ+ so 'nuśiñṭo bravīteti sa hāyastaḥ pitur ardham eyāya taṁ+ hovācānanuśiñya vāva kila mā bhagavān bravīd anu tvāśiñam iti.*

**Перевод:**

**Then why did you say that you were taught? How can one who does not know these things say he has have been taught? He went to his father in distress and said, “Sir! Though not teaching me everything, you said that you had taught me.”**

### 5.3.5

*pañca mā rājanya-bandhuḥ praśnān aprākñīt teñāṁ naikaṁ ca nāśakaṁ vivaktum iti sa hovāca yathā mā tvaṁ tadaitān avado yathāham eñāṁ naikaṁ ca na veda yady aham imān avediñyaṁ kathaṁ te nāvakñyāmīti.*

**Перевод:**

**That person of the royal lineage asked me five questions and I could not answer one of them. The father answered. “I do not know the answer to one of the questions that you have described. If I had known them, why would I not tell you?”**

**Комментарий:**

Rājanya-bandhu means a person acting like a kñatriya. Just as you told me that you could not answer any of the questions, I also cannot answer one among them.

### 5.3.6

*sa ha gautamo rājño 'rdham eyāya tasmai ha prāptāyārhāṁ cakāra sa ha prātaḥ sa-bhāga udeyāya taṁ+ hovāca mānuñasya bhagavan gautama vittasya varaṁ vṛṇīthā iti sa hovāca tavaiva rājan mānuñaṁ vittaṁ yām eva kumārasyānte vācam abhāñathās tām eva me brūhīti sa ha kṛcchrī babhūva.*

**Перевод:**

**Gautama went to the king’s place. The king worshipped him when he arrived. In the morning, when the king was in the assembly, Gautama arrived. The king said, “O respected Gautama! Ask for a benediction of human wealth.” Gautama said, “O king! Let human wealth remain with you. Tell me the topics that you spoke to my son.” The king became sad.**

### 5.3.7

*taṁ ha ciraṁ vasety ājñāpayāṁ cakāra taṁ+ hovāca yathā mā tvaṁ gautamāvado yatheyaṁ na prāk tvattaḥ purā vidyā brāhmaṇān gacchati tasmād u sarveñu lokeñu kñatrasyaiva praśāsanam abhūd iti tasmai hovāca.*

**Перевод:**

**The king told him to stay there a long time. The king said, “I will do as you said. The knowledge that I will teach did not come from brāhmaṇas previous to you. The teachings came from kñatriyas in all places. “He then spoke it to him.**

**Комментарий:**

Since the teaching was not available among brāhmaṇas, it was not effective to approach brāhmaṇas to learn it. Therefore Gautama had to tolerate staying there a long time.

### 4

### 5.4.1

*asau vāva loko gautamāgnis tasyāditya eva samid raśmayo dhūmo 'har arciś candramā aṅgārā nakñatrāṇi visphuliṅgāḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**O Gaurama! Svarga is the fire. The sun is its firewood. Its rays are the smoke. The day is the flame. The moon is the ember. The constellations are the sparks.**

**Комментарий:**

The fifth question is answered first since the answer helps the other questions.

Svarga is lit by the sun as fire is fed by wood. Smoke (rays) arises from the wood (sun). The day illuminates like the flame. The moon produces the time that weakens the day. Thus it is compared to embers. Constellation are regarded as limbs of the moon. Thus they are compared to the sparks.

### 5.4.2

*tasminn etasminn agnau devāḥ śraddhāṁ juhvati tasyā āhuteḥ somo rājā sambhavati.*

**Перевод:**

**In that fire, the devatās offer faith. From that oblation king Soma arises.**

**The devatās are the senses. Faith is water for śruti says śraddhā vā āpah: water is faith. (Taittirīya-brāhmaṇa) The senses offer water into the fire of Svarga. Because faith, a special intellectual quality of the ātmā cannot offer anything, faith means water. Why should the senses make oblations made of subtle elements (called water) into the fire of Svarga? Since it is impossible for the jīva embraced by subtle elements to go to Svarga on its own, without use of senses, the senses perform the oblations. Or the actions to attain heaven depend on the senses. Having gone to Svarga by his karmas, the jīva becomes endowed with a body suitable to enjoy Svarga. Soma-rāja refers to the body made of subtle elements, and the jīva possessing that body.**

### 5

### 5.5.1

*parjanyo vāva gautamāgnis tasya vāyur eva samid abhraṁ dhūmo vidyud arcur aśanir aṅgārā hrādanayo visphuliṅgāḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**Parjanya is the fire. Vāyu is the firewood. Clouds are the smoke. Lightning is the flame. Thunderbolt is the ember. The thunder is the spark.**

**Комментарий:**

Parjanya is the devatā of rain. Hrādanayaḥ is the rumbling of clouds.

### 5.5.2

*tasminn etasminn agnau devāḥ somaṁ+ rājānaṁ juhvati tasyā āhuter varñaṁ+ sambhavati.*

**Перевод:**

**In that fire the devatās offer Somarājā. From that oblation arises rain.**

**Комментарий:**

With the exhaustion of karmas causing enjoyment on Svarga, the body called Somarājā, becoming liquid, falls with the jīva into cloud clusters.

### 6

### 5.6.1

*pṛthivī vāva gautamāgnis tasyāḥ saṁvatsara eva samid ākāśo dhūmo rātrir arcir diśo 'ṅgārā avāntara-diśo visphuliṅgāḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**O Gautama! Earth is the fire. The year is its fuel. Ākāśa is the smoke. Night is the flame. The directions are the embers. The intermediate directions are the sparks.**

**Комментарий:**

By the year, the earth produces crops. Since earth is the fire and earth is dark by its nature, night is said to be earth’s flame.

### 5.6.2

*tasminn etasminn agnau devā varñaṁ juhvati tasyā āhuter annaṁ+ sambhavati.*

**Перевод:**

**The devatās offer rain into the fire. From that oblation food arises.**

**Комментарий:**

The subtle elements, water, or faith, combining with the senses become a form for enjoying Svarga, and with the exhaustion of puṇyas, those elements dissolve and, meeting parjanya (devatā of rain, who is now the fire—oblations are offered into him), become rain, fall to earth and become various grains or food.

### 7

### 5.7.1

*puruño vāva gautamāgnis tasya vāg eva samit prāṇo dhūmo jihvārciś cakñur aṅgārāḥ śrotraṁ visphuliṅgāḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**O Gautama! Man is the fire. His voice is the firewood. Prāṇa is the smoke. His tongue is the flame. His eyes are the embers. His ears are the sparks.**

**Комментарий:**

Man becomes fuelled up (samidhyate) by words. He becomes eloquent.

### 5.7.2

*tasminn etasminn agnau devā annaṁ juhvati tasyā āhute retaḥ sambhavati.*

**Перевод:**

**The devatās offer food into the fire. From that oblation arises semen.**

**Комментарий:**

The subtle elements attached to the jīva in the form of food are eaten by man (the fire) and become semen.

### 8

### 5.8.1

*yoñā vāva gautamāgnis tasyā upastha eva samid yad upamantrayate sa dhūmo yonir arcir yad antaḥ karoti te 'ṅgārā abhinandā visphuliṅgāḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**O Gautama! Woman is the fire. Her sexual organ is the firewood. The invitation is the smoke. The womb is the flame. The act is the embers. The pleasure is the sparks.**

**Комментарий:**

Invitation means indicating desire for union. The womb being red is equated with the flame. Antaḥ karoti means the sex act. Abhinandā means the small amount of pleasure. The semen enters the woman by the womb and matures as a foetus.

### 5.8.2

*tasminn etasminn agnau devā reto juhvati tasyā āhuter garbhaḥ sambhavati.*

**Перевод:**

**The devatās offer semen into that fire. From the oblation arises the fetus.**

**Комментарий:**

The semen enters the woman by the womb and matures as a fetus.

### 9

### 5.9.1

*iti tu pañcamyām āhutāv āpaḥ puruña-vacaso bhavantīti sa ulbāvṛto garbho daśa vā nava vā māsān antaḥ śayitvā yāvad vātha jāyate.*

**Перевод:**

**The water in the fifth oblation is called man. The fetus enclosed in membrane remains for nine or ten months until it is born.**

**Комментарий:**

Among the oblations of faith (water), Somarāja, rain, food and semen, semen is the fifth oblation. In that fifth oblation, the subtle elements called water, are called man. The word iti indicates that the fifth question has been answered.

### 5.9.2

*sa jāto yāvad āyuñaṁ jīvati taṁ pretaṁ diñṭam ito 'gnaya eva haranti yata eveto yataḥ sambhūto bhavati.*

**Перевод:**

**Being born, he lives his length of life. The dead person is destined for the next world. The fires carry him from where he came, from where he was born.**

**Комментарий:**

The fetus, surrounded by the membrane, remaining in the womb for nine, ten, eleven or twelve months, is then born. Being born he lives his life. At the end of life, the dead man is destined for the next world (diñṭam). The devatās or fires carry him from heaven, parjanya and other fires by which he came here, and from the fire of the womb from which he was born. Then he again appears in heaven, rain, earth, semen and the womb.

The description is given to invoke renunciation.

Covered with stool, urine, wind and mucus from the womb, covered by the membrane, affected by the pungent drinks of the mother, seed of semen and blood--the fetus, grows, but remains a long time there, restricted in virility, strength, ability, power and knowledge.

Then he emerges in pain, with difficulty, from the womb, and lives life according to karma. Again he mounts the turning wheel, and rotates in many births. Hearing this produces detachment. This is the purpose of the description.

### 10

### 5.10.1

*tad ya itthaṁ viduḥ. ye ceme 'raṇye śraddhā tapa ity upāsate te 'rciñam abhisambhavanty arciño 'har ahna āpūryamāṇa-pakñam āpūryamāṇa-pakñād yān ñaḍ udaṅṅ eti māsāṁ+s tān.*

**Перевод:**

**Those who know this wandering jīva, or those who meditate on Brahman in the forest with faith and austerity, attain the deity of fire. From fire they attain the day, from day they attain the waxing phase of the moon, from there they attain the six months of uttarāyaṇa.**

**Комментарий:**

The third question concerning the difference of the two paths is answered.

Tat refers to the jīva wandering in saṁsāra, previously described. They know this jīva, who is different from his material body, which assumes forms of faith, soma, rain, food and semen in Svarga, rain, earth, man and woman. They also know the jīva has the Lord within himself. This is stated in Vedānta sutra 4.3.15. This person attains Brahman. These persons attains (abhisaṁbhavanti) the deity of fire.

### 5.10.2

*māsebhyaḥ saṁvatsaraṁ+ saṁvatsarād ādityam ādityāc candramasaṁ candramaso vidyutaṁ tat puruño 'mānavaḥ sa enān brahma gamayaty eña deva-yānaḥ panthā iti.*

**Перевод:**

**From the six months they attain the year, from the year they attain the sun. From the sun they attain the moon. From the moon they attain lightning. From there the spiritual being takes him to Brahman (the Lord). This is the deva-yāna panthā, the path of the devatās.**

**Комментарий:**

The words such as fire, day, fortnight etc. mean the devatās. From lightning (tat) they attain the puruña who is devoid of saṁsāra (amānavaḥ). Coming from the spiritual world, he makes the two types of meditators attain the planet of the Lord. Amānavaḥ sa etya brahmalokān gamayati: the spiritual person comes and takes him to the spiritual planets. Bṛhad-ārāṇayaka Upaniñad)

### 5.10.3

*atha ya ime grāma iñṭā-pūrte dattam ity upāsate te dhūmam abhisambhavanti dhūmād rātriṁ rātrer apara-pakñam apara-pakñād yān ñaḍ dakñiṇaiti māsāṁ+s tān naite saṁvatsaram abhiprāpnuvanti.*

**Перевод:**

**Those who perform charitable acts in the village go to smoke, from smoke to night, from night to the waning phase of the moon, from there to the southern course of the sun, but they do not attain the year.**

**Комментарий:**

Those men live in the village and perform activities such as sacrifices, charity and homa (īñṭa-pūrte) and related actions like fasting (iti). Or they perform those actions with faint (iti). They attain the deity of smoke (dhūsmam). They attain successively the various devatās.

### 5.10.4

*māsebhyaḥ pitṛ-lokaṁ pitṛ-lokād ākāśam ākāśāc candramasam eña somo rājā tad devānām annaṁ taṁ devā bhakñayanti.*

**Перевод:**

**From the six months of the southern course of the sun they attain pitṛ-loka. From pitṛ-loka they go to ākāśa. From ākāśa they go to the moon. This is Somarājā. He is food for the devatās. The devatās eat him.**

**Комментарий:**

This answers the first question concerning where people go on dying. He does not become the moon deity, but rather attains a body capable of enjoying on that planet. He becomes an assistant of the devatās like food. The resident devatās engage him in their service like an animal. This is explained in Vedānta-sūtra [3.1.7](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vs/3.1.7).

### 5.10.5

*tasmin yāvat sampātam uñitvāthaitam evādhvānaṁ pūnar nivartante yathetam ākāśam ākāśād vāyuṁ vāyur bhūtvā dhūmo bhavati dhūmo bhūtvābhraṁ bhavati.*

**Перевод:**

**Remaining there until their karmas are exhausted, they return by the same path. Attaining ākāśa, from ākāśa they go to Vāyu, from Vāyu to smoke and from smoke to a cloud.**

**Комментарий:**

Spending up the karmas that brought them there, not all their karmas, they return by the same path. This answers the second question about how they return. They return by the same path in reverse order. They meet ākāśa and then Vāyu. Thought it says they become Vāyu and the smoke (bhūtvā), this means they meet the devatās of Vāyu and smoke. They do not develop bodies of Vāyu or smoke. The devatās of those stages remain from creation till destruction of the universe. Becoming these devatās would be irrelevant for the jīvas descending from Svarga at every moment. The waters that make up the celestial bodies of the jīvas on the moon planet, at the exhaustion of their good karmas, turn to liquid and enter ākāśa. They jīvas are in close contact with ākāśa and, having had difference in their bodies destroyed, are similar. From there those water bodies are led from there by Vāyu and become similar to Vāyu. In close contact with Vāyu the jīvas are said to become Vāyu (vāyur bhutvā). The jivas attain clouds holding water (abhram). Abhra is derived from apo vibharti, carrying water.

### 5.10.6

*abhraṁ bhūtvā megho bhavati megho bhūtvā pravarñati ta iha vrīhi-yavā oñadhi-vanaspatayas tila-māñā iti jāyante 'to vai khalu durniñprapataraṁ yo yo hy annam atti yo retaḥ siñcati tad bhūya eva bhavati.*

**Перевод:**

**The jīvas with their bodies attain water bearing clouds and then water showering clouds. Then they are shower down with the rain. They are born within rice, barley, herbs, sesame and dhal. It is difficult for them to escape this condition. When a man eat those plants as food and then eject semen, those jīvas enter the semen.**

**Комментарий:**

Megha is derived from mih, to sprinkle. Thus here megha means a cloud about to shower water. In close contact with the sprinkling cloud, the jīva the falls to earth in the showers.

In contact with the showers and embraced by the subtle elements, the jīvas attain the bhāva of those plants but do not become those plants, since it is not here said that they had karmas to make them come plants. Rather they are in close contact with those plants. This is explained.

*anyādhiñṭhite pūrvavad abhilāpāt*

**The jīvas do not become plants, since other jīvas are in those bodies as their karma. They merely contact the plants just as previously they contacted ether and rain, and attaining such a body by karma is described using other words. Vedānta-sūtra** [**3.1.25**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vs/3.1.25)

They leave those places after a long time (durniñprapataram). This is a long time compared to the time of passing from ākāśas to Vāyu etc. The grains flowing from the sides of mountains in streams enter rivers and then the ocean. They are eaten by makaras and other fish. These are eaten by others. Along with the fish they all merge in the ocean. Pulled up by the clouds along with the ocean water, the jīvas again are sprinkled from clouds on deserts or stones or buildings. Falling, they remain there. Sometimes they are drunk by deer or tigers, and these are eaten by other animals. This continues. Sometimes they are born with plants which are not eaten, but dry up. When they are born with plants and eaten, they enter semen and taken a body. This is very rare among all the plants.

The descending jīva in contact with the grain later (bhuyaḥ) becomes that person who eats food and ejects semen. This means he enters that person and enters the semen.

### 5.10.7

*tad ya iha ramaṇīya-caraṇā abhyāśo ha yat te ramanīyāṁ yonim āpadyeran brāhmaṇa-yoniṁ vā kñatriya-yoniṁ vā vaiśya-yoniṁ vātha ya iha kapūya-caraṇā abhyāśo ha yatte kapūyāṁ yonim āpadyeran śva-yoniṁ vā sūkara-yoniṁ vā caṇḍāla-yoniṁ vā.*

**Перевод:**

**Those of good conduct, descending from above, will be born in auspicious wombs, as brāhmaṇas, kñatriyas or vaiśyas. Those who did sinful acts, and descended will be born in sinful wombs such as those of dogs, pigs or caṇḍālas.**

**Комментарий:**

Abhyāsaḥ here means “coming or approaching.” It is well known (ha), that among them (tat), coming here, from the other planets, when (yat) they have remaining sukṛtis after enjoyment, they attain good birth as brāhmaṇas etc. When they have remaining contemptible karmas (kapuya-caraṇāḥ), they take birth in low wombs.

### 5.10.8

*athaitayoḥ pathor na katareṇa ca na tānīmāni kñudrāṇy asakṛd āvartīni bhūtāni bhavanti jāyasva mriyasvety etat tṛtīyaṁ sthānaṁ tenāsau loko na sampūryate tasmāj jagupseta tad eña ślokaḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**Those creatures who do not go by either of these two paths become small creatures born again and again in such bodies. Continually they are born and die in this condition. That is the third condition. By that condition of sin, the heavenly planets do not become full. Therefore one should feel disgust with the world of saṁsāra. There is a famous verse about this.**

**Комментарий:**

The fourth question is here answered. Atha indicates the remaining answer. Whatever creatures do not go by either of (katarenacana) the two paths, devoid of either knowledge or karma to put them on these two paths, become small creatures like flies, mosquitoes etc.

Again and again they are born and die in that condition (jāyasva mṛyasva). Imperative verb form is used to indicate repetition.

This condition of sinful acts (tat) is the third condition (tritīyam sthānam), the condition of the small entities as compared to those who go to Svarga or the spiritual world. It is designated as the third condition, meaning sinful actions. Because of sinful acts designated as the third condition, the heavenly planet is not become full. This means it is not attained (saṁpuryate). Thus the third condition repeats the previous statement of being continuously born and dying (jāyasva mṛyasva). Since jīvas generally are spread in this condition, and very few go to the heavenly planets, the heavenly planets do not become full. By their not ascending to and descending from the heavenly planets, the heavenly planets do not become full.

Since the life of those in saṁsāra is filled with difficulties, one should feel disgust with it (tasmāt). “I should not fall into such a great ocean in this world.”

### 5.10.9

*steno hiraṇyasya surāṁ pibaṁś ca*  
*guros talpam āvasan brahma-hā ca*  
*ete patanti catvāraḥ*  
*pañcamaś catvāraṁ+s tair iti*

**Перевод:**

**Those who steals gold, drinks liquor, sleeps with his guru’s wife, kills a brāhmaṇa, these four, and the fifth, the person who associates with these four, fall.**

**Комментарий:**

Another result of knowledge of the five fires which gives liberation is described.

### 5.10.10

*atha ha ya etān evaṁ pañcāgnīn veda na sa ha tair apy ācaran pāpmanā lipyate śuddhaḥ pūtaḥ puṇya-loko bhavati ya evaṁ veda ya evaṁ veda.*

**Перевод:**

**He who know the five fires in this way, will not be contaminated with sin if he associates with these five types. He who knows this is pure and bright, and attains the worlds of the pure.**

**Комментарий:**

He is devoid of sins past and future.

## 11 - Vaiśvānara-vidyā

### 5.11.1

*prācīnaśāla aupamanyavaḥ satyayajñaḥ pauluñir indradyuno bhāllaveyo janaḥ śārkarākñyo buḍila āśvatarāśvis te haite mahā-śālā mahā-śrotriyāḥ sametya mīmāṁ+sāṁ cakruḥ ko nu ātmā kiṁ brahmeti.*

**Перевод:**

**The son of Upamanyu, called Prāśinaśāla, the son of Puluña named Satya-yajñā, the son of bhāllavi named Indradyumna, the son of Śarkarākñya named Janana, the son of Āśvataraśva named Buḍila-- great householders, who were very learned in studies of the Vedas-- gathered together and deliberated on the nature of Brahman, the ātmā within all beings.**

**Комментарий:**

The son of Upamanyu, called Prāśinaśāla, the son of Puluña names Satya-yajñā, the son of bhāllavi named Indradyumna, the son of Śarkarākñya named Janana, the son of Āśvataraśva named Buḍila, great householders having big houses (mahāśalāḥ), who were very learned in studies of the Vedas (mahā-śrotriyāḥ), gathered together and deliberated on the nature of Brahman, dwelling as antaryāmī, ātmā within them.

### 5.11.2

*te ha sampādayāṁ cakrur uddālako vai bhagavanto 'yam āruṇiḥ sampratīmam ātmānam vaiśvānaram adhyeti taṁ+ hantābhyāgacchāmeti taṁ+ hābhyājagmuḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**They decided. “Uddālaka, the son of Aruṇi, knows Brahman. Let us go to him.” They approached him.**

**Комментарий:**

Deliberating, they decided on (sampādayāṁ cakruḥ) some teacher of Brahman who is ātmā.

Revered ones! The son of Aruṇa Uddālaka now knows (adhyeti) ātmā known as Vaiśvānara.

### 5.11.3

*sa ha sampādayāṁ cakāra prakñyanti mām ime mahā-śālā mahā-śrotriyās tebhyo na sarvam iva pratipatsye hantāham anyam abhyanuśāsānīti.*

**Перевод:**

**Uddālaka thought, “These great householders, great kñatriyas will ask me. I cannot speak everything to them. I should direct them to another person.”**

**Комментарий:**

Seeing them come, Uddālaka thought, “They are great householders, great kñatriyas. They will ask me about Vaiśvanara. I cannot speak, presenting everything to them. He concluded that he should tell them of someone who had that knowledge.

### 5.11.4

*tān hovācāśvapatir vai bhavaganto 'yaṁ kaikeva sampratīmam ātmānaṁ vaiśvānaram adhyeti taṁ+ hantābhyāgacchāmeti taṁ hābhyājagmuḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**He said to them, “Aśvapati, son of Kekaya, knows Vaiśvānara Brahman. Let us go to him.” They all approached him.**

### 5.11.5

*tebhyo ha prāptebhyaḥ pṛthag arhāṇi kārayāṁ cakāra sa ha prātaḥ sañjihāna uvāca na me steno janapade na kadaryo na madya-po nānāhitāgnir nāvidvān na svarī svariṇī kuto yakñyamāṇo vai bhagavanto 'ham asmi yāvad ekaikasmā ṛtvije dhanaṁ dāsyāmi tāvad bhagavadbhyo dāsyāmi vasantu bhagavanta iti.*

**Перевод:**

**When they arrived, he had gifts given to them individually. In the morning, leaving his room, the king said, “Respectable sirs! In my kingdom there is no thief, no pauper, no drunkard, no qualified person without a fire altar, no person of the three upper varṇas without knowledge of the Vedas, no adulterer and no prostitute at all. I will perform a sacrifice. Respected gentlemen! I will give the fees that I give to the priests to each of you. Please stay here, respected sirs!”**

**Комментарий:**

I do not live off the wealth of such persons. Thus the gifts are untainted. Very soon I will have a sacrifice performed. As much wealth that I give to each of the priests I will give to each of you. Stay here till that time.

### 5.11.6

*te hocur yena haivārthena puruñaś caret taṁ+ haiva vaded ātmānam evemaṁ vaiśvānaraṁ+ sampraty adhyeñi tam eva no brūhīti.*

**Перевод:**

**They said, “One should speak only about that for which a person comes. You know about Vaiśvānara Brahman. Teach that to us.”**

**Комментарий:**

One should speak the topic for which a person has come. You should fulfil his desire. We want to know about Vaiśvānara ātmā. You are now meditating on that ātmā. Do not refuse, and say I will not teach it to you. Do not offer something else. Vaiśvānara means he who leads men or the world (viśvān nayati). Viśvanāra becomes vaiśvānara just as rakña becomes rākñasa or vaya becomes vāyasa.

### 5.11.7

*tān hovāca prātar vaḥ prativaktāsmīti te ha samit-pāṇayaḥ pūrvāhne praticakramire tān hānupanīyaivaitad uvāca.*

**Перевод:**

**He said to them, “I will answer you tomorrow.” They came before noon with wood in their hands. He taught them without giving initiation to them.**

**Комментарий:**

To purify them of pride he said that tomorrow he would answer them. Understanding the king’s intention, carrying firewood in their hands, they in the forenoon, saying “Make us students.” The king saw that they had given up pride in being brāhmaṇas by asking for initiation by bringing the wood, and also understood that they were qualified for being taught. But he also understood the scripture injunction that brāhmaṇas should not be given initiation by a non-brāhmaṇa. He did not accept the firewood they offered for initiation, but in friendship gave them the teachings. Giving up pride in their large houses, and carrying wood in their hands, they came to the king who was of lower birth and asked for knowledge. Similarly others as well who desire knowledge should act in the same way.

### 12

### 5.12.1

*aupamanyava kaṁ tvam ātmānam upāssa iti divam eva bhagavo rājann iti hovācaiña vai su-tejā ātmā vaiśvānaro yaṁ tvam ātmānam upāsse tasmāt tava sutaṁ prasutam āsutaṁ kule dṛśyate.*

**Перевод:**

**O Aupamanyava! Who do you worship as ātmā? He said, “O respected king! I worship the heavenly planets as Vaiśvānara.” The king said, “The Brahman you worship as the heavens is called Sutejā. Because of that worship pressing of soma (having many sons) is seen in your family.”**

**Комментарий:**

Wanting to teach them about Vaiśvānara ātmā, he began to question them one by one, desiring to know which parts they knew--understanding how much they knew and did now know about Vaiśvānara ātmā. “Dear sir! I meditate on Vaiśvānara, whose body is heavenly planets.” The king then spoke. That Vaiśvānara ātmā who you worship as heaven is Sutejā: that which has brilliant effulgence. Heaven is brilliant because of the sun, moon etc. The Vaiśvānara that you worship as heaven is called Sutejā because it is endowed with brilliant light. Suta-prasutam āsutam means sacrifices involving pressing out soma, which last one day, several days or many days respectively. Thus by worshipping the heavens as Brahman one attains many sons.

### 5.12.2

*atsy annaṁ paśyasi priyam atty annaṁ paśyati priyaṁ bhavaty asya brahma-varcasaṁ kule ya etam evam ātmānaṁ vaiśvānaram upāste mūrdhā tv eña ātmana iti hovāca mūrdhā te vyapatiñyad yan māṁ nāgamiñya iti.*

**Перевод:**

**You eat food. You see dear ones. He who meditates on this Vaiśvānara eats food, sees dear ones and has knowledge of Brahman in his family. But this is only the head of Brahman. If you had not come to me, your head would have fallen off.**

**Комментарий:**

By the power of that worship you experience worldly enjoyment (annam). You see dear ones such as sons. One who worships this Vaiśvānara that you worship also experiences worldly enjoyments, sons and progeny who know Brahman. He then teaches him that this worship is partial, by showing the result. Worshipping limited heaven as Vaiśvānara is only the heard of Vaiśvānara, one limb, not Vaiśvānara himself. Thinking of heaven, which is only the head, as the whole of Vaiśvānara would produce a great mistake, since it is opposite knowledge, if you had not come to me for the truth. You acted properly, since you came to me.

### 13

### 5.13.1

*atha hovāca satyayajñaṁ pauluñiṁ prācīnayogya kaṁ tvam ātmānam upāssa ity ādityam eva bhagavo rājann iti hovācaiña vai viśva-rūpa ātmā vaiśvānaro yaṁ tvam ātmānam upāsse tasmāt tava bahu viśva-rūpaṁ kule dṛśyate.*

**Перевод:**

**Then he spoke to Satya-yajña, son of Puluña. O Prācīna-yogya! Who do you worship as ātmā?” He replied, “Respected king! I worship the sun as ātmā.” The king said, “That Vaiśvānara ātmā whom you worship is viśva-rūpa, illuminator of the universe. Therefore it is seen that there are many objects effulgent objects in your family.”**

**Комментарий:**

Prācīna-yogya is another name of Satya-yajña. Viśva-rūpa means he who illuminates or manifests (rūpyate) the universe. Since the sun illuminates the universe it is called viśva-rūpa. By that worship or meditation, there is seen in your family many things like sons and gems which light up the world.

### 5.13.2

*pravṛtto 'śvatarī-ratho dāsī-niñko 'tsy annaṁ paśyasi priyam atty annaṁ paśyati priyaṁ bhavaty asya brahma-varcasaṁ kule ya etam evam ātmānaṁ upāste caksūñ ṭv etad ātmana iti hovācāndho 'bhaviñyo yan māṁ nāgamiñya iti.*

**Перевод:**

**You possess mule drawn cars and necklaces worn by female servants. You enjoy pleasures and see many sons. He who worships ātmā in this way will enjoy pleasures and see many sons, and have progeny who know Brahman. This the eye of Brahman. If you had not come to me you would go blind.**

**Комментарий:**

You have under your control (pravṛttaḥ) carts drawn by mules (āśvatarī-rathaḥ), and necklaces worn by female servants (dāsī-niñkaḥ).

### 14

### 5.14.1

*atha hovācendradyumnaṁ bhāllaveyaṁ vaiyāghra-padya kaṁ tvam ātmānam upāssa iti vāyum eva bhagavo rājann iti hovācaiña pṛthag-vartmātmā vaiśvānaro yaṁ tvam ātmānam upāsse tasmāt tvāṁ pṛthag-balaya āyanti prṭhag-ratha-śreṇayo 'nuyanti.*

**Перевод:**

**The king asked Indradyumna, son of Bhallavi, “O Vaiyāghra-padya! Who do you worship as Brahman?” He answered, “Respected king! I worship Vāyu as Brahman.” “Vaiśvānara ātmā whom you worship as Brahman is called “pṛthag-varmā,” various paths. From that worship,**

**various offerings come to you. Groups of various chariots come to you.”**

**Комментарий:**

Vāyu or air moves about in various ways. Thus it is called “various paths.” Food and cloth from various directions come to you. Groups of chariots follow to you.

### 5.14.2

*atsy annaṁ paśyasi priyam atty annaṁ paśyati priyaṁ bhavaty asya brahma-varcasaṁ kule ya etam evam ātmānaṁ vaiśvānaram upāste prāṇas tv eña ātmana iti hovāca prāṇasya udakramiñyan yan māṁ nāgamiñya iti.*

**Перевод:**

**You eat food. You see dear ones. He who meditates on this Vaiśvānara eats food, sees dear ones and has knowledge of Brahman in his family. But this is only the prāṇa of Brahman. If you had not come to me, your prāṇas would leave your body.**

### 15

### 5.15.1

*atha hovāc janaṁ śārkarākñyam śārkārākñya! kaṁ tvam ātmānam upāssa iti ākāśam eva bhagavo rājann iti hovācaiña bahula ātmā vaiśvānaro yaṁ tvam ātmānam upāsse tasmāt tvāṁ bahulo ‘si prajayā ca dhanena ca*

**Перевод:**

**Then the king said to Jana, the son of Śarkarāksa, “O son of Sarkāraksa! Whom do you worship as ātmā?” He said, “Revered sir! I worship ākāśa as ātmā.” Vaiśvānara ātmā whom you worship is Bahula, abundant. From that worship you have abundance of offspring and wealth.**

**Комментарий:**

Because ākāśa is the greatest of the elements, it is called bahula, abundant.

### 5.15.2

*atsy annaṁ paśyasi priyam atty annaṁ paśyati priyaṁ bhavaty asya brahma-varcasaṁ kule ya etam evam ātmānaṁ vaiśvānaram upāste saṁdehas tv eña ātmana iti hovāca saṁdehast te vyaśīryad yan māṁ nāgamiñya iti.*

**Перевод:**

**You eat food. You see dear ones. He who meditates on this Vaiśvānara eats food, sees dear ones and has knowledge of Brahman in his family. But this is only the trunk of Brahman. If you had not come to me, your trunk would be destroyed.**

**Комментарий:**

Saṁdeha means the middle of the body. “Is that the upper body or lower body?” Because of this doubt (saṁdeha) it is called saṁdeha (doubtful part of the body). Viśīryad means “it would be destroyed.”

### 16

### 5.16.1

*atha hovāca buḍilam āśvatarāśviṁ vaiyāghra-padya kaṁ tvam ātmānam upāssa iti apa eva bhagavo rājann iti hovācaiña vai rayir ātmā vaiśvānaro yaṁ tvam ātmānam upāsse tasmāt tvaṁ+ rayi-mān puñṭi-mān asi.*

**Перевод:**

**Then the king said to Budila, the son of Aśvataraśva, “O son of Vaighra-padya! Whom do you worship as ātmā?” He said, “Revered sir! I worship water as ātmā.” Vaiśvānara ātmā whom you worship is Rayi, treasure. From that worship you are wealthy and plump.**

### 5.16.2

*atsy annaṁ paśyasi priyam atty annaṁ paśyati priyaṁ bhavaty asya brahma-varcasaṁ kule ya etam evam ātmānaṁ vaiśvānaram upāste bastir eña ātmana iti hovāca bastir te vyabhetsyad yan māṁ nāgamiñya iti.*

**Перевод:**

**You eat food. You see dear ones. He who meditates on this Vaiśvānara eats food, sees dear ones and has knowledge of Brahman in his family. But this is only the bladder of Brahman. If you had not come to me, your bladder would have burst.**

**Комментарий:**

Rivers have swift currents (raya) and thus water is called rayi. Or because water is a cause of gaining wealth (raya), it is called rayi. Rayimān means wealth. Basti is the bladder.

### 17

### 5.17.1

*atha hovācoddālakam āruṇiṁ gautama kaṁ tvam ātmānam upāssa iti pṛthivī eva bhagavo rājann iti hovācaiña vai pratiñṭhātmā vaiśvānaro yaṁ tvam ātmānam upāsse tasmāt tvaṁ pratiñṭhito 'si prajayā ca paśubhiś ca.*

**Перевод:**

**Then the king said to Uddālaka, the son of Aruṇi, “O Gautama! Whom do you worship as ātmā?” He said, “Revered sir! I worship earth as ātmā.” Vaiśvānara ātmā whom you worship is Pratiñṭhā, the support. From that worship you are well established with sons and animals.”**

### 5.17.2

*atsy annaṁ paśyasi priyam atty annaṁ paśyati priyaṁ bhavaty asya brahma-varcasaṁ kule ya etam evam ātmānaṁ vaiśvānaram upāste pādau tv etāv ātmana iti hovāca pādau te vyamlāsy etāṁ yan māṁ nāgamiñya iti.*

**Перевод:**

**You eat food. You see dear ones. He who meditates on this Vaiśvānara eats food, sees dear ones and has knowledge of Brahman in his family. But this is only the feet of Brahman. If you had not come to me, your feet would withered.**

**Комментарий:**

Because earth supports all beings, it is called pratiñṭha, fixed. Vyamāsyetām means the feet would have withered or become limp.

### 18

### 5.18.1

*tān hovācaite vai khalu yūyaṁ pṛthag ivemam ātmānaṁ vaiśvānaraṁ vidvāṁso 'nnam attha yas tv etam evaṁ prādeśa-mātram abhivimānam ātmānaṁ vaiśvānaram upaste sa sarveñu lokeñu sarveñu bhūteñu sarveñv ātmasv annam atti.*

**Перевод:**

**You know Vaiśvānara ātmā in various ways and experience worldly pleasures accordingly. He who meditates on Vaiśvānara ātmā as the all-pervading, who accepts limited forms, experiences Brahman in all planets, all beings and all ātmās.**

**Комментарий:**

The king spoke to the sages again. You, of this nature (ete), know Vaiśvānara ātmā in a various types of worship (pṛthak) and experience worldly pleasures (annam) according to that worship. You eat food and see sons etc. He who worship the Vaiśvānara ātmā, limited to particular places like heaven (prādeśa-mātram), but who is spread everywhere (abhivimānam), who is the ātmā of special persons, not everyone, experiences Brahman (annam atti) who exists in all planets, all beings and in all ātmās. Annam cannot refer to ordinary food since aspirants of liberation reject the eating of persons under control of karma. The limitation on the Lord related to heaven etc. is for use in yoga.

### 5.18.2

*tasya ha vā etasyātmano vaiśvānarasya mūrdhaiva su-tejāś cakñur viśva-rūpaḥ prāṇaḥ pṛthag-vartmātmā sandeho bahulo bastir eva rayiḥ pṛthivy eva pādāv uru eva vedir lomāni barhir hṛdayaṁ gārhapatyo mano 'nvārhāryapacana āstyam āhavanīyaḥ*

**Перевод:**

**The head of Vaiśvānara and the worshipper is the heavens. The eye is the sun. The prāṇa is Vāyu. The trunk is ākāśa. The bladder is water. The feet are earth. One’s chest is the fire pit, one’s hairs are the kuśa grass, one’s heart is the gārhapatya fire, one’s mind is the anvārhārya-pacana fire and one’s face is the āhavanīya fire.**

**Комментарий:**

By the offering of oblations to prāṇa etc. in the three fires within the body, being fixed in Vaiśvānara-vidya while thinking that one’s head as heaven etc., one worships Paramātmā. Sutejā is designated as the head of that (tasya) Vaiśvānara ātmā and of the worshipper (etasya). One should contemplate that the head is the head of the worshiper. The head of the Vaiśvānara ātmā is described within the body of the worshipper. He is the recipient of oblations to prāṇas made by the worshipper.
