The head or sutejā of the worshipper is the head of the Lord which is the heavens. His eye or viśvarūpa or sun is the eye of the Lord. Prāṇa or various paths or air is the prāṇa of the Lord. The trunk of the worshipper or ākāśa is the trunk of the Lord. The bladder of the worshipper or rayi or water is the water and bladder of the Lord. The feet of the worshipper or earth are the feet of the Lord. In this way in the body of the worshipper, the Lord, Vaiśvānara, who has the three worlds as his body, and who is present there, should be the object of meditation. The thighs, hairs, heart, mind, and face of the worshipper should be imagined to be the fire pit, kuśa grass, gārhapatya fire, anvāhāryapacana fire and the ahavanīya fire, elements of the fire sacrifice meant for Vaiśvānara, the Lord, who is the recipient of the oblations to prāṇa. The oblations to prāṇa constitute the sacrifice. In this way one should worship Vaiśvānara, the Lord by the oblations of prāṇa.

### 19

### 5.19.1

*Tad yad bhaktaṁ prathamam āgacchet tad dhomīyaṁ+ sa yāṁ prathamām āhutiṁ juhuyāt tāṁ juhuyāt prāṇāya svāheti prāṇas tṛpyati.*

**Перевод:**

**The first food which comes to the person (tat) should be offered (homīyam) as an oblation. When he offers the first oblation, he should offer saying “praṇaya svāhā.” Offering in this way the prāṇa is satisfied.**

**Комментарий:**

The first food (bhaktam) which comes to the person (tat) should be offered (homīyam) as an oblation. In offering the first oblation saying praṇaya svāhā, the prāṇa is satisfied.

### 5.19.2

*prāṇe tṛpyati cakñus tṛpyati cakñuñi tṛpyaty ādityas tṛpyaty āditye tṛpyati dyaus tṛpyati divi tṛpyantyāṁ yat kiñca dyauś cādityaś cādhitiñṭhatas tṛpyati tasyānu tṛptiṁ tṛpyati prajayā paśubhir annādyena tejasā brahma-varcaseneti.*

**Перевод:**

**When prāṇa is satisfied, the eye is satisfied. When the eye is satisfied, the sun is satisfied, when the sun is satisfied, heaven is satisfied. When heaven is satisfied, everything under control of heaven and the sun is satisfied. Then all these are satisfied, the sacrificer is then satisfied with offspring, animals, abundant food, power arising from studying the Vedas.**

**Комментарий:**

Mention of the eye, sun and heaven being satisfied means the devatās of the eye, sun and heaven become satisfied since unconscious objects cannot become satisfied.

### 20

### 5.20.1

*athaṁ yāṁ dvitiyāṁ juhuyāt tāṁ juhuyāt vyānāya svāheti. Vyānas tṛpyati.*

**Перевод:**

**When he offers the second oblation of food, he should offer saying vyānāya svāhā. By that the vyāna is satisfied.**

### 5.20.2

*vyāne tṛpyati śrotraṁ tṛpyati. śrotre tṛpyati candramāstṛpyati.*  
*candramasi tṛpyati diśastṛpyanti. dikñu tṛpyantiñu yatkiñca diśaścandramāścādhitiñṭhanti tat tṛpyati. tasyānu tṛptiṁ tṛpyati prajayā paśubhirannādyena tejasā brahmavarcaseneti*

**Перевод:**

**When vyāna is satisfied, the ear is satisfied. When the ear is satisfied the moon is satisfied. When the moon is satisfied the directions are satisfied. When the directions are satisfied everything controlled by the directions and moon is satisfied. After satisfying all these, the sacrificer become satisfied with offspring, animals, abundant food, power arising from studying the Vedas.**

### 21

### 5.21.1

*atha yāṁ tṛtīyāṁ juhuyāt tāṁ juhuyāt apānāya svāheti. apānastṛpyati.*

**Перевод:**

**When he offers the third oblation he should offer saying, apānāya svāhā. Apāna becomes satisfied**

### 5.21.2

*apāne tṛpyati vāk tṛpyati. vāci tṛpyantyām agnistṛpyati. agnau tṛpyati*  
*pṛthivī tṛpyati. pṛthivyāṁ tṛpyantyāṁ yatkiñca pṛthivī*  
*cāgniścādhitiñṭhataḥ tat tṛpyati. tasyānu tṛptiṁ tṛpyati prajayā*  
*paśubhirannādyena tejasā brahmavarcaseneti.*

**Перевод:**

**When apāna is satisfied, the voice is satisfied. When the voice is satisfied, fire is satisfied. When fire is satisfied the earth are satisfied. When the earth are satisfied everything controlled by the earth and fire is satisfied. After satisfying all these, the sacrificer become satisfied with offspring, animals, abundant food, and power arising from studying the Vedas.**

### 22

### 5.22.1

*atha yāṁ caturthiṁ juhuyāt tāṁ juhuyāt samānāya svāheti. Samānas tṛpyati.*

**Перевод:**

**When he offers the fourth oblation he should offer saying, samānāya svāhā. Samāna becomes satisfied**

### 5.22.2

*samāne tṛpyati manastṛpyati. manasi tṛpyati parjanyastṛpyati. parjanye*  
*tṛpyati vidyut tṛpyati. vidyuti tṛpyantyāṁ yatkiñca vidyucca*  
*parjanyaścādhitiñṭhataḥ tat tṛpyati. tasyānu tṛptiṁ tṛpyati prajayā*  
*paśubhirannādyena tejasā brahmavarcaseneti.*

**Перевод:**

**When apāna is satisfied, the mind is satisfied. When the mind is satisfied parjanya is satisfied. When parjanya is satisfied lightning is satisfied. When lightning is satisfied everything controlled by lightning and parjanya is satisfied. After satisfying all these, the sacrificer become satisfied with offspring, animals, abundant food, and power arising from studying the Vedas.**

### 23

### 5.23.1

*atha yāṁ pañcamiṁ juhuyāt tāṁ juhuyāt udānāya svāheti. udānastṛpyati.*

**Перевод:**

**When he offers the fifth oblation he should offer saying, udānāya svāhā. Udāna becomes satisfied.**

### 5.23.2

*udāne tṛpyati tvak tṛpyati tvaci tṛpyantyāṁ vāyus tṛpyati. vāyau tṛpyati ākāśas tṛpyati. ākāśo tṛpyati yatkiñca vāyuś cākāśaś cādh- tiñṭhataḥ tat tṛpyati. tasyānu tṛptiṁ tṛpyati*  
*prajayā paśubhirannādyena tejasā brahmavarcaseneti.*

**Перевод:**

**When udāna is satisfied, skin is satisfied. When skin is satisfied, air is satisfied. When air is satisfied, ākāśa is satisfied. When ākāśa is satisfied everything controlled by air and ākāśa is satisfied. After satisfying all these, the sacrificer become satisfied with offspring, animals, abundant food, power arising from studying the Vedas.**

**Комментарий:**

Tejasā brahmavarcasena means with power arising from studying the Vedas.

### 24

### 5.24.1

*sa ya idam avidvān agnihotraṁ juhoti, yathā 'ṅgārān apohya bhasmani*  
*juhuyāt tādṛk tat syāt.*

**Перевод:**

**He who performs this sacrifice without knowing is like removing the embers and offering oblations to ashes.**

**Комментарий:**

He who performs the oblations to the prāṇas without knowing the accessories like the chest being the fire pit etc. is like a person offering sacrifice to ashes. It is useless.

### 5.24.2

*atha ya etad evaṁ vidvān agnihotraṁ juhoti, tasya sarveñu lokeñu bhūteñu sarveśvātmasu hutaṁ bhavati.*

**Перевод:**

**He who performs this sacrifice with knowledge, offers oblations to all worlds, all beings and all ātmās.**

**Комментарий:**

By worship of the Lord who is in everything, one performs worship of everything.

### 5.24.3

*tat yatheñīka (kā) tūlam agnau protaṁ pradūyeta, evaṁ hāsya sarve*  
*pāpmānaḥ pradūyante, ya etadevaṁ vidvānagnihotraṁ juhoti.*

**Перевод:**

**For the person who performs this sacrifice in knowledge all sins are burned up just as muñja grass fibers thrown into fire burns up.**

**Комментарий:**

Burning up sins is a result of worship of Brahman (the Lord). Here oblations using prāṇas, which is aṅga of that worship is glorified by stating the results of the aṅgī. Offering oblations of prāṇas is understood to be an aṅga of Vaiśvānara-vidyā.

### 5.24.4

*Tasmād u haivaṁvit yady api caṇḍālāyocchiñṭaṁ prayacchet, ātmani*  
*haivāsya tad vaiśvānare hutaṁ syād iti. tadeña ślokaḥ.-yatheha kñudhitā bālā mātaraṁ paryupāsate evaṁ sarvāṇi bhūtāny agni-hotram upāsata iti agnihotramupāsata iti.*

**Перевод:**

**If a person with this knowledge gives food remnants to a caṇḍāla, it is still an offering to Vaiśvānara ātmā. Just as hungry children wait on their mother, all beings wait on this sacrifice (to the prāṇas).**

**Комментарий:**

Vaiśvānara vidyā is praised. Giving food remnants to an unqualified person such as a caṇḍāla because it is undertaken while thinking of it as an offering to Vaiśvānara ātmā is not adharma. There is a verse concerning offering oblations to the prāṇas.

As hunger children gather around the mother to ask her for food, all beings gather around person who makes oblations to prāṇa iti chāndogyopaniñadi pañcamo 'dhyāyaḥ (pañcamaḥ praṇaṭhakaḥ)

## Part 6

## 1 - Sad-vidyā

### 6.1.1

*Śvetaketur hāruṇeya āsa. taṁ ha pitovāca, "śvetaketo vasa brahmacaryam.*  
*na vai somyāsmat kulīno 'nanūcya brahma-bandhur iva bhavati" iti.*

**Перевод:**

**Śvetaketu was the grandson of Aruṇa. His father Uddālaka said, “O Śvetaketu! Live as a brahmacārī.”**

**Комментарий:**

A story is told to teach sad-vidyā. Ha indicates that he was well known. Śvetaketu was the son of Uddālaka, who was the son of Aruṇa. Śvetaketu had not taken upanayana though he was twelve years old and thus had not studied the Vedas. O boy qualified for soma, or very handsome (somya)! O Śvetaketu! Live as a brahmacārī in order to study. No one in our family has not studied the Vedas or acted like a brahma-bandhu. One who is not a brāhmaṇa but mentions brāhmaṇas as him family members is called brahma-bandhu. Though he should have received his upanayana at eight years and was qualified, that time had passed. This was because his father had gone away on a long journey.

### 6.1.2

*sa ha dvādaśavarñaḥ upetya caturviṁśativarñaḥ sarvān vedānamadhītya*  
*mahāmanā anūcānamānī stabdha eyāya. taṁ ha pitovāca. -*

**Перевод:**

**Going to the gurukula when twelve years gold, he at twenty-four had studied all the Vedas. He was proud, thinking he had studied everything. Thinking himself perfect, he returned home. His father spoke to him.**

### 6.1.3

*śvetaketo ! yan nu somyedaṁ mahāmanā anūcānamānī stabdho 'si,*  
*uta tam ādeśam aprākñyo yenāśrutaṁ śrutaṁ bhavati amataṁ*  
*matamavijñātaṁ vijñātamiti.*  
*kathaṁ nu bhagavaḥ sa ādeño bhavatīti.*

**Перевод:**

**O Śvetaketu! Since you are proud, and think you know everything and are perfect, did you inquire about the principle by which everything unheard becomes heard, by which everything unconceived becomes understood, by which everything unknown becomes known.**

**Sir, how can such a principle (ruler) exist?**

**Комментарий:**

O handsome Śvetaketu! Since (yat) you are proud, and think you know everything and are perfect, did you inquire about the principle (ādeśam)? Knowing his son was devoid of Brahma-jñāna, the father informed the son that no one is perfect unless he has knowledge of Brahman- who is the nimitta and upādana of the universe. He asked him this question ouf of compassion to produce in him inquiry about Brahman, after destroying his pretense of being perfect.

Ādeña means the ruler according to Vyāsārya. One cannot understand the teachings unless the guru is satisfied with the student’s surrender.

*tad viddhi praṇipātena paripraśnena sevayā \|*  
*upadekñyanti te jñānaṁ jñāninas tattva-darśinaḥ \|\|*

**Know the Lord by surrender, questioning and service. The seers of truth, the jñānīs, will teach you this knowledge. BG** [**4.34**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/4.34)

How is it possible to know something by knowing another thing? How can knowing one thing produce knowledge of everything? Thinking in this way the son responded. How can that ruler possibly exist?

### 6.1.4

*yathā somyaikena mṛtpiṇḍena sarvaṁ mṛnmayaṁ vijñātaṁ syāt;*  
*vācārambhaṇaṁ vikāro nāmadheyaṁ mṛttiketyeva satyam*

**Перевод:**

**Handsome boy! All clay objects become known by knowing a lump of clay. The transformations assume names for purposes of speech. They are actually clay.**

**Комментарий:**

It is possible for an object to be the upādana (material cause) and the upādeya (its result).

By knowing a lump of earth, upādāna, pots and dishes made of clay (upādeya) are known.

The pot (asat) arise from the lump of clay. One may object that in knowing a lump of clay all clay objects are not known, since there is difference between the upādana and the object produced. To answer this it is said that the transformation of clay into the form of a pot are given (ārambhaṇam) names (nāmadheyam) for purposes of speech (vacā). The lump of clay takes on names and forms for indicating a change of state. To indicate the nondifference of the cause and effect a proof is given: the pots are made of clay (mṛtikā ity eva satyam). They are not different from the clay. Or it is true that things made of clay are also clay. Since the cause and the effect are nondifferent, in knowing the cause, the effect is known. Thus the transformation which assumes various names is real. It is not just a name. It is real clay. To say that only the clay is real and not the transformations is rejected.

### 6.1.5

*yathā somyaikena lohamaṇinā sarvaṁ lohamayaṁ vijñātaṁ syāt;*  
*vācārambhaṇaṁ vikāro nāmadheyaṁ loham ityeva satyam*

**Перевод:**

**Handsome boy! By knowing a nugget of gold all objects made of gold are known. Its transformation is given a name for purposes of speech. It is gold.**

### 6.1.6

*yathā somyaikena nakha-nikṛntanena sarvaṁ kārñṇāyasaṁ vijñātaṁ*  
*syāt; vācārambhaṇaṁ vikāro nāmadheyaṁ kṛñṇāyasamityeva satyam-*  
*evaṁ somya sa ādeśo bhavatīti*

**Перевод:**

**Handsome boy! By knowing a nail clipper all iron objects are known. Its transformation is given a name for purposes of speech. It is iron. “Boy! The ruler is of this nature.**

**Комментарий:**

Nail clipper stands for a lump of iron.

### 6.1.7

*na vai nūnaṁ bhagavantas ta etad avediñuḥ; yad hy etad avediñyan,*  
*kathaṁ me nāvakñyan' iti. "bhagavāṅs tv eva me tad bravītu " iti.*  
*"tathā somya" iti hovāca.*

**Перевод:**

**“My respected teachers did not know this. If they had known they would have told me. Dear father! Please teach me.”**

**His father said, “I agree, dear boy!”**

**Комментарий:**

My respected teacher did not know this. If they had known they would have taught it to me. Since they did not teach me I assume they did not know. He should not have insinuated this about a guru, but said this out of fear of again being sent to the gurukula. Thus he prayed to his father to teach him.

### 2

### 6.2.1

*sad eva somyedam agra āsīd ekam evādvitīyam.*  
*tadhaika āhuḥ, 'asad evedam agra āsīd ekam evādvitīyam. tasmād asatas saj jāyat \[iti\]*

**Перевод:**

**Handsome boy! Previous to creation of the universe only Brahman (sat) existed. It is one cause, and has no ruler.**

**Some say that before creation only asat existed. It alone existed, without a second. From that, asat arose the sat.**

**Комментарий:**

This section begins in order to explain that Brahman is the sole cause of the universe, in order to understand how knowing one entity produces knowledge of everything. In order to explain that clay is the cause of clay pots in the afternoon, one explains that the pot existed previously in the morning as a lump of clay.

Previous to the universe--the state of plurality with divisions of name and form previous to (idam agre), exist only sat—a state of oneness without no differentiated names and forms, devoid of any other ruler (advītīyam). Sat means the supreme Lord, who has the quality of being related to names and causes all activity or inactivity. He has prakṛti, jīvas and time as his body, and is endowed with qualities useful for relation of the universe, since that is topic at hand. The word eva emphasizes the denial of the logicians’ claim that asat existed previous to creation. It denies their idea that the effect did not exist at that time.

The logicians (eke) say that the asat alone existed before the creation. This is asat-kārya-vāda.

Sat indicates that the Lord is the efficient cause. Advitīyam means there is no other efficient cause. Ekam indicates that he is the material cause. Ekam advitīyam indicates that he is both the efficient cause and the material cause.

The meaning is this: previous to the creation of the universe visible with names and forms and variety only Brahman (sat), which is one (ekam), without various names and forms and devoid of any other cause (advitīyam), existed. Just as the universe existing now is real, the Lord existed previously without divisions of name and form.

The logicians say that before the creation only asat, with no names and forms, existed and not sat. Only this and nothing else existed. There was no existence of anything to give shelter or there were no existence of any state at all (asat). Or asat denies the statement that only sat existed previously. Since nothing existed as a shelter (tasmāt), from the state of asat arose forms that we know to exist.

### 6.2.2

*"kutas tu khalu somyaivaṁ syāt" iti hovāca "katham asataḥ saj jāyeta"iti.*  
*Sat tv eva somyedam agra āsīd ekam evādvitīyam.*

**Перевод:**

**“Handsome boy! How could it be like that?” He said, “How could the asat give rise to the sat? Before creation only Brahman existed, the one cause, with no other cause.”**

**Комментарий:**

What is the proof that from the asat arose this world? It contradicts what was stated and has no proof. How can the nonexistent give rise to existence of things? Thus only sat gives rise to the universe.

### 6.2.3

*Tad aikñata, bahu syāṁ prajāyeyeti. tat tejo 'sṛjata. Tat teja aikñata, bahu*  
*syāṁ prajāyeyeti. Tad apo 'sṛjata. tasmāt yatra kvacana śocati svedate vā*  
*puruñaḥ, tejasa eva tad adhy āpo jāyante.*

**Перевод:**

**Sat deliberated, “May I become many. May I manifest in a remarkable way.” He created fire. Fire deliberated, “May I become many.” Fire created water. Thus, when a person laments or perspires, from the heat (fire) water appears.**

**Комментарий:**

Tat means sat or supreme Brahman, the Lord. May I become many in the form of the universe of unlimited variety, consisting of individual jīvas and matter! For that purpose, he decided, “I should produce remarkably in the form of totality made of elements like fire.” Then he created fire. Since fire and other elements have no consciousness, when it is said that fire deliberated it means that the Lord, dwelling in the elements, deliberated. Thus fire means the Lord.

### 6.2.4

*tā āpa aikñanta. bahvayaḥ syāma prajāyemahīti. tā annam asṛjanta.*  
*tasmāt yatra kvacana varñati, tad eva bhūyiñṭham annaṁ bhavati.*  
*adbhaya eva tad adhy annādyaṁ jāyate.*

**Перевод:**

**Water deliberated. “May I become many. May I manifest in various forms.” Water produced food (earth). Thus where it rains, there food appears in plenty. From water alone food appears.**

**Комментарий:**

Food means earth, since that is the conclusion of Vedānta-sūtras.

### 3

### 6.3.1

*teñāṁ khalv eñāṁ bhūtānāṁ trīṇy eva bījāni bhavantya(ntya) āṇḍajaṁ*  
*jīvajam udbhijjam iti.*

**Перевод:**

**There are three seeds of these beings: those born from eggs, those born from other living beings and those born from seeds.**

**Комментарий:**

All other beings arise from three seed--fire, water, earth. Those born from eggs are birds and snakes etc. Those born from other beings are those born from wombs such as humans. Those born from seeds are plants. This category includes beings born from perspiration. This is stated in Vedānta-sūtra [3.1.22](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vs/3.1.22). It is understood that they are different from the Lord.

### 6.3.2

*seyaṁ devataikñata, "hantāham imās tisro devatā anena jīvenātma-*  
*nā 'nupraviśya nāma-rūpe vyākaravāṇīti {2}tāsāṁ trivṛtam trivṛtam ekaikāṁ*  
*karavāṇi" iti.*

**Перевод:**

**The Lord deliberated. “Let me enter the elements as the jīva, who is my body and let me create names and forms. Let me make each element threefold.”**

**Комментарий:**

The supreme Lord (sa) who is sat deliberated. Entering fire, water and earth presided by their devatās, through the jīva, who is my body (ātmanā), let me make names and forms like devatās. Ātmā can mean body.

The elements could not combine for specialized creation. To enable this, the Lord made them threefold. The Lord deliberated, “Let me make each one threefold. ”

### 6.3.3

*seyaṁ devatemās tisro devatā anenaiva jīvenā ''tmanā 'nupraviśya*  
*nāmarūpe vyākarot tāsāṁ trivṛta tri-vṛtam ekaikāṁ karot.*

**Перевод:**

**The Lord entered the three elements by means of the jīva who is the body of the Lord, and made names and forms. He made each into three.**

**Комментарий:**

The order is reversed. Actually he first made each element into three and then made the names and forms.

### 6.3.4

*yathā tu (nu) khalu somyemās tisro devatās trivṛt trivṛd ekaikā bhavati,*  
*tan me vijānīhīti.*

**Перевод:**

**Handsome boy! Know from me how the three elements become threefold.**

### 4

### 6.4.1

*Yad agne rohitaṅ rūpam, tejasas tad-rūpam. Yac chuklam, tad apām. yat*  
*kṛñṇam, tad annasya. Apāgād agner agnitvam. vācārambhaṇaṁ vikāro*  
*nāma-dheyam; trīṇi rūpāṇīty eva satyam.*

**Перевод:**

**The nature of fire that is seen to be red is the form of tejas before fire is combined in three parts. The nature of fire that is seen to be white is the form of water before fire is combined in three parts. The nature of fire that is seen to be black is the form of food (earth) before fire is combined in three parts. From giving up that state of fire, the real nature of fire manifests. The transformation and the names are composed of fire, water and food, for accomplishing speech.**

**Комментарий:**

With the combination of the three which destroys their previous states (apagāt), the nature of fire (agneḥ agnitvam), another state, manifests. The transformation, the state of fire, and the name “fire” is touched by fire, water and food, for accomplishing the transaction involving speech. Real fire is the made of fire, water and food.

### 6.4.2

*Yad ādityasya rohitaṅ rūpam, tejasas tad rūpam. Yac chuklam, tad apām.*  
*yat kṛñṇam, tad annasya. Apāgād ādityād ādityatvam. vācārambhaṇaṁ*  
*vikāro nāma-dheyam; trīṇi rūpāṇīty eva satyam.*

**Перевод:**

**The red aspect of the sun belongs to tejas (fire). The white aspect of the sun belongs to water. The black aspect of the sun belongs to food. From giving up that state, the nature of the sun manifests. The transformation, the state of the sun, and the name “sun” is touched by fire, water and food, for accomplishing the transaction involving speech. Real sun is made of fire, water and food.**

### 6.4.3

*Yac candramaso rohitaṅ rūpam, tejasas tad rūpam. Yac chuklam, tad apām.*  
*yat kṛñṇam, tad annasya. Apāgāc candrāc candratvam. vācārambhaṇaṁ*  
*vikāro nāma-dheyam; trīṇi rūpāṇīty eva satyam.*

**Перевод:**

**The red aspect of the moon belongs to fire. The white aspect of the moon belongs to water. The black aspect of the moon belongs to food. From giving up that state, the nature of the moon manifests. The transformation, the state of the moon, and the name “moon” is touched by fire, water and food, for accomplishing the transaction involving speech. Real moon is made of fire, water and food.**

### 6.4.4

*Yad vidyuto rohitaṅ rūpam, tejasas tad rūpam. Yac chuklam, tad apām.*  
*yat kṛñṇam, tad annasya. Apāgād vidyuto vidyuttvam. vācārambhaṇaṁ*  
*vikāro nāma-dheyam; trīṇi rūpāṇīty eva satyam.*

**Перевод:**

**The red aspect of lightning belongs to fire. The white aspect of the lightning belongs to water. The black aspect of lightning sun belongs to food. From giving up that state, the nature of lightning manifests. The transformation, the state of lightning, and the name “lightning” is touched by fire, water and food, for accomplishing the transaction involving speech. Real lightning is made of fire, water and food.**

### 6.4.5

*etaddha sma vai tad vidvāṅsa āhuḥ pūrve mahāśālā mahā-śrotriyāḥ, "na*  
*no 'dya kaścanāśrutam amatam avijñātam udāhariñyati" iti hy ebhyo*  
*vidāñ cakruḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**Great householders who knew the Vedas, being in knowledge said, “No one today can declare anything not heard, conceived or understood beyond fire, water and earth.” They understood everything from these three elements.**

**Комментарий:**

Those who knew previously that the cause is not separated from the effect, great householders, who knew all the Vedas, said this. “No one can declare anything not heard, conceived or known by us that is beyond fire, water and food.” They know everything from knowing fire, water and food.

### 6.4.6

*Yad u rohitam ivābhūditi, tejasas tad rūpam iti tad vidāñ cakruḥ yad u*  
*śuklam vābhūd iti, apāṅ rūpam iti tad vidāñ cakruḥ. Yad u kṛñṇam ivābhūd iti, annasya rūpam iti tad vidāñ cakruḥ.{6}yad vavijñātam ivābhūd iti, etāsām eva devatānāṅ samāsa iti tad vidāñ cakruḥ*

**Перевод:**

**They knew that what appeared as red was fire. What appeared as white was water. What appeared as black was food. What was not known, they understood to be a combination of tejas, water and food.**

**Комментарий:**

Everything is included in fire, water and food. They knew that what appeared as red, white and black was fire, water and food. Whatever was unknown, perceived as yellow, crimson, grey, or golden was understood to be a combination of red, white and black of fire, water and food, not anything different.

### 6.4.7

*yathā tu(nu) khalu somyemās tisro devatāḥ puruñaṁ prāpya trivṛt*  
*trivṛd ekaikā bhavati, tan me vijānāhīti.*

**Перевод:**

**Handsome boy! Know from me how the three devatās, attaining the person, become threefold.**

**Комментарий:**

Since everything in the material world is made of fire, water and earth (food), that nondifference is praised in order to show that the body made of flesh, blood, marrow and bone and the senses, mind and prāṇas are all fire, water and earth.

Please hear about the transformation by which the three elements change into three aspects, after the three elements, which are to be enjoyed by the person, approached the person.

### 5

### 6.5.1

*Annam aśitaṁ tredhā vidhīyate. tasya yaḥ sthaviñṭho dhātus tat purīñaṁ*  
*bhavati; yo madhyamas tan māṅsam; yo 'ṇiñṭhas tan manaḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**Food which is eaten takes three forms. The gross part becomes stool. The middle portion becomes flesh. The subtle portion becomes mind.**

**Комментарий:**

The food which is eaten and digested by the fire in the stomach is divided into three—the gross portion, middle portion and subtle portion. The gross part becomes stool, the middle portion becomes flesh and the subtle portion becomes nourishment for the mind. Mind is actually not a transformation of food since it is the result of ahaṅkāra (in sattva).

### 6.5.2

*āpaḥ pītās tredhā vidhiyante. tāsāṁ yaḥ sthaviñṭho dhātustanmūtraṁ*  
*bhavati; yo madhyamas tal lohitam; yo 'ṇiñṭhaḥ sa prāṇaḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**Water which is drunk takes three forms. The gross part becomes urine. The middle portion becomes blood. The subtle portion becomes prāṇa.**

### 6.5.3

*tejo 'śitaṁ tredhā vidhīyate. tasya yaḥ sthaviñṭho dhātus tad asthi bhavati;*  
*yo madhyamaḥ sa majjā; yo 'ṇiñṭhaḥ sā vāk.*

**Перевод:**

**Fire (oil, ghee etc.) which is eaten takes three forms. The gross part becomes bone. The middle portion becomes marrow. The subtle portion becomes speech.**

**Комментарий:**

Tejas here means oil, ghee etc. The subtle portion of ghee and oil which is fattening, nourishes the voice.

### 6.5.4

*Anna-mayaṅ hi somya mana āpomayaḥ prāṇas tejomayī vāg iti. bhūya eva*  
*mā bhagavān vijñāpayatv iti. tathā somyeti hovāca.*

**Перевод:**

**Handsome boy! Mind is composed of food. Prāṇa is composed of water. Voice is composed of fire.**

**“Respected father! Please instruct me.” The father said, “Handsome boy! I will do so.”**

**Комментарий:**

Since mind, prāṇa and voice are nourished by the subtle portions of food, water and Fire, mind is said to be composed of food, prāṇa is said to be composed of water and voice is said to be composed of fire.

It is seen that mice, only by eating food develop voice and prāṇa. Fish by eating only water develop their minds and voices. Others just by eating oil and ghee develop mind and prāṇas.

How you can say that mind is food, prāṇa is water and voice is fire? Since all foods are combination of the three elements, there is no contradiction to the statement made that mind is food. Wanting further knowledge, Śvetaketu makes a further request. Since I cannot properly understand how fire is voice, water is prāṇa and food is mind, please give more examples.

### 6

### 6.6.1

*dadhnaḥ somya mathyamānasya yo 'ṇimā sa ūrdhvaḥ samudīñati tat*  
*sarpir bhavati.*

**Перевод:**

**The subtle portion that rises when yogurt is churned is ghee.**

### 6.6.2

*Evam eva khalu somyānn asyāśyamānasya yo 'ṇimā sa ūrdhvaḥ*  
*samudīñati tan mano bhavati.*

**Перевод:**

**Similarly, the subtle portion extracted from food that is eaten is what nourishes the mind.**

**Комментарий:**

The subtle portion extracted from food that is eaten is what nourishes the mind.

### 6.6.3

*apāṅ somya pīyamānānāṁ yo 'ṇimā sa ūrdhvaḥ samudīñati sa prāṇo*  
*bhavati.*

**Перевод:**

**Handsome boy! When water is drunk the subtle portion that rises nourishes prāṇa.**

### 6.6.4

*tejasaḥ somyāśyamānasya yo 'nimā sa ūrdhvaḥ samudīñati sā vāg bhavati.*

**Перевод:**

**Handsome boy! When oil or ghee is consumed the subtle portion that rises nourishes the voice.**

### 6.6.5

*annamayaṅ hi somya mana āpomayaḥ prāṇastejomayī vāgiti.*  
*bhūya eva mā bhagavān vijñāpayatviti. tathā somyeti hovāca.*

**Перевод:**

**Handsome one! The mind is supported by food. Prāṇa is supported by water. Voice is supported by fire. “Dear father! Please instruct me further.” The father said, “I will do so, handsome one!”**

### 7

### 6.7.1

*ñoḍaśaklaḥ somya puruñaḥ. pañcādaśāhāni mā 'śīḥ; kāmam apaḥ piba;*  
*āpomayaḥ prāṇo na pibato vicchetsyata iti.*

**Перевод:**

**The person endowed with a mind has sixteen parts. For fifteen days do not eat. Drink water as much as you like. Prāṇa is supported by water. Prāṇa will not depart for the person who drinks water.**

**Комментарий:**

A person has a mind endowed with sixteen parts. The sixteen parts become nourished by food which is eaten every day. Do not eat for fifteen days. Drink water as you please. By drinking water the prāṇa will not stop since prāṇa is nourished by water. The teacher spoke this.

### 6.7.2

*sa ha pañcadaśāhāni nā ''śa. atha hainam upasasāda, "kiṁ bravīmi bho"*  
*iti. ṛcaḥ ?? somya yajūṅñi sāmānīti. sa hovāca, "na vai mā prati bhānti bho"*  
*iti.*

**Перевод:**

**He did not eat for fifteen days. He then approached his father. “What shall I recite?” The father said, “Dear son! Recite the Ṛg, Yajur and Sāma Vedas.” The son said, “They do not manifest to me.”**

### 6.7.3

*taṅ hovāca yathā somya mahato 'bhyāhitasyaiko 'ṅgāraḥ khadyota-*  
*mātraḥ pariśiñṭaḥ syāt; tena tato 'pi na bahu dahet - evaṁ somya te*  
*ñoḍaśānāṁ kalānām ekā kalā 'tiśiñṭā syāt; tayaitarhi vedān nānu-*  
*bhavasi. aśān. atha me vijñāsyasīti.*

**Перевод:**

**The father said to his son, “Handsome one! When one ember of a fueled fire remains like a firefly, it cannot burn much. Similarly, handsome one, when one part of sixteen parts remains, you will not know the Vedas. Please eat. Then you will understand.”**

**Комментарий:**

By fasting from food for fifteen days, fifteen portions were destroyed. One portion remained. Because of the small portion, you cannot remember the Vedas. Eat and you will understand what I had said: mind is supported by food.

### 6.7.4

*sa hā ''śa. atha hainamupasasāda. taṁ ha yat kiñca papraccha, sarvaṁ*  
*ha pratipede.*  
*taṁ hovāca*

**Перевод:**

**The son ate food. He approached his father. He knew everything that his father asked. The father spoke to him.**

### 6.7.5

*yathā somya mahato 'bhyāhitasyaikamaṅgāraṁ khadyotamātraṁ*  
*pariśiñṭaṁ taṁ tṛṇair upasamādhāya pra\[ā\] jvālayet; tena tato 'pi bahu*  
*dahet –*  
*evaṁ somya te ñoḍaśānāṁ kalānāmekā kalā 'tiśiñṭā '-*  
*bhūta; sā 'nnenopasamāhitā prājvālīt, (prājvālī); tayaitarhi vedān*  
*anubhavasi. annamaṁ hi somya mana āpomayaḥ prāṇas tejomayī vāgiti.*  
*tad dhāsya vijajñāviti - vijajñāviti.*

**Перевод:**

**“Handsome boy! The remaining ember from a great kindled fire, small as a firefly, when kindled again by grass, blazes strongly. It will burn much more than previously. Handsome one! One part of the sixteen remained. Kindled by food it blazed brightly. By that you knew the Vedas. Handsome one! The mind is nourished by food. Prāṇa is nourished by water. Voice is nourished by fire” Śvetaketu understood.**

### 8

### 6.8.1

*uddālako hā ''ruṇiḥ śvetaketuṁ putram uvāca - svapnāntaṁ me somya*  
*vijānīhīti. Yatraitat puruñaḥ svapiti nāma, satā somya tadā sampanno*  
*bhavati. Svam apīto bhavati. Tasmād enaṁ svapitīty ācakñate, svaṁ*  
*hy apīto bhavati.*

**Перевод:**

**Uddālaka, son of Aruṇa, said to his son Śvetaketu, “Handsome one! Know from me about deep sleep. Handsome one! When a person sleeps, he meets with the Lord. He merges with the Lord. He is described as svapiti since he merges with the Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

In order to teach that Brahman alone is he by whom knowing everything is known, Uddālaka taught that Brahman was the ātmā of unconscious things like earth, fire and water. Now he begins to explain teach that the Lord is within all conscious beings as well.

Know from my words about deep sleep (svapnāntam). When a person sleeps, he meets (sampannaḥ) the Lord (satā). Previously the father said sad eva somya. This is the same sat, the Lord. The “merging” with the Lord is explained as the word svapiti (he sleeps) through the phrase svam apītaḥ: he merges with the Lord (svam).

Since the jīva is different from the Lord how can the word svam, which should relate to the jīva, apply here to the Lord? The jīva in his waking state is spread with awareness of external suffering, and has qualities of attachment and greed with identity as a devatā or human with names and forms. The Lord, having jīva as his body, is internally and externally devoid of contaminating identity as human or devatā with names and forms, and is devoid of attachment repulsion, greed and bewilderment. He resides within the jīva in this state. Just as the Lord is within earth, water fire, so the Lord is within the jīva. Thus the jīva is the effect and the Lord is the cause. Knowing that cause, the effect becomes known.

### 6.8.2

*sa yathā śakuniḥ sūtreṇa prabaddho diśaṁ diśaṁ patitvā 'nyatrā ''-*  
*yatanamalabdhvā bandhanam evopaśrayate, evameva khalu somya*  
*tan mano diśaṁ diśaṁ patitvā 'nyatrā ''yatanamalabdhvā*  
*prāṇam evopaśrayate. Prāṇa-bandhanaṁ hi somya mana iti.*

**Перевод:**

**Handsome one! As a bird tied by a thread, after flying everywhere, not having any other support, takes shelter of its place of bondage, the mind, flying everywhere, not having any other support, takes shelter of prāṇa. Handsome one! The mind is bound to prāṇa.**

**Комментарий:**

A bird tied by a thread to the hand of a hunter after flying everywhere, not finding any shelter, takes shelter of the hand of the hunter (bandhanam). At the time of sleep, the mind, which is nourished by food, flies everywhere. During waking and dreaming, the mind is attracted to various objects. Not finding any rest, in order to rest, it joins closely with mukhya-prāṇa. Since mind is linked to prāṇa, when prāṇa leaves, the mind etc. leave.

In the state of deep sleep, the senses such as voice and the mind, embracing the prāṇa, give up their activities. In that state only prāṇa functions.

### 6.8.3

*aśanā\[yā\]pipāse me somya vijānīhīti. Yatraitat puruño 'śiśiñati nāma,*  
*āpa eva tad aśitaṁ nayante. tat yathā go-nāyo 'śva-nāyaḥ puruña-nāya*  
*iti, evaṁ tad apa ācakñate 'śanāyeti. Tatraitac chuṅgam utpatitaṁ*  
*somya vijānīhi, nedam amūlaṁ bhaviñyatīti.*

**Перевод:**

**Understand from me hunger and thirst. When a person desires to eat, water takes away what was eaten to the body (for nourishment). As one speaks of the person who takes away the cow, horse or man, water is called the taker of food. Handsome one! Know that the body, like a sprout, arises from food. It is not without a root.**

**Комментарий:**

To conclude that the Lord is the cause of the jīva, it was explained that the jīva unites with the Lord during deep sleep. Related to that, the sense embrace prāṇa. Now it is explained that the Lord is the root of the universe by explaining the meaning of words like “taker of food.” When (yatra) it is said that (nāma) a person desires to eat (aśiśiñati), water takes the eaten food. Digesting the food, water takes it to the body in the form of rasa.

When the food is digested, again one desires to eat. As one says “taker of a cow” taker of a horse” or “taker of a man” one says taker of food. Āśanāya means “desiring to eat.”

Know that the body, like a sprout (śuṅgam) arises from food which takes the form of rasa, becoming liquid because of water. The body has a cause.

### 6.8.4

*tasya kva mūlaṁ syād anyatrānnāt.*  
*Evam eva khalu somyānnena śuṅgenāpo mūlam anviccha, adbhiḥ somya*  
*śuṅgena tejo mūlam anviccha, tejasā somya śuṅgena san mūlam anviccha.*  
*San mūlāḥ somyemāḥ sarvāḥ prajāḥ sadāyatanāḥ sat-pratiñṭhāḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**Other than food, what can be its cause? Handsome one! Seek out the cause, water, from the sprout, food. Seek out the cause, fire from the sprout, water. Seek out the cause, the Lord, from the sprout, fire. All these beings have the Lord as their cause. All beings take shelter of the Lord and are established in him.**

**Комментарий:**

The body must have a cause. Food must be its cause, since no other cause can be found. One infers food as the cause, with the body as the effect, being nourished by food. Similarly food is an effect. Water can be inferred as its cause. Similarly fire is water’s cause. Finally the Lord is cause of fire.

### 6.8.5

*atha yatraitatpuruñaḥ pipāsati nāma, teja eva tat pītaṁ nayate. tat yathā*  
*gonāyo 'śvanāyaḥ puruñanāya iti, evaṁ etat teja ācañṭa udanyeti.*  
*Tatraitad eva śuṅgam utpatitaṁ somya vijānīhi. nedamamūlaṁ*  
*bhaviñyatīti.*

**Перевод:**

**When a person desires to drink, fire takes away what was drunk to the body (for nourishment). As one speaks of the person who takes away the cow, horse or man, fire is called the taker of water (udanya). Handsome one! Know that the body, like a sprout, arises from water dried by fire. The body is not without a root.**

### 6.8.6

*tasya kva mūlaṁ syād anyatrādbhyaḥ. adbhiḥ somya śuṅgena tejo*  
*mūlam anviccha, tejasā somya śuṅgena san mūlam anviccha.*  
*sanmūlāḥ somyemāḥ sarvā prajāḥ sadāyatanāḥ satpratiñṭhāḥ.*  
*yathā tu khalu somyemāstisro devatāḥ puruñaṁ prāpya trivṛt trivṛdekaikā*  
*bhavati, tad uktaṁ purastād eva bhavati.*  
*asya somya puruñasya prayato vāg manasi saṁpadyate, manaḥ prāṇe,*  
*prāṇastejasi, tejaḥ parasyāṁ devatāyām.*  
*sa ya eño 'ṇimā.*

**Перевод:**

**Other than water, what can be its cause? Handsome one! Seek out the cause fire, from the sprout, water. Seek out the cause, the Lord from the sprout, fire. Seek out the cause, the Lord, from the sprout, fire. All these beings have the Lord as their cause. All beings take shelter of the Lord and are established in him.**

**Handsome one! The three devatās, entering the person, become divided into three. That was previously explained. When the person dies, voice enters mind. Mind enters prāṇa. Prāṇa enters fire. Fire enters the supreme Lord. The Lord is most subtle.**

**Комментарий:**

Having shown that in deep sleep the jīva merges with the Lord, now it is shown that at the time of death the jīva merges with the Lord.

The voice does not merge with the mind completely but embraces it. Similarly mind embraces prāṇa. Prāṇa attains the jīva and with the jīva attains all subtle elements of which fire is representative. After that the subtle elements along with the jīva merge in the Lord in order that they take rest from experiencing material happiness and distress. Aṇimā (subtle) means the Lord is beyond the scope of conception.

### 6.8.7

*Aitad ātmyam idaṁ sarvam. tat satyam. sa ātmā. tat tvam asi śvetaketo iti.*  
*bhūya eva mā bhagavān vijñāpayatv iti. tathā somyeti hovāca.*

**Перевод:**

**Everything has the Lord as its ātmā. This is a fact. The Lord is ātmā. Your inner ātmā is the Lord. Revered father! Instruct me again.” His father said, “Handsome one! I will do so.”**

**Комментарий:**

Everything has the Lord as its ātmā (tad-ātmyam). You also (tvam asi) have the Lord as your ātmā (tat). One should not object that this meaning is lakñaṇā of the word tat. But to give the meaning you (jīva) are the Lord is most despicable. Ultimately tvam can refer to the Lord. You are he who has everything as his ātmā (tad-ātmyam). There is no lakñaṇā in this case.

### 9

### 6.9.1

*yathā somya madhu madhukṛto nistiñṭhanti nānātyayānāṁ vṛkñāṇāṁ*  
*rasān samavahāram ekatāṁ rasaṁ gamayanti.*

**Перевод:**

**Handsome one! Bees produce honey. Gathering juices from trees from different directions, they make it one as honey.**

### 6.9.2

*te yathā tatra na vivekaṁ labhante, amuñyāhaṁ vṛkñasya raso 'smy amuñyāhaṁ vṛkñasya rasosmīti evam eva khalu somyemāḥ sarvāḥ prajāḥ sati saṁpadya na viduḥ, sati*  
*saṁpadyāmaha iti.*

**Перевод:**

**The essences do not distinguish and think “I am the essence of that tree. I am the essence of that tree.” Handsome one! Similarly all the living beings merging in the Lord are not aware “We have merged in the Lord.”**

### 6.9.3

*sa iha vyāghro vā siṁho vā vṛko vā varāho vā kīṭo vā pataṅgo vā daṁśo*  
*vā maśako vā yad yad bhavanti tadābhavanti.*

**Перевод:**

**Whether tiger, lion, wolf, boar, worm, fly, gnat or mosquito, whatever they were, they become that again.**

**Комментарий:**

Rising from deep sleep, living beings become (ābhavanti) what they were previous to sleep. Previous to sleep they identified themselves as tiger etc. after sleep, they again think themselves tiger etc. They awake with their previous impressions. The merging during deep sleep (being temporary) is not a contradiction to their state on waking.

### 6.9.4

*sa ya eño 'ṇimā. Aitad ātmyam idaṁ sarvam. tat satyam. sa ātmā. tat*  
*tvam asi śvetaketo iti.*  
*bhūya eva mā bhagavān vijñāpayatv iti. tathā somyeti hovāca.*

**Перевод:**

**He is most subtle. Everything has the Lord as his ātmā. That is a true fact. The Lord is the ātmā. You are that ātmā, Śvetaketu.” “Respected father! Please instruct me further. “Yes, Handsome one! I will do that.”**

**Комментарий:**

Being merged in the Lord during deep sleep, after waking up, the person should remember “I came from the Lord.” Does not a person remember on coming out of a house, “I came out of the house”? Thus Śvetaketu asks for further instruction.

### 10

### 6.10.1

*imāḥ somya nadyaḥ purastāt prācyaḥ syandante. paścāt pratīcyaḥ. tāḥ*  
*samudrāt samudram evāpiyanti. samudra eva bhavanti. tā yathā tatra na*  
*viduḥ, iyam aham asmi, iyam aham asmīti.-,*

**Перевод:**

**Handsome one! These eastern rivers flow to the east. The western rivers flow to the west. They come from the ocean and merge into the ocean. They become the ocean. They are not aware, “I am this river.” “I am that river.”**

### 6.10.2

*Evam eva khalu somyemāḥ sarvāḥ prajāḥ sata āgamya na viduḥ sata*  
*āgacchāmaha iti.*  
*sa iha vyāghro vā siṁho vā vṛko vā varāho vā kīṭo vā pataṅgo vā daṁśo*  
*vā maśako vā yad yad bhavanti tadābhavanti.*

**Перевод:**

**Handsome one! When all beings come from the Lord (after sleep), they are not aware “I came from the Lord.” Whether tiger, lion, wolf, boar, worm, fly, gnat or mosquito, whatever they were, they become that again.**

### 6.10.3

*sa ya eño 'ṇimā, aitadātmyamidaṁ sarvam. tat satyam. sa ātmā. tat*  
*tvamasi śvetaketo iti.*  
*bhūya eva mā bhagavān vijñāpayatviti. tathā somyeti hovāca.*

**Перевод:**

**“He is most subtle. Everything has the Lord as his ātmā. That is a true fact. The Lord is the ātmā. You are that ātmā, Śvetaketu.” “Respected father! Please instruct me further.” “Yes, Handsome one! I will do that.”**

**Комментарий:**

On rising from sleep one is aware of one’s identity. Thus it is concluded that he was not destroyed during sleep. After dying, one does not remember the previous life. When the jīva separates from the body, is the jīva destroyed? Thus Śvetaketu asks for further instruction.

### 11

### 6.11.1

*asya somya mahato vṛkñasya yo mūle 'bhyāhanyāt jīvan sravet; yo*  
*madhye 'bhyāhanyāt, jīvan sravet; yo 'gre 'bhyāhanyāt, jīvan sravet.*  
*sa eña jīvenā ''tmanā 'nuprabhūtaḥ pepīyamāno modamānas tiñṭhati.*

**Перевод:**

**Handsome one! If one strikes a large tree at the root, it lives, and sap flows. If one strikes the tree in the middle, it lives, and sap flows. If one strikes the tree at the top. It lives and sap flows. The tree, pervaded by a jīva, sucking up water and content, remains standing.**

**Комментарий:**

The jīva pervading the tree from root to top, causing sap to flow, sucks up water by the root and feels content.

### 6.11.2

*tasya yad ekāṁ śākhāṁ jīvo jahāti, atha sā śuñyati; dvitīyāṁ jahāti, atha*  
*sā śuñyati; tṛtīyāṁ jahāti atha sā śuñyati; sarvaṁ jahāti, sarva: śuñyati-,*  
*evam eva khalu somya viddhīti hovāca.*

**Перевод:**

**When the jīva gives up one branch, that branch dries up. When it gives up a second branch, it also dries up. When it gives up a third branch, that also dries up. When the jīva leaves all branches, they all dry up. Handsome one! Understand this in a similar manner.**

**Комментарий:**

If the jīva leaves part of the tree it dries up. If the jīva leaves the whole tree, the whole tree dries up. When each branch dries up, the jīva is not destroyed. The jīva has withdrawn from part of the tree. The jīva is present in other parts of the tree. The jīva is not destroyed. When the tree dies, the jīva is not destroyed. The jīva has left the tree. When humans die, the jīva has left the body. The body dies, not the jīva. Jīva’s destruction would entail no accountability for his previous acts and accepting responsibility for acts not done. Thus, though the jīva merges with the Lord during deep sleep and death, the jīva is not destroyed.

### 6.11.3

*jīvāpetaṁ vāva kiledaṁ mriyate; na jīvo mriyate iti.*  
*sa ya eño 'ṇimā, aitadātmyamidaṁ sarvam. tat satyam. sa ātmā. tat*  
*tvam asi śvetaketo iti.*  
*bhūya eva bhagavān vijñāpayatviti. tathā somyeti hovāca.*

**Перевод:**

**“The body dies when the jīva leaves. The jīva does not die.**

**He is most subtle. Everything has the Lord as his ātmā. That is a true fact. The Lord is the ātmā. You are that ātmā, Śvetaketu.” “Respected father! Please instruct me further.” “Yes, Handsome one! I will do that.”**

**Комментарий:**

The Lord (sat) is without form and touch but is the cause of many universes filled with variety of plants and animals. How is this possible?

### 12

### 6.12.1

*Nyagrodha-phalam ata āhareti. idaṁ bhagava iti. bhindhīti. bhinnaṁ*  
*bhagava iti. kimatra paśyasīti. aṇavya ivemā dhānā bhagava iti.*  
*Āsām aṅgaikāṁ bhindhīti. bhinnā bhagava iti. Kim atra paśyasīti. na*  
*kiṁcana bhagava iti.*

**Перевод:**

**Bring a fruit from the banyan tree.**

**This is the fruit, respected father.**

**Break it open.**

**I have broken it open, father.**

**What do you see?**

**Very small seeds.**

**Break one of the seeds.**

**I have broken it, father.**

**What do you see?**

**I see nothing.**

**Комментарий:**

Aṇavya iva means aṇūni eva (very small).

### 6.12.2

*taṁ hovāca - yaṁ vai somyaitam aṇimānaṁ na nibhālayase, etasya vai*  
*kila somyaiño 'ṇimna evaṁ mahān nyagrodhas tiñṭhati.*

**Перевод:**

**The father said to Śvetaketu, “Handsome one! Because of this subtle being that you do not see, this large banyan tree stands.”**

**Комментарий:**

Because of the Lord who is very subtle, this gross universe that you see arises.

### 6.12.3

*śraddhatsva somyeti. sa ya eño'ṇimā, aitad ātmyam idaṁ sarvam. tat*  
*satyam. sa ātmā. tat tvam asi śvetaketo iti.*  
*bhūya eva mā bhagavān vijñāpayatviti. tathā somyeti hovāca.*

**Перевод:**

**“Have faith in this, handsome one! He is most subtle. Everything has the Lord as his ātmā. That is a true fact. The Lord is the ātmā. You are that ātmā, Śvetaketu.” “Respected father! Please instruct me further.” “Yes, Handsome one! I will do that.”**

**Комментарий:**

How is the Lord the cause? I cannot perceive him. If I cannot see him, how can I believe that he is the cause of the universe?

### 13

### 6.13.1

*Lavaṇam etad udake 'vadhāyātha mā prātar upasīdathā iti. sa ha tathā*  
*cakāra. taṁ hovāca yad doñā lavaṇam udake 'vādhāḥ, aṅga ! tad āhareti.*  
*taddhāvamṛśya na viveda. yathā vilīnam evāṅga.*

**Перевод:**

**“Put this salt in water and come to me in the morning.”**

**Śvetaketu did that.**

**Uddālaka said to him, “Bring the salt that you put in the water in the evening.”**

**He searched for the salt but did not find it, since it had dissolved.**

**Комментарий:**

Just as the salt was dissolved in the water but could not be perceived by the eye or by touch, the Lord within the jīva cannot been seen or touched.

### 6.13.2

*athāsyā ''dāv ācāmeti; katham iti. lavaṇam iti. madhyād ācāmeti; katham iti.*  
*Lavaṇam iti. Antād ācāmeti; katham iti. Lavaṇam iti. abhiprāsyaitat atha*  
*mopasīdathā iti. taddha tathā cakāra. Tac chaśvat saṁvartate. taṁ hovāca-*  
*atra vāva kila sat somya na nibhālayase atraiva kileti.*

**Перевод:**

**Sip the water from the top. How is it?**

**It is salty.**

**Sip from the middle. How is it?**

**It is salty.**

**Sip from the bottom. How is it?**

**It is salty.**

**Throw it away and come to me.**

**The son did that.**

**“The salt is always in the water everywhere.”**

**The father said to his son, “Handsome one! You do not see the Lord here but he is here.”**

**Комментарий:**

The salt could be perceived by taste. The Lord like the salt, cannot be perceived by the eye or touch. Thus the Lord, the ātmā of the universe can be known through the teachings of the ācāryas and scriptures. The lord (sat) exists here but you cannot see him.

### 6.13.3

*sa ya eño 'ṇimā aitadātmyamidaṁ sarvam. tat satyam. sa ātmā. tat*  
*tvamasi. śvetaketo iti.*  
*bhūya eva mā bhagavān vijñāpayatviti. tathā somyeti hovāca.*

**Перевод:**

**“He is most subtle. Everything has the Lord as his ātmā. That is a true fact. The Lord is the ātmā. You are that ātmā, Śvetaketu.” “Respected father! Please instruct me further.” “Yes, Handsome one! I will do that.”**

**Комментарий:**

Śvetaketu requested him to show by example the method of understand the Lord who pervades everything.

### 14

### 6.14.1

*yathā somya puruñaṁ gandhārebhyo 'bhinaddhākñam ānīya taṁ tato 'ti-*  
*jane visṛjet. sa yathā tatra prāṅ vodaṅ vā 'dhārāṅ vā pratyaṅ vā pradhmāyīta*  
*abhinaddhākña ānīto bhinaddhākño visṛñṭaḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**Taking a blindfolded person from the country of Gandhāra, one leaves him in an isolated place. He shouts in the east, in the north, in the south and the west, “I have been blindfolded and brought here. I have been blindfolded and left here.”**

### 6.14.2

*tasya yathā 'bhinahanaṁ pramucya prabrūyādetāṁ deśaṁ gandhārā*  
*etāṁ diśaṁ vrajeti; sa grāmāt grāmaṁ pṛcchan paṇḍito medhāvī*  
*gandhārānevopasaṁpadyeta evam evehācāryavān puruño veda. tasya tāvad eva ciraṁ yavan na vimokñye atha saṁpatsya iti.*

**Перевод:**

**Someone takes off his blindfold and says, “Gandhāra is in this direction. Go in this direction.” The wise and clever man asks at various villages and arrives at Gandhāra.**

**Similarly a person who has an ācārya knows that the Lord is here. Only as long as the body remains does he not become free. Then he achieves the Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

Medhavī means he does not forget what is told to him. Paṇḍita means he is capable of deliberation and reasoning. By the instructions of the ācārya, the person meditates on the Lord within himself. There will be delay until he is free from his body afflicted by prārabdha-karmas. After that he achieves the Lord (sat).

### 6.14.3

*sa ya eño 'ṇimā, aitadātmyamidaṁ sarvam. tat satyam. sa ātmā. tat*  
*tvamasi śvetaketo iti.*  
*bhūya eva mā bhagavān vijñāpayatviti. tathā somyeti hovāca.*

**Перевод:**

**“He is most subtle. Everything has the Lord as his ātmā. That is a true fact. The Lord is the ātmā. You are that ātmā, Śvetaketu.” “Respected father! Please instruct me further.” “Yes, Handsome one! I will do that.”**

**Комментарий:**

One identifies oneself as man or devatā, related to respective bodies. Simply leaving the body would not qualify a person for meeting the Lord. Thus Śvetaketu inquires further.

### 15

### 6.15.1

*puruñaṁ somyopatāpinaṁ jñātayaḥ paryupāsate, jānāsi māṁ jānāsi*  
*māmiti. tasya yāvan na vāṅ manasi saṁpadyate manaḥ prāṇe*  
*prāṇas tejasi tejaḥ parasyāṁ devatāyām, tāvaj jānāti.*

**Перевод:**

**Relatives gather around a person in great affliction. They say, “Do you recognize me? Do you recognize me?” He is aware of them until his voice merges into mind, mind merges into prāṇa and prāṇa merges into fire and fire merges into the Lord.**

### 6.15.2

*atha yadā 'sya vāṅ manasi saṁpadyate manaḥ prāṇe prāṇastejasi tejaḥ*  
*parasyāṁ devatāyām, atha na jānāti.*

**Перевод:**

**When voice merges into mind, mind into prāṇa, prāṇa into fire and fire in the Lord, he no longer recognizes anyone.**

**Комментарий:**

After the person enters the Lord he no longer recognizes anyone. In the state of death when the jīva merges with the Lord, the identity with the body also ceases. There is no need to worry about an eternal connection with the body. This however does not refer to the absolute destruction of relation with the body.

### 6.15.3

*sa ya eño 'ṇimā, aitad ātmyam idaṁ sarvam. tat satyam. sa ātmā. tat*  
*tvamasi śvetaketo iti.*  
*bhūya eva mā bhagavān vijñāpayatviti. tathā somyeti hovāca.*

**Перевод:**

**“He is most subtle. Everything has the Lord as his ātmā. That is a true fact. The Lord is the ātmā. You are that ātmā, Śvetaketu.” “Respected father! Please instruct me further.” “Yes, Handsome one! I will do that.”**

**Комментарий:**

Obstacle is created by identity as devatā or human. Can mere understanding that the Lord is the ātmā within oneself destroy that obstacle? Śvetaketu thus requests further teachings.

### 16

### 6.16.1

*puruñaṁ somyota hasta-gṛhītam ānayanti, apahārñit steyam akārñīt*  
*paraśum asmai tapateti. sa yadi tasya kartā bhavati, tata*  
*evānṛtam ātmānaṁ kurute. so 'nṛtābhisaṁdho 'nṛtenātmānam antardhāya*  
*paraśuṁ taptaṁ prati-gṛhṇāti. sa dahyate. atha hanyate.*

**Перевод:**

**Handsome one! They bring a man after catching him with their hands. “He has stolen. He is a thief. Heat the axe for him. If he committed theft, he will be proved a liar. Covering himself by his lie, he grasps the heated axe, burns and is killed.**

**Комментарий:**

The thief is tested. If he holds the heated and his hand burns, he is guilty. He is then killed.

### 6.16.2

*atha yadi tasyākartā bhavati. tata eva satyam ātmānaṁ kurute. sa*  
*satyābhisandhaḥ satyenātmānam antardhāya paraśuṁ taptaṁ*  
*pratigṛhṇāti, sa na dahyate; atha mucyate.*

**Перевод:**

**If he has not committed the crime, he makes himself truthful. Covering himself with truth, when he grasps the heated axe, he does not burn. He is then freed.**

### 6.16.3

*sa yathā tatra na dāhyeta aitadātmyamidaṁ sarvam. tat satyam. sa*  
*ātmā. tat tvamasi śvetaketo iti. taddhāsya vijajñāviti vijajñāviti.*

**Перевод:**

**“Similarly the person who knows the Lord within himself is not burned by saṁsāra.**

**Everything has the Lord as his ātmā. That is a true fact. The Lord is the ātmā. You are that ātmā, Śvetaketu.” Śvetaketu understood and undertook meditation.**

**Комментарий:**

You also have the Lord as your very self. Meditating on the Lord dwelling within yourself, you free yourself from saṁsāra.

## Part 7

### 1

### 7.1.1

*adhīhi bhagava iti hopasasāda sanatkumāraṁ nārada. taṁ hovāca*  
*yad vettha, tena mopasīda. tatasta ūrdhvaṁ vakśyāmīti.*

**Перевод:**

**Nārada approached Sanatkumāra and said, “Please teach me, respected one!” Sanatkumāra said, “Tell me what you know by your words. I will teach what is beyond that.”**

### 7.1.2

*sa hovāca – ṛg-vedaṁ bhagava 'dhyemi yajur-vedaṁ sāma-vedam ātharvaṇaṁ caturtham itihāsas-purāṇaṁ pañcamaṁ vedānāṁ vedaṁ pitryaṁ rāśiṁ daivaṁ nidhi vākovākyam ekāyanam, deva-vidyāṁ brahma-vidyāṁ bhūta-vidyāṁ kñatra-vidyāṁ nakñatra-vidyāṁ*  
*sarpa-deva-jana-vidyām. Etad bhagavo 'dhyemi.*

**Перевод:**

**Nārada said, “I studied Ṛg Veda, Yajur Veda, Sāma Veda, Atharvana—the fourth Veda, itihāsas-purāṇa or Mahābhārata—the fifth, grammar, śraddhā-kalpa, mathematics, omens, mineralogy, argumentation, the Ekāyana branch of the Vedas, worship of devatās, vedāṅgas like śikñā, knowledge of controlling others, Dhanur Veda, astronomy, antidotes for poison, music and Ayurveda.**

### 7.1.3

*so 'haṁ bhagavo mantra-vid evāsmi nātmavit. śrutaṁ hy eva me*  
*bhagavad-dṛśobhyaḥ, tarati śokam ātmavid iti. so 'haṁ bhagavaḥ śocāmi;*  
*taṁ mā bhagavāñchokasya pāraṁ tārayatv iti.*  
*taṁ hovāca. Yad vai kiṁcaitad adhyagīñṭhāḥ, nāmaivaitat*

**Перевод:**

**I know mantras sir, but I do not know about ātmā. I have heard from persons like you that the knower of ātmā surpasses sorrow. Respected sage! I am in lamentation. You should deliver me from lamentation.**

**Sanat-kumāra said, “What you have studied is only names.”**

**Комментарий:**

Knowing all this, I am fixed in the scriptures or fixed in the karma, predominated by mantras. But I do not know about Parabrahman, the Lord. I have heard from persons like you that the knower of the Lord surpasses saṁsāra (śokam). I have fallen in the ocean of suffering because I lack knowledge of the Lord. Deliver me from the ocean of suffering by knowledge of ātmā.

### 7.1.4

*nāma vā ṛgvedo yajurvedaḥ sāmaveda ātharvaṇaścaturtha ityihāsa -*  
*purāṇaḥ pañcamo vedānāṁ vedaḥ pitryo rāśirdaivo nidhirvākovākyame-*  
*kāyanaṁ devavidyā brahmavidyā bhūtavidyā kñatravidyā nakñatravidyā*  
*sarpadeva- janavidyā. nāmaivaitat. nāmopāssveti.*

**Перевод:**

**Ṛg Veda is only names. Yajur Veda, Sāma Veda, Atharvana—the fourth Veda, itihāsas-purāṇa or Mahābhārata—the fifth, grammar, śraddhā-kalpa, mathematics, omens, Mineralogy, argumentation, the Ekāyana branch of the Vedas, worship of devatās, vedāṅgas like śikñā, knowledge of controlling others, Dhanur Veda, astronomy, antidotes for poison, music and Ayurveda are only names. Meditate upon the name as Brahman.**

**Комментарий:**

The whole universe is made of names and forms. The Vedas etc. are also words, which speak of names and forms. Thus everything is names.

### 7.1.5

*sa yo nāma brahmetyupāste, yāvan nāmno gataṁ tatrāsya yathākāma-*  
*cāro bhavati, yo nāma brahmety upāste.*  
*asti bhagavo nāmno bhūya iti. nāmno vāva bhūyo 'stīti. tanme bhagavān*  
*bravītviti.*

**Перевод:**

**He who meditates on names as Brahman will wander as he pleases wherever there are names.**

**Revered sir! Is there anything greater than the name?**

**Yes there is something greater than the name.**

**Please tell me sir!**

### 2

### 7.2.1

*vāg vāva nāmno bhūyasī. vāg vā ṛg-vedaṁ vijñāpayati Yajur-vedaṁ*  
*sāma-vedam ātharvaṇaṁ caturtham itihāsa-purāṇaṁ pañcamaṁ devānāṁ*  
*vedaṁ pitryaṁ rāśiṁ daivaṁ nidhiṁ vākovākyam ekāyanaṁ deva-vidyāṁ*  
*brahma-vidyāṁ bhūtavidyāṁ kñatra-vidyāṁ nakñatra-vidyāṁ*  
*sarpa-deva-janavidyām, divañca pṛthivīñca vāyuñcākāśañ cāpaś ca*  
*tejaś ca devāṁś ca manuñyāṁś ca paśūṁś ca vayāṁsi ca tṛṇa-vanaspatīñchavāpadānyākīṭa-pataṅga-pipīlikaṁ dharmañ cādharmañ ca satyañcānṛtañ ca sādhu cāsādhu ca hṛdaya-jñañcāhṛdaya-jñañca.*  
*Yad vai vāṅ nābhaviñyat - na dharmo nādharmo vyajñāpayiñyat; na satyam,*  
*nānṛtam, na sādhu, nāsādhu, na hṛdayajño, nāhṛdayajño vāg evaitat*  
*sarvaṁ vijñāpayati. Vācam upāssveti.*

**Перевод:**

**Speech is greater than the name. Speech alone makes known the Ṛg Veda, Yajur Veda, Sāma Veda, Atharvana—the fourth Veda, itihāsas-purāṇa or Mahābhārata—the fifth, grammar, śraddhā-kalpa, mathematics, omens, mineralogy, argumentation, the Ekāyana branch of the Vedas, worship of devatās, vedāṅgas like śikñā, knowledge of controlling others, Dhanur Veda, astronomy, antidotes for poison, music and Ayurveda, heaven, earth, air, ether, water, fire, devatās, humans, animals, birds, animals down to worms, flies, ants, dharma, adharma, truth, lies, good and bad, attractive and unattractive.**

**If speech did not exist, dharma and adharma, would not be known. There would be no truth no lies, no good, no bad, no attractive and no unattractive. Speech alone makes everything known. Worship speech as Brahman.**

### 7.2.2

*sa yo vācaṁ brahmetyupāste, yāvad vāco gatam, tatrāsya yathākāmacāro*  
*bhavati yo vāca brahmetyupāste. asti bhagavo vāco bhūya iti. vāco vāva*  
*bhūyo 'stīti. tanme bhagavān bravītviti.*

**Перевод:**

**He who meditates on speech as Brahman wanders as he pleases wherever there is speech.**

**Is there anything greater than speech?**

**There is something greater than speech.**

**Revered sir! Please tell me.**

### 3

### 7.3.1

*mano vāva vāco bhūyaḥ. yathā vai dve vā ''malake dve vā kole dvau*  
*vā 'kñau muñṭir anubhavati, evaṁ vācañca nāma ca mano 'nubhavati. sa*  
*yadā manasā manasyati - mantrānadhīyeyeti, athādhīte; karmāṇi*  
*kurvīyeti, atha kurute; putrāṁśca paśūṁśceyeti, athecchate; imañ ca*  
*lokam amuñ ceccheyeti, athecchate. mano hyātmā. mano hi loko mano*  
*hi brahma. mana upāssveti.*

**Перевод:**

**Mind is greater than speech. Like two amalaki, kola or akña fruits within the hand, speech and name are held within the mind. When person thinks of studying mantras, he studies them. When he thinks of performing karmas, he performs them. When he thinks of having sons or animals, he desires to have them. When he thinks of desires for this world and the next, he desires. The mind is ātmā. The mind is the world. The mind is Brahman. Meditate on mind.**

**Комментарий:**

Since speech and other actions are dependent on mental activity, mind is superior. Ability to act (in this world), which are attributes of the ātmā, depend on the mind. Thus the mind is said to be ātmā. Since the world depends on actions of the mind, the mind is said to be the world. Because the mind is greater (bṛhat) than speech and name it is said to be Brahman.

### 7.3.2

*sa yo mano brahmety upāste, yāvan manaso gatam, tatrāsya yathā-*  
*kāmacāro bhavati, yo mano brahmety upāste.*  
*asti bhagavo manaso bhūya iti. manaso vāva bhūyo 'stīti. Tan me*  
*bhagavān bravītv iti.*

**Перевод:**

**He who meditates on the mind as Brahman, becomes free to wander wherever the mind goes.**

**Sir, is there something greater than mind?**

**Yes there is something greater than mind.**

**Please tell me that, sir.**

### 4

### 7.4.1

*saṁkalpo vāva manaso bhūyān. yadā vai saṅkalpayate, atha mana-*  
*syati, atha vācam īrayati, tāmu nāmnīrayati. nāmni mantrā ekaṁ*  
*bhavanti. mantreñu karmāṇi.*

**Перевод:**

**Will is greater than mind. When a person wills, he then thinks, and then activates his voice and then uses names. The mantras are one with the name. The karmas are one with the mantras.**

**Комментарий:**

When a person wills, “This action is suitable” then he thinks “this action should be done.” He then stimulates his voice. The then engages his voice in pronouncing words.

### 7.4.2

*tāni ha vā etāni saṅkalpaikāyanāni saṅkalpātmakāni saṅkalpe*  
*pratiñṭhitāni. samakḷpatāṁ dyāvāpṛthivī, samakalpetāṁ vāyuś cākā-*  
*śañ ca, samakalpantām āpaś ca tejaścā teñāṁ saṅkḷptyai varñaṁ saṅkalpate.*  
*varñasya saṅkḷptyā annaṁ saṅkalpate. annasya saṅkḷptyai prāṇāḥ*  
*saṅkalpante. prāṇānāṁ saṅkḷptyai mantrāḥ saṅkalpante. Mantrāṇāṁ*  
*saṅkḷptyai karmāṇi saṅkalpante. karmaṇāṁ saṅkḷptyai lokaḥ (kāḥ)*  
*saṅkalpate(nte). lokasya saṅkḷptyai sarvaṁ saṅkalpate. sa eña saṅkalpaḥ saṅkalpam upāssveti.*

**Перевод:**

**Names etc. follow will, are caused by will, and exist only because of will. Heaven and earth willed. Air, ether, water and fire willed. By their will, rain wills. By rain’s will, food wills. By food’s will, prāṇa wills. By prāṇa’s will, mantras will. By mantras’ will, karmas will. By karmas’ will, the world wills. By the world’s will, everything wills. This is the greatness of will. Worship will as Brahman.**

**Комментарий:**

The heavens and earth willed, “We will exist with firmness.” Air etc. willed, “We will exist in our particular forms. Let us perform our particular duties.” Without will, the actions do not arise. Rain is dependent on air, water etc. Rain willed that they function. Food is dependent on rain. Food willed that rain function. The prāṇas will nourishment of food. Since pronunciation of mantras is dependent on prāṇa, the mantras desired prāṇa to function. Karmas like fire sacrifices are dependent on memory generated from mantras. Going to Svarga is dependent on performance of karmas. All beings are dependent on various planets to live.

Here, consciousness for willing is projected on unconscious elements, in order to praise will. Or the willing is done by the devatās in charge of the various elements. Worship will as Brahman.

### 7.4.3

*sa yaḥ saṅkalpaṁ brahmetyupāste, saṁkḷptān vai sa lokān dhruvān*  
*dhruvaḥ, pratiñṭhitān pratiñthito 'vyathamānān avyathamāno 'bhisiddhayati.*  
*yāvat saṅkalpasya gatam, tatrāsya yathākāmacāro bhavati, yaḥ saṅkalpaṁ*  
*brahmetyupāste. asti bhagavaḥ saṅkalpād bhūya iti. saṅkalpādvāva bhūyo '-*  
*stīti. tanme bhagavān bravītviti.*

**Перевод:**

**He who meditates on will as Brahman, being immovable, steadfast and untroubled attains places which are attained through will, which are eternal, steadfast with enjoyable objects and untroubled by affliction of enemies, and is free to wander as he desires wherever his will extends.**

**Sir, is there something greater than will?**

**Yes, there is something greater than will.**

**Please tell me, sir.**

### 5

### 7.5.1

*cittaṁ vāva saṅkalpād bhūyaḥ. yadā vai cetayate, atha saṅkalpayate;*  
*atha manasyati; atha vācam īrayati; tām u nāmnīrayati, nāmni mantrā*  
*ekaṁ bhavanti, mantreñu karmāṇi.*

**Перевод:**

**Citta is greater than will. When he employs citta, he then uses will. Then he thinks. Then he invokes his voice. He engages his words in names. The mantras become one with the names. The karmas become one with the mantras.**

**Комментарий:**

Buddhi (mental apprehension), ahaṅkāra (identity) and citta (deliberation) are aspects of mind. When the mind considers at a particular time the usefulness of an object of the past or future, it is called citta.

### 7.5.2

*tāni ha vā etāni cittaikāyanāni cittātmāni (tmakāni) citte pratiñṭhitāni.*  
*tasmād yady api bahuvit acitto bhavati, nāyam astīty evainam āhuḥ,*  
*yad ayaṁ veda. Yad vā ayaṁ vidvān, nettham acittaḥ syād iti. atha*  
*yady alpa-viccittavān bhavati, tasmā evota śuśrūñante. cittaṁ*  
*hy evaiñām ekāyanam, cittam ātmā, cittaṁ pratiñṭhā. cittam upāssveti.*

**Перевод:**

**All these follow citta. They are caused by citta, and exist only because of citta. People thus say that if a person is knowledgeable but without citta, he is nothing and knows nothing. If he were in knowledge he would not be without citta. On the other hand, if a person has little knowledge of scripture but has citta, people desire to hear him. Citta is the goal of these. Citta is ātmā. Citta is the support. Meditate on citta.**

### 7.5.3

*sa yaś cittaṁ brahmetyupāste, cittān vai sa lokān dhruvān dhruvaḥ,*  
*pratiñṭhitān pratiñṭhito 'vyathamānān avyathamāno 'bhisiddhayati -*  
*yāvac cittasya gatam, tatrāsya yathākāmacāro bhavati, yaś cittaṁ*  
*brahmetyupāste.*  
*asti bhagavaś cittād bhūya iti. Cittād vāva bhūyo 'stīti. Tan me bhagavān*  
*bravītviti.*

**Перевод:**

**He who meditates on citta as Brahman, being eternal, steadfast and untroubled, attains places which are abundant, which are eternal, steadfast with enjoyable objects and untroubled by affliction of enemies, and is free to wander as he desires wherever his citta extends.**

**Sir, is there something greater than citta?**

**Yes, there is something greater than citta.**

**Please tell me, sir.**

### 6

### 7.6.1

*dhyānaṁ vāva cittāt bhūyaḥ. dhyāyatīva pṛthivī, dhyāyatīvāntarikñam,*  
*dhyāyatīva dhaur dhyāyantīvāpo dhyāyantīva parvatā dhyāyantīva deva-manuñyāḥ.*  
*tasmād ya iha manuñyāṇāṁ mahattāṁ prāpnuvanti, dhyānāpādāṁśā ivaiva te bhavanti. atha ye 'lpāḥ, kala-hinaḥ piśunā upavādinas te. atha ye prabhavo dhyānāpādāṁśā ivaiva te bhavanti. dhyānam upāssveti.*

**Перевод:**

**Meditation is greater than citta. Earth meditates. Antarikña meditates. Heaven meditates. Water meditates. Mountains meditate. Devatās and humans meditate. Those who attain greatness among men are similar to meditators. Those who are inferior in status are quarrelsome, slandering and abusive. Those who are great are like meditators. Meditate on meditation as Brahman.**

**Комментарий:**

Meditation is continuous thought of object without inference of another thought. Thus, yogīs become motionless by meditation. Objects like earth are said to meditate in order to broadcast the glories of meditation. They are without movement and thus are similar to persons meditating. Thus they become causes of glorifying meditation. One should meditate in a seated position. (Vedānta-sūtra [4.1.7](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vs/4.1.7)) Those who are great are like meditators in that they are silent. Those who are inferior, śūdras, are quarrelsome, fault-finding, (piśunāḥ) and speak of one’s faults to others (upavādinaḥ). They are not similar to meditators at all. Those who are great are like meditators, being silent.

### 7.6.2

*sa yo dhyānaṁ brahmetyupāste, yāvat dhyānasya gatam, tatrāsya*  
*yathākāmacāro bhavati, yo dhyānaṁ brahmetyupāste.*  
*asti bhagavo dhyānāt bhūya iti. dhyānādvāva bhūyo 'stīti. tanme*  
*bhagavān bravītviti.*

**Перевод:**

**He who meditates on meditation as Brahman wanders as he pleases, as far as meditation takes him.**

**Sir, is there something greater than meditation?**

**Yes, there is something greater than meditation.**

**Please tell me, sir.**

### 7

### 7.7.1

*vijñānaṁ vāva dhyānāt bhūyaḥ. vijñānena vā ṛg-vedaṁ vijānāti*  
*yajur-vedaṁ sāma-vedam ātharvaṇaṁ caturtham itihāsas-purāṇaṁ pañcamaṁ*  
*vedānāṁ vedaṁ pitryaṁ rāśiṁ daivaṁ nidhiṁ vākovākyam ekāyanaṁ*  
*deva-vidyāṁ brahma-vidyāṁ bhūta-vidyāṁ kñatra-vidyāṁ nakñatra-vidyāṁ*  
*sarpa-deva-jana-vidyām; divañ ca pṛthivīñ ca vāyuñ cākāśañ cāpaś ca tejaś ca*  
*devāṁś ca manuñyāṁś ca vayāṁsi ca tṛṇa-vanaspatīn śvāpadāni*  
*ākīṭa-pataṅga-pipīlikaṁ dharmañ cādharmañ ca satyañ cā- nṛtañ ca sādhu*  
*cāsādhu ca hṛdaya-jñañ cāhṛdaya-jñañcānnañ ca rasañ cemañ ca loka-*  
*m amuñ ca vijñānenaiva vijānāti. Vijñānam upāssveti.*

**Перевод:**

**Vijñānam is greater than meditation. By vijñāna one knows the Ṛg Veda, Yajur Veda, Sāma Veda, Atharvana—the fourth Veda, itihāsas-purāṇa or Mahābhārata—the fifth, grammar, śraddhā-kalpa, mathematics, omens, mineralogy, argumentation, the Ekāyana branch of the Vedas, worship of devatās, vedāṅgas like śikñā, knowledge of controlling others, Dhanur Veda, astronomy, antidotes for poison, music and Ayurveda, heaven, earth, air, ether, water, fire, devatās, humans, animals, birds, animals down to worms, flies, ants, dharma, adharma, truth, lies, good and bad, attractive and unattractive, food and rasa, this world and the next world. Meditate on vijñāna.**

**Комментарий:**

Vijñāna is knowledge produced by scriptures. It is the cause of continuous remembrance or meditation. Thus it is greater.

### 7.7.2

*sa yo vijñānaṁ brahmety upāste, vijñānavato vai sa lokān jñāna-*  
*vato 'bhisiddhati-yāvad vijñānasya gatam, tatrāsya yathākāma- cāro*  
*bhavati, yo vijñānaṁ brahmetyupāste.*  
*asti bhagavo vijñānād bhūya iti. vijñānādvāva bhūyo 'stīti. Tan me*  
*bhagavān bravītviti.*

**Перевод:**

**He who meditates on vijñāna as Brahman, being full of knowledge from scriptures, attains worlds full of knowledge and wanders as he pleases to the extent of his scriptural knowledge.**

**Sir, is there something greater than vijñāna?**

**Yes, there is something greater than vijñāna.**

**Please tell me, sir.**

**Комментарий:**

Jñāna indicates general knowledge and vijñāna indicates special knowledge. Thus having special knowledge he can attain all planets of knowledge.

### 8

### 7.8.1

*balaṁ vāva vijñānād bhūyaḥ. api ha śataṁ vijñānavatām eko*  
*balavān ākampayate. sa yadā balī bhavati, athotthātā bhavati, utti-*  
*ñaṭhan paricaritā bhavati; paricarann upasattā bhavati; upasīdan drañṭā*  
*bhavati śrotā bhavati mantā bhavati boddhā bhavati kartā bhavati*  
*vijñātā bhavati. balena vai pṛthivī tiñṭhati, balenāntarikñam, balena*  
*dyaur balena parvātā balena deva-manuñyā balena paśavaś ca vayāṁsi ca*  
*tṛṇa-vanaspatayaḥ śvāpadāny-ākīṭa-pataṅga-pipīlikaṁ balena lokas tiñṭhati.*  
*balam upāsveti.*

**Перевод:**

**Strength is greater than vijñāna. One strong person makes a hundred persons having vijñāna tremble. If he is strong, he stands before elders to greet them. Standing and respecting, he serves them. Serving them, he approaches them for knowledge. Approaching he sees. Seeing he hears, he reflects, he meditates, performs actions and realizes the scriptures.**

**The earth remains because of strength. Antarikña, heaven, mountains, devatās, humans, animals, birds, grass, trees, animals down to worms, flies and ants and the world remain because of strength. Worship strength as Brahman.**

### 7.8.2

*sa yo balaṁ brahmetyupāste, yāvad balasya gatam tatrāsya yathā-*  
*kāmacāro bhavati, yo balaṁ brahmatyupāste.*  
*asti bhagavo balād bhūya iti. balād vāva bhūyo 'stīti. tanme bhagavān*  
*bravītviti.*

**Перевод:**

**He who meditates on strength as Brahman wanders as he pleases to the extent of his strength.**

**Sir, is there something greater than meditation?**

**Yes, there is something greater than meditation.**

**Please tell me, sir.**

### 9

### 7.9.1

*annaṁ vāva balād bhūyaḥ. tasmād yady api daśa rātrīr nāśnīyāt, yady uha*  
*jīvet athavā, adrañṭā 'śrotā 'mantā 'bodvā 'kartā 'vijñātā bhavati.*  
*Athānnasyāye drañṭā bhavati, śrotā bhavati, mantā bhavati, boddhā*  
*bhavati, kartā bhavati, vijñātā bhavati. annamupāssveti.*

**Перевод:**

**Food is greater than strength. Therefore, if he does not eat for ten nights, and still lives, he cannot see, cannot hear, cannot contemplate, cannot meditate, cannot perform actions, and cannot realize anything. Obtaining food he can see, hear, contemplate, meditate, act and realize. Worship food as Brahman.**

**Комментарий:**

Strength depends on food. Thus food is superior. Annasya āye means “on obtaining food.”

### 7.9.2

*sa yo 'nnaṁ brahmetyupāste, annavato vai sa lokān pānavato 'mi-*  
*siddhayati, yāvadannasya gatam tatrāsya yathākāmacāro bhavati, yo 'nnaṁ*  
*brahmety upāste.*  
*asti bhagavo 'nnād bhūya iti. Annād vāva bhūyo 'stīti. Tan me bhagavān*  
*bravītv iti .*

**Перевод:**

**He who worships food as Brahman attains worlds full of food and drinks and wanders as he desires, wherever there is food.**

**Sir, is there something greater than food?**

**Yes, there is something greater than food.**

**Please tell me, sir.**

### 10

### 7.10.1

*āpo vā annād bhūyasyaḥ, tasmād yadā suvṛñṭir na bhavati, vyā-*  
*dhīyante prāṇāḥ, annaṁ kanīyo bhaviñyatīti. atha yadā suvṛñṭirbhavati,*  
*ānandinaḥ prāṇā bhavanti, annaṁ bahu bhaviñyatīti. āpa evemā mūrtāḥ*  
*yeyaṁ pṛthivī, yadantarikñam, yad dhyauḥ, yat parvatāḥ,*  
*yaddevamanuñyāḥ, yat paśavaśca vayāṁsi ca; tṛṇavanaspatayaḥ*  
*śvāpadāny-ākīṭa-pataṅga-pipīlikam āpa evemā mūrtāḥ. apa upāssveti.*

**Перевод:**

**Water is greater than food. Therefore, when there is lack of rain, the living beings fear that food will be scarce. When there is abundant rain, living beings become joyful, thinking there will be plentiful food. Water is all these forms—earth, antarikña, heaven, mountains, devatās, humans, animals, birds, grass, trees, animals down to worms, flies and ants. Worship water as Brahman.**

### 7.10.2

*sa yo 'po brahmetyupāste, āpnoti sarvān kāmāṁs tṛptimān bhavati,*  
*yāvadapāṁ gatam, tatrāsya yathākāmacāro bhavati, yo 'po*  
*brahmetyupāste.*

**Перевод:**

**He who meditates on water as Brahman obtains satisfaction of all desires and wanders as he desires wherever there is water.**

### 7.10.3

*asti bhagavo ' dbhayo bhūya iti. adbhayo vāva bhūyo 'stīti. tanme*  
*bhagavān bravītviti.*

**Перевод:**

**Sir, is there something greater than water?**

**Yes, there is something greater than water.**

**Please tell me, sir.**

### 11

### 7.11.1

*tejo vā adbhayo bhūyaḥ. tadvā etad vāyum āgṛhyākāśam abhitapati.*  
*tad āhuḥ - niśocati, nitapati, varñiñyati vā iti. teja eva tat pūrvaṁ*  
*darśayitvā 'thāpaḥ sṛjate. Tad etad ūrdhvābhiś ca tiraścībhiś ca vidyudbhir āhrādāś caranti. tasmād āhuḥ - vidyotate, stanayati, varñañyati vā iti.*  
*teja eva tat pūrvaṁ darśayitvā 'thāpaḥ sṛjate. tena upāssveti.*

**Перевод:**

**Fire is greater than water. When fire makes air motionless and heats the ether, they say, “It burns, it heats, it causes rain.” Showing itself first, fire then creates rain. Thunder sounds with lightning that moves upwards and sideways. They say, “Fire flashes, it thunders, it causes rain. Having shown itself first, it creates rain.” Therefore meditate on fire.**

### 7.11.2

*sa yas tejo brahmetyupāste; tejasvī tejasvī vai sa tejasvato lokān*  
*bhāsvato 'pahata-tamaskān abhisiddhati, yāvat tejaso gatam, tatrāsya*  
*yathākāmacāro bhavati, ya stejo brahmety upāste.*  
*asti bhagavas tejaso bhūya iti. tejaso vāva bhūyo 'stīti. Tan me bhagavān*  
*bravītviti.*

**Перевод:**

**He who meditates on fire as Brahman, becoming brilliant, attains brilliant, shining worlds free of darkness, and wanders as he desires, as far as fire extends.**

**Sir, is there something greater than fire?**

**Yes, there is something greater than fire.**

**Please tell me, sir.**

### 12

### 7.12.1

*ākāśo vāva tejaso bhūyān. ākāśe vai sūryācandramasā bubhau vidyun-nakñatrāṇy-agniḥ. ākāśenāhvayati, ākāśena śṛṇoti, ākāśena pratiśṛṇoti, ākāśe ramate, ākāśe na ramate, ākāśe jāyate, ākāśam abhijāyate. Ākāśam upāssveti.*

**Перевод:**

**Ether is greater than fire. The sun and moon exist within ether. Lightning, constellations and fire exist in ether. One calls out in ether. By ether, one hears and answers. One enjoys in ether, suffers in ether, and is born in ether. One is born, expecting space within ether. Worship ether as Brahman.**

**Комментарий:**

Sprouts are born (jāyate), expecting (abhi) some room to grow.

### 7.12.2

*sa ya ākāśaṁ brahmetyupāste, ākāśavato vai sa lokān prakāśa-*  
*vato 'saṁbādhān urugāyavato 'bhisiddhayati. yāvadākāśasya gatam*  
*tatrāsya yathākāmacāro bhavati, ya ākāśaṁ brahmetyupāste.*  
*asti bhagava ākāśād bhūya iti. ākāśādvāva bhūyo 'stīti. tanme bhagavānbravītviti.*

**Перевод:**

**He who meditates on ether as Brahman, attains effulgent, glorious worlds like the ether, which are spacious, without obstacles, and wanders as he desires, wherever there is ether.**

**Sir, is there something greater than ether?**

**Yes, there is something greater than ether.**

**Please tell me, sir.**

### 13

### 7.13.1

*smaro vā ākāśād bhūyaḥ (yān). tasmād yadyapi bahava āsīran asmarantaḥ,*  
*naiva te kañcana śṛṇuyuḥ, na manvīran, na vijānīran, yadā vāva te*  
*smareyuḥ, atha śṛṇuyuḥ atha manvīran, atha vijānīran. smareṇa vai*  
*putrān vijānāti; smareṇa paśūn. Smaram upāssveti.*

**Перевод:**

**Memory is greater than ether. Therefore if many people gather, but do not remember, they hear nothing, contemplate nothing, know nothing. If they remember, they hear, contemplate, and know. By memory, one recognizes one’s sons and animals. Worship memory as Brahman.**

### 7.13.2

*sa yaḥ smaraṁ brahmety upāste, yāvat smarasya gatam tatrāsya yathā-*  
*kāmacāro bhavati, yaḥ smaraṁ brahmety upāste.*  
*asti bhagavaḥ smarād bhūya iti. Smarād vāva bhūyo'stīti. Tan me*  
*bhagavān bravītviti.*

**Перевод:**
