**He who meditates on memory as Brahman wanders as he desires wherever there is memory.**

**Sir, is there something greater than memory?**

**Yes, there is something greater than memory.**

**Please tell me, sir.**

### 14

### 7.14.1

*āśā vāva smarād bhūyasī. Āśayed dho vai smaro mantrān adhīte, karmāṇi*  
*kurute, putrāṁś ca paśuṁś cecchate, imañca lokamamuñcecchate.*  
*Āśām upāssveti.*

**Перевод:**

**Aspiration is greater that memory. Motivated by aspiration, memory recites mantras, performs karmas, desires sons and animals, and desires this world and the next. Meditate on aspiration as Brahman.**

### 7.14.2

*sa ya āśāṁ brahmety upāste, āśayā 'sya sarve kāmāḥ samṛddhayanti;*  
*amoghā hy asyāśiño bhavanti, yāvad āśāyā gatam tatrāsya yathākāmacāro*  
*bhavati, ya āśāṁ brahmetyupāste.*  
*asti bhagava āśāyā bhūya iti. āśāyā vāva bhūyo 'stīti. Tan me bhagavān*  
*bravītv iti.*

**Перевод:**

**For he who meditates on aspiration as Brahman, all desirable objects increase in that worship of aspiration as Brahman. His aspirations will be fulfilled. He wanders everywhere by his desire, wherever there is aspiration.**

**Sir, is there something greater than aspiration?**

**Yes, there is something greater than aspiration.**

**Please tell me, sir.**

### 15

### 7.15.1

*prāṇo vāva āśāyā bhūyān. yathā vā arā nābhau samarpitāḥ, evam asmin*  
*prāṇe sarvaṁ samarpitam. prāṇaḥ prāṇena yāti. prāṇaḥ prāṇaṁ dadāti;*  
*prāṇāya dadāti. prāṇo ha pitā, prāṇo mātā, prāṇo bhrātā, prāṇaḥ svasā,*  
*prāṇa ācāryaḥ, prāṇo brāhmaṇaḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**The jīva is greater than aspiration. Just as spokes are fixed to the hub, everything is supported by the jīva. Bhūta-mātrā prajñā-mātrāsv arpitāḥ: all things are fixed in the jīvas. (Kausitaki Upaniñad). The jīva moves because of another jīva. A jīva gives another jīva. Jīva gives to another jīva. Father is jīva. Mother is a jiva. Brother is a jīva. Sister is a jīva. The guru is a jīva. The brāhmaṇa is a jīva.**

**Комментарий:**

Prāṇa here means the jīva who is accompanied by prāṇa. Just the spokes are all fixed to the center, everything is fixed to the jīva. What moves is a jīva. The jīva moves by a jīva such as a horse. The giver is a jīva. What is given, such a cow, is a jīva. Who accepts the gift is a jīva.

### 7.15.2

*sa yadi pitaraṁ vā mātaraṁ vā bhrātaraṁ vā svasāraṁ vācāryaṁ vā*  
*brāhmaṇaṁ vā kiñcit bhṛśam iva pratyāha, dhik tvā 'stvity evainam āhuḥ,*  
*pitṛhā vai tvam asi, mātṛhā vai tvam asi, bhrātṛhā vai tvam asi, svasṛhā vai*  
*tvam asi, ācāryahā vai tvam asi, brāhmaṇahā vai tvam asīti.*

**Перевод:**

**If one speaks harshly to father, mother, brother, sister, teacher, or brāhmaṇa, people say, “Shame on you. You are the killer of your father, your mother, your brother, your sister, your teacher. You are the killer of a brāhmaṇa.”**

**Комментарий:**

If you speak of father, etc. they are only lumps of flesh. Who is indicated by those words is not the jīva. This statement is the response.

### 7.15.3

*atha yady apy enān utkrānta-prāṇān śūlena samāsaṁ vyatisaṅdahet,*  
*naivainaṁ brūyuḥ pitṛhā 'sīti, na mātṛhā 'sīti, na bhrātṛhā 'sīti, na*  
*svasṛhā 'sīti, nācāryahā 'sīti, na brāhmaṇahā 'sīti.*

**Перевод:**

**If, after they have died, a person pierces them with a lance or burns them, no one says to him, “You are a killer of your father, mother, brother, sister, teacher or brāhmaṇa.”**

### 7.15.4

*prāṇo hyaitāni sarvāṇi bhavati.*  
*sa vā eña paśyan evaṁ manvāna evaṁ vijānan ativādī bhavati. Taṁ cet*  
*brūyuḥ ativādyasīti, ativādy asmīti brūyāt; nāpahnuvīta.*

**Перевод:**

**All of these are jīva. Seeing, contemplating and knowing in this way, one becomes an ativadi. If someone says to him, “You are an ativādī.” He should say, “I am an ativādī” He should not hide.**

**Комментарий:**

One criticizes a person who commits violence to a person when the jīva is in his body.

When the body is devoid of the jīva, one does not criticize him for violence since the person is no longer present. So words like “father” refer to the jīva in the body.

The jīva is higher that all others mentioned from name to aspiration. He who speaks of the jīva as the highest speaks in a superior way (ativādī). He should not hide this.

### 16

### 7.16.1

*eña tu vā ativadati, yaḥ satyenātivadati.*  
*so 'haṁ bhagavaḥ satyenātivadānīti.*  
*satyaṁ tv eva vijijñāsitavyam iti. satyaṁ bhagavo vijijñāsa iti.*

**Перевод:**

**But he who speaks because of the highest is ativādī.**

**I should speak of the highest, dear sir!**

**You should meditate on the highest.**

**Dear sir! I will meditate on the highest truth.**

**Комментарий:**

Nārada did not ask what is beyond the jīva. Sanakumāra by his own initiative gives instructions on the highest of all, the Lord.

The word tu indicates a unique fact. The word satya means Brahman, the Lord, since he is without change, having the same form constantly (satata). One who speaks, characterized by making the Lord the object of worship (satyena), who distinguishes the Lord from the jīva previously discussed, is the speaker of the highest (ativadati). He who has Brahman, the Lord, as the object of his meditation is ativādī.

satyaṁ tv eva vijijñāsitavyam means “you should meditate on the Lord.” This will bring about direct realization of the Lord.

satyaṁ bhagavo vijijñāse means “I will meditate on the Lord.”

### 17

### 7.17.1

*yadā vai vijānāti, atha satyaṁ vadati. nāvijānan satyaṁ vadati.*  
*Vijānann eva satyaṁ vadati. vijñānaṁ tv eva vijijñāsitavyam iti. vijñānaṁ*  
*bhagavo vijijṇāsa iti.*

**Перевод:**

**When a person realizes the Lord, he speaks of the Lord. Not realizing, he does not speak of the Lord. Realizing, one speaks of the Lord. One should become realized.**

**Dear sir, I undertake that realization.**

**Комментарий:**

The cause of speaking about the Lord (ativādī) is realizing the Lord.

Vijānāti here means directly seeing the Lord. When one realizes the Lord, one speaks of the Lord as the highest truth: satyena ativadati or satyam vadati.

vijñānaṁ tv eva vijijñāsitavyam means “one should become realized.” This is the meaning of this phrase later also. Contemplation (manana) is the process to accomplish this.

Revered father, I will undertake realization which is the cause of being an ativādī (vijñāse).

### 18

### 7.18.1

*yadā vai manute 'tha vijānāti. nāmatvā vijānāti. matvaiva vijānāti.*  
*Matis tv eva vijijñāsitavyeti. matiṁ bhagavo vijijñāsa iti.*

**Перевод:**

**When a person contemplates, he realizes the Lord. Not contemplating, he does not realize. Contemplating he realizes. Contemplation alone should be undertaken.**

**Respected father! I will undertake contemplation.**

**Комментарий:**

One should undertake contemplation to achieve meditation on the Lord.

### 19

### 7.19.1

*yadā vai śraddadhāti, atha manute. nāśraddadhanmanute. Śraddadhad eva*  
*manute. śraddhā tv eva vijijñāsitavyeti. śraddhāṁ bhagavo vijijñāsa iti.*

**Перевод:**

**When one has faith, he contemplates. Without faith, he cannot contemplate. Having faith one contemplates. One should undertake acquisition of faith.**

**Revered father! I will undertake acquisition of faith.**

**Комментарий:**

In order to establish hearing as a cause of contemplation, in order to accomplish hearing, faith in Brahman is necessary. One should have faith in hearing about Brahman.

### 20

### 7.20.1

*yadā vai nistiñṭhati. atha śraddadhāti. nānistiñṭhan śraddadhāti.*  
*nistiñṭhanneva śraddadhāti. niñṭhā tv eva vijijñāsitavyeti. niñṭhāṁ bhagavo vijijñāsa iti.*

**Перевод:**

**When one has steadiness, he has faith. Without steadiness he does not have faith. With steadiness he has faith. One should develop steadiness.**

**Revered father! I will strive for steadiness.**

**Комментарий:**

Steadiness means conviction that one should hear about Brahman and nothing else. This should achieved as a method of establishing faith in hearing.

### 21

### 7.21.1

*yadā vai karoti, atha nistiñṭhati. nākṛtvā nistiñṭhati. kṛtvaiva nistiñṭhati.*  
*Kṛtis tv eva vijijñāsitavyeti. kṛtiṁ bhagavo vijijñāsa iti.*

**Перевод:**

**When a person strives with effort, he becomes steady. Without striving with effort, he is not steady. Striving with effort, he becomes steady. Striving with effort should be attained.**

**Revered father! I desire to strive with effort.**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛti means effort. It means controlling the mind, detecting the cause of obstruction to hearing about the Lord. One should attain this state since it is the cause of steadiness, in the form of concentration on hearing.

### 22

### 7.22.1

*yadā vai sukhaṁ labhate, atha karoti. nāsukhaṁ labdhvā karoti.*  
*sukhameva labdhvā karoti. sukhaṁ tv eva vijijñāsitavyamiti. sukhaṁ*  
*bhagavo vijijñāsa iti.*

**Перевод:**

**When a person attains (knowledge of) the Lord’s happiness, he controls his mind. Without happiness he cannot control his mind. Attaining happiness, he can control his mind. One should attain happiness.**

**Revered rather! I desire to attain that happiness.**

**Комментарий:**

One cannot control the mind (kṛti) without understanding the extreme friendliness of the Lord. Therefore one must understand the happiness in Brahman, characterized by his extreme friendliness, which is the cause of mental control to allow detecting the obstacles to hearing. Since it is impossible to attain happiness before having faith, contemplation, hearing etc. happiness in this case means attaining knowledge concerning one’s object of attainment. Without this knowledge of favorability in the object of hearing, it is impossible to control the mind to detect obstacles to hearing. One should know about the extreme favorable attitude in the Lord, the object of hearing. Vijijñāsitvatyam here means one should know, or one should attain or one should meditate on this.

### 23

### 7.23.1

*yo vai bhūmā tat sukham. nālpe sukhamasti. bhūmaiva sukham. bhūmā*  
*tveva vijijñāsitavya iti. bhūmānaṁ bhagavo vijijñāsa iti.*

**Перевод:**

**He who is bountiful is happiness. Without plentitude there is no happiness. Plenitude is happiness. One should attain understanding of the Lord’s plenitude.**

**Revered father! I will understand he who is full of good qualities.**

**Комментарий:**

Bhūmā means abundant. It does not mean plentiful in terms of numbers. It means plentiful in excellent qualities since it is used here in apposition to happiness. Thus bhūmā also does not mean simply plentiful happiness (modifier of happiness) but rather abundance caused by excellence. It means he who acts as the shelter of abundant qualities. Thus the meaning is “that which has abundant qualities is that which is full of favorability.”

### 24

### 7.24.1

*yatra nānyat paśyati, nānyacchṛṇoti, nānyat vijānāti, sa bhūmā. atha*  
*yatrānyat paśyati, anyacchṛṇoti, anyat vijānāti, tad alpam. yo vai bhūmā,*  
*tad amṛtam. atha yad alpam, tan martyam.*  
*sa bhagavaḥ kasmin pratiñṭhita iti. sve mahimni, yadi vā na mahimnīti*

**Перевод:**

**Where one sees nothing else, hears nothing else, knows nothing else that is he who is abundant. Where one sees something else, hears something else, knows something else that is something much less. He who is abundant in qualities is immortal. He who is meager in qualities is subject to death.**

**Revered father! In what is this abundant Lord established?**

**He is established in his own greatness, and not in his greatness.**

**Комментарий:**

In experiencing something where one sees, hears or knows nothing else, that is bhūmā. This is because nothing else exists. “How can nothing else exist when jīvas and matter are different from the Lord?” If they are different from the Lord, how can they be included in the Lord? The Lord is endowed with jīvas and matter as his attributes. “How can it be said that there is nothing besides the Lord when one experiences powers of the Lord in the universe?” The Lord is distinct from his powers which are controlled by him. It is also taught that the Lord is the ātmā of everything. Tarati śokam ātmvit: he who knows ātmā surpasses lamentation. (Ch [7.1.3](https://vedarama.com/en/books/chandogya/7.1.3)) Jīvas and matter are not the ātmā. Rather the Lord enters within everything else and controls everything as the ātmā. Thus other things exist besides the Lord.

Like the word Brahman, bhūmā means “devoid of differentiation of objects,” devoid of “This is not that.” In this case, this absence of differentiation arises from apṛthak-siddhi (oneness through identity of quality and object). Jīva’s and matter’s existence depends on the Lord’s existence.

Bhūmā is devoid of birth and death (amṛtam) and endowed with eight qualities. The nitya muktas are also beyond birth and death but are dependent on the Lord.

All things depend on some support. Thus Nārada asks a question.

The Lord is supported by his own svarūpa. This means he has no support. He is not supported by material objects such as cattle and not by even by himself. To deny both conditions Sanatkumāra says, ‘Or not by his own powers (yadi vā na mahimni).”

### 7.24.2

*go aśvam iha mahimety ācakñate, hasti-hiraṇyaṁ*  
*dāsa-bhāryaṁ kñetrāṇy āyatanānīti. Nāham eva bravīmīti hovāca, anyo*  
*hy anyasmin pratiñṭhita iti.*

**Перевод:**

**They say that cows, horses, elephants, gold, servants, wife, lands and houses etc. are greatness.**

**I do not say this, since in this case that one object is established upon another object. The father said this.**

**Комментарий:**

There is no denial of the Lord’s greatness. Asya mahimānam iti vīta-śokaḥ: Knowing the Lord’s greatness one gives up sorrow. (Muṇḍaka Upaniñad) But the Lord is not supported by his own vibhūtis or greatness. He also denies that the Lord is supported by his own svarūpa. It is impossible that something should support itself (anyo hy anyasmin pratiñṭhita).

### 25

### 7.25.1

*sa evādhastāt, sa upariñṭāt, sa paścāt, sa purastāt, sa dakñiṇataḥ, sa*  
*uttarataḥ, sa evedaṁ sarvam iti.*  
*athāto 'haṅkārādeśa eva – aham evādhastāt, aham upariñṭāt, ahaṁ*  
*paścāt, ahaṁ purastāt, ahaṁ dakñiṇataḥ, aham uttarataḥ, aham evedaṁ*  
*sarvam iti.*

**Перевод:**

**Bhūmā alone is below. He is above. He is behind. He is in front. He is in the south. He is in the north. He is everything.**

**The instruction about ahaṅkāra (self) is w presented. I am below. I am above. I am behind. I am in front. I am to the south. I am to the north. I am everything.**

**Комментарий:**

“One sees nothing except the Lord everywhere” cannot be correct, since jīvas and matter are perceived to be different from the Lord. This is the response to that objection.

The statement “He is everything” (sa evedaṁ sarvam)” means that the Lord is the ātmā with all jīvas and matter as his body. In this way the Lord is spread everywhere. Thus it is said that he alone is below and above etc. No other being has this quality. In worshipping the Lord as the ātmā of all beings, one should also meditate on the Lord as the ātmā of one’s own self.

The instructions about the self are presented (ahaṅkārādeśaḥ). Atha indicates the same topic under discussion. Ataḥ indicates that the subject is the supreme Lord, not the jīva. Ahaṅkāra means “thinking of oneself.” This is a meditation on the Lord as the self within oneself. This is instruction for meditating on the Lord, not on the jīva. Ahaṅkārādeśaḥ means teachings about actions of worship of the Lord as the self. The method is then shown.

The Lord existing in all directions (bhūmā) is known as aham. It is not a teaching that the jīva is the ātmā of everything. That is impossible for the jīva. This meditation is performed so that the worshipper understands that the Lord is his very ātmā.

### 7.25.2

*athāta ātmādeśa eva - ātmaivādhastāt, ātmopariñṭāt, ātmā paścāt,*  
*ātmā purastāt, ātmā dakñiṇataḥ, ātmottarataḥ, ātmaivedaṁ sarvam iti.*  
*sa vā eña evaṁ paśyann evaṁ manvāna evaṁ vijānan ātmā-ratir ātma-krīḍa ātmā-mithuna ātmānandaḥ; sa svarāḍ bhavati. tasya sarveñu lokeñu kāmacāro bhavati.*  
*atha ye 'nyathā 'to vidur anya-rājān aste kñayya-lokā bhavanti. teñāṁ sarveñu lokeñv akāmacāro bhavati.*

**Перевод:**

**Ātmā is below. Ātmā is above. Ātmā is behind. Ātmā is in front. Ātmā is to the south. Ātmā is to the north Ātmā is everything.**

**He who sees thus, contemplates thus and realizes thus, will attain pleasure with the Lord, play with the Lord, pairing with the Lord, and bliss with the Lord. He will be self-ruling. He will wander in whatever worlds he desires.**

**Those who meditates differently will have others to rule them, will attain perishable planets. They cannot travel as they please.**

**Комментарий:**

This section shows that the previous reference to aham means the Lord. The word ātmā is used here. Previously ātmā refered to the Lord as in tarati śokam ātmavit: one who knows the Lord surpasses suffering. Atha again indicates the same subject. Teachings that the Lord is ātmā is now explained.

Bhūmā who is the ātmā of all is also the ātmā of the meditator. This is also the meaning of the previous section. The two statements about being the ātmā of all things resolve into one object.

The meditator’s joy usually derived from objects like garlands is in the Lord alone. His playing is with the Lord alone. His joy usually derived from association with women is in the Lord alone. His bliss arising from vibhūtis is in the Lord alone. Experience of all types of happiness of the person who meets the Lord through hearing, contemplation and meditation is includes in the Lord’s experience of bliss. He is his own king (svarāṭ): he is not subject to karma or to rules and prohibitions since he is endowed with satya-saṅkalpa. Those who do not practice in this way are subject to karma and rules and prohibitions (anya-rājān). They attain perishable planets, unlike those who worship bhūmā.

## 26.

### 7.26.1

*tasya ha vā etasyaivaṁ paśyata evaṁ manvānasyaivaṁ vijānata ātmataḥ*  
*prāṇa ātmata āśā '' tmataḥ smara ātmata ākaśa ātmatas teja ātmata āpa*  
*ātmata āvirbhāva-tirobhāvāv ātmato ''nnam ātmato balam ātmato*  
*vijñānam ātmato dhyānam ātmataś cittam ātmataḥ saṇkalpa ātmato mana*  
*ātmato vāg ātmato nāmātmato mantra ātmataḥ karmāṇy ātmata evedaṁ*  
*sarvam iti.*

**Перевод:**

**For the person who sees in this way, contemplates in this way and realizes in this way, jīva arises from the Lord, aspiration arises from the Lord, memory arises from the Lord, ether arises from the Lord, fire arises from the Lord, water arises from the Lord, the appearance and disappearance of all these arise from the Lord. Food arises from the Lord, strength arises from the Lord, scriptural knowledge arises from the Lord, meditation arises from the Lord, citta arises from the Lord, will power arises from the Lord, mind arises from the Lord, voice arises from the Lord, name arises from the Lord, mantra arises from the Lord, karmas arise from the Lord. All this arises from the Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

This makes clear that the antaryāmī (ātmā) of the meditator of the previous section is the ātmā of all things up to the jīva (prāṇa).

### 7.26.2

*tadeña ślokaḥ - na paśyo mṛtyuṁ paśyati na rogaṁ nota duḥkhatām.*  
*sarvaṁ ha paśyaḥ paśyati sarvam āpnoti sarvaśaḥ. iti.*  
*sa ekadhā bhavati tridhā bhavati pañcadhā.*  
*saptadhā navadhā caiva punaś caikādaśa (śaḥ) smṛtaḥ.*  
*śatañca daśa caikañca sahasrāṇi ca viśaṁtiḥ.*  
*āhāraśuddhau sattva-śuddhiḥ sattva-śuddhau dhruvā smṛtiḥ.*  
*smṛti-lambhe sarva-granthīnāṁ vipramokñaḥ.*  
*tasmai mṛdata-kañāyāya {devarñaye nāradāya}tamasaḥ pāraṁ darśayati*  
*bhagavān sanatkumāra. taṁ skanda ity ācakñate taṁ skanda ity ācakñate.*

**Перевод:**

**There is a verse.**

**The seer of Brahman does not see death, sickness or suffering.**

**The seer of Brahman sees everything and attains everything completely.**

**He can assume one form, two forms, three forms, five forms seven forms, nine forms, or eleven forms, a hundred and ten forms or a thousand and twenty forms.**

**If food is pure, the mind is pure. If the mind is pure, memory of the Lord is steady. When memory is fixed, one is freed of all knots.**

**Unto Nārada, washed of all impurities, revered Sanatkumāra taught about the supreme Lord beyond ignorance. Sanatkumāra is called Skanda.**

**Комментарий:**

Paśyaḥ means a person who has realized the Lord, in meditation. This is similar to seeing the Lord directly.

He does not see death, sickness or anything unfavorable that produces suffering in this world. Seeing the Lord directly, he attains all desires objects at all times, just by his will.

He attains the eight qualities such as destruction of all sins. He sees no suffering: there is nothing that hinders his attaining liberation from the world. He is able to assume a number of bodies at his will.

The person must be devoid of sin, for that is an obstacle to the meditation in the antaḥkaraṇa. Thus one cannot eat foods in the modes of rajas and tamas. He should eat only sāttvika foods. By pure food, the mind becomes pure. From the pure mind, constant meditation can take place (dhruva-smṛtiḥ). With constant meditation, one is freed from the knots of ignorance and material attachment which are difficult to untie.

Sanatkumāra taught Nārada who had purified his mind about the supreme Lord who can be seen by meditation. The teachings could only be given to a person qualified with a pure mind since the teachings concern meditating on Brahman in name etc. Sanatkumāra is called Skanda since he destroyed obstacles to knowledge of Brahman.

## Part 8

### 1

### 8.1.1

*atha yad idam asmin brahma-pure daharaṁ puṇḍarīkaṁ veśma daharo 'sminn antarākāśaḥ tasmin yad antas tad anveñṭavyaṁ tad vāva vijijñāsitavyam iti.*

**Перевод:**

**In the city of Brahman, there is a lotus-like abode and a space (ākāśa). One should seek what is within that space. That is to be realized. In that lotus space, one should contemplate and meditate upon the ākāśa and what is within that (antaḥ).**

**Комментарий:**

A new subject, dahara-vidyā begins with the word atha. There is a small abode of the Lord shaped like a lotus, the heart, in the body of the meditator, which is called a city, since it has nine gates. In that dwelling (the heart) there is a fine ākāśa (daharaḥ). Ākāśa refers to the supreme Lord, manifests completely (ā-kāśate). The reason will be explained later.

### 8.1.2

*Tañ cet brūyuḥ – yad idam asmin brahmapure daharaṁ puṇḍarīkaṁ*  
*veśma daharo 'sminn antarākāśaḥ, kiṁ tad atra vidyate yad anveñṭavyaṁ*  
*yad vāva vijijñāsitavyam iti, sa brūyāt*

**Перевод:**

**If the student asks, “What should be sought and realized in the ākāśa in the lotus space in the city of Brahman?” The teacher should reply as follows.**

**Комментарий:**

The student may ask the teacher about what should be sought, since he does not know about the qualities in the Lord who is called the small space (daharākāśa) within the small heart. And since the Lord is within everything, what can exist within the Lord?

### 8.1.3

*yāvān vā ayam ākāśas tāvān eño 'ntarhṛdaya ākāśaḥ ubhe asmin dyāvāpṛthivī antar eva samāhite ubhāv agniś ca vāyuśca sūryācandramasāv ubhau vidyun nakñatrāṇi. Yac cāsyehāsti yac ca nāsti, sarvaṁ tad asmin samāhitam iti.*

**Перевод:**

**The ākāśa in the heart is as vast as the external ākāśa. In this ākāśa within the heart exist both heaven and earth, both fire and air, the sun and moon, lightning and the constellations. What exists externally and does not exist externally exists within that space in the heart.**

**Комментарий:**

The ākāśa in the heart is as vast as the external ākāśa. Heaven and earth indicate places of enjoyment, enjoyable objects and instruments of enjoyment. Sun, moon, etc. indicate the enjoyers within those places. Whatever enjoyables exist in this world of the worshipper and what does not exist there, existing only as desires in the mind, exist in the daharākāśa. This indicates the greatness of that daharākaśa. It contains the whole universe and is superior in enjoyment to the enjoyment in this external universe.

### 8.1.4

*Tañ cet brūyuḥ – asmiṁś ced idaṁ brahmapure sarvaṁ samāhitaṁ sarvāṇi*  
*ca bhūtāni sarve ca kāmāḥ, yadainajjarāvāpnoti pradhvaṁsate*  
*vā kiṁ tato 'tiśiñyata iti.*

**Перевод:**

**The student may ask, “If in that city of Brahman everything-- all beings and all enjoyables-- exist there, what remains when the body ages and is destroyed?”**

### 8.1.5

*sa brūyāt - nāsya jarayaitaj jīryati, na vadhenāsya hanyate; etat satyaṁ*  
*brahma-puram.*  
*asmim kāmāḥ samāhitāḥ. eña ātmā 'pahatapāpmā vijaro vimṛtyur viśoko*  
*vijighatso 'pipāsaḥ satyakāmaḥ satya-saṅkalpaḥ.*  
*yathā hy eveha prajā anvāviśanti yathānuśāsanaṁ yaṁ yam antabhikāmā bhavanti yaṁ jana-padaṁ yaṁ kñetra-bhāgaṁ taṁ tam evopajīvanti.*

**Перевод:**

**The teacher should answer thus. “This Brahman, daharākāśa, does not wither by old age of the body. It is not die when the body dies. This is the unchanging city of Brahman. In this Brahman are all auspicious qualities. This ātmā has no sin, no old age, no death, no suffering, no hunger, and no thirst. All his desires are fulfilled. Everything manifests by his will. Just like citizens, following the king and obeying his orders and maintain their lives by objects that they request, such as a certain country and piece of land, in this life, so they live in the next world (under another person’s control).**

**Комментарий:**

This Brahman called daharākāśa (etat) existing like a city, endowed with attractive objects, places, means of enjoyment and enjoyers, is not just a means of providing space, like the space in a pot. It is more like a city which provides support for objects and places for enjoyment than a pot which simply provides space for yogurt etc.

“But even if it is self-supporting, since it exists within the heart within the body, it will be subject to old age and death.” This Brahman is without change (satyam). Therefore it does not follow the body’s ageing and death. Or satyam refers to the definition given later tad yat sat tad amṛtam ([8.3.1](https://vedarama.com/en/books/chandogya/8.3.1)). Thus satyam refers to the Lord who monitors all jīvas and matter. He is different from the pot which contains mere space.

Kāmāḥ means having auspicious qualities. Those qualities are then listed. The Lord consists of (ātmā) these qualities. Satya-saṅkalpa means that things manifest by his will. Papa refers not only to sin by to pious acts as well as in Chāndogya Upaniñad [8.4.1](https://vedarama.com/en/books/chandogya/8.4.1). na suṛtaṁ na duñkṛtaṁ sarve pāpamāno to nivartante: All sins turn from him since the Lord is devoid of all sin.

Thus the Lord is not contaminated by sin or piety. Satya-kāmaḥ means he has eternal pleasurable places, objects, and means to experience them. Tad akñare parame vyoman: the Lord has eternal vibhūtis. (Mahā-nārāyaṇa Upaniñad)

In this world, people, abiding by the rules of the king, follow him. What do they do? They desire temporary results (antam) of their actions such as country and land, following the rules of the king. They sustain their lives on these results. As they live in this way under the king in this world, they live in the next world also, under another’s control.

Or as people pursue results that they desire in this world, and follow the rules of the king, so in next life also, they sustain their lives.

### 8.1.6

*Tad yatheha karma-cito lokaḥ kñīyate, evam evāmutra puṇya-cito lokaḥ*  
*kñīyate. Tad ya ihātmānam ananuvidya vrajanty etāṁś ca satyān kāmān,*  
*teñāṁ sarveñu lokeñv akāmacāro bhavati.*  
*atha ya ihātmānam anuvidya vrajanty etāṁś ca satyān kāmān, teñāṁ*  
*sarveñu lokeñu kāmacāro bhavati.*

**Перевод:**

**Just as in this world the environment earned by pious acts is destroyed, in the next world also the planet earned by pious acts is destroyed.**

**Those people who, without knowing the Lord who has eternal auspicious qualities, go to the next world, and do not wander as they please in all worlds.**

**Those who, knowing the Lord who has eternal auspicious qualities, go from this world, wander in all worlds as they desire.**

**Комментарий:**

Not only are those who go to the next world by karmas not independent, but they have temporary results.

The planet earned by acts like serving the king are destructible. The planet earned by pious acts in the next life is also destructible.

Not meditating on the Lord (ātmānam) who has the eight qualities and other auspicious qualities eternally (satyān kāmān), those who go from this world do not travel in all worlds as they please. They are under another’s control.

Those who, knowing the Lord with the eight qualities and other auspicious qualities depart from this world, experience worlds filled with the Lord’s vibhūtis, and not the temporary worlds. They are fully satisfied and not under other’s control.

### 2

### 8.2.1

*sa yadi pitṛ-loka-kāmo bhavati, saṅkalpād evāsya pitaraḥ samu -*  
*ttiñṭhanti. tena pitṛ-lokena saṁpanno mahīyate.*

**Перевод:**

**If he desires to see one of his departed ancestors, by his will that person arrives. With him, he is worshipped.**

**Комментарий:**

This person, liberated, if he desires to see persons of previous lives, by his will, they arrive. With them he is worshipped. The liberated person by his will, without effort, attains what he desires.

### 8.2.2

*atha yadi mātṛ-loka-kāmo bhavati saṅkalpād evāsya mātaraḥ*  
*samuttiñṭhanti. tena mātṛ-lokena saṁpanno mahīyate.*

**Перевод:**

**If he desires to see one of his mothers of previous lives, by his will that person arrives. With her, he is worshipped.**

### 8.2.3

*atha yadi bhrātṛloka-kāmo bhavati, saṅkalpād evāsya bhrātaraḥ*  
*samuttiñṭhanti. tena bhrātṛ-lokena saṁpanno mahīyate.*

**Перевод:**

**If he desires to see one of his departed brothers, by his will that person arrives. With him, he is worshipped.**

### 8.2.4

*atha yadi svasṛ-loka-kāmo bhavati, saṅkalpād evāsya svasāraḥ*  
*samuttiñṭhanti. tena svasṛ-lokena saṁpanno mahīyate.*

**Перевод:**

**If he desires to see one of his departed sisters, by his will that person arrives. With her, he is worshipped.**

### 8.2.5

*atha yadi sakhi-loka-kāmo bhavati, saṅkalpād evāsya sakhāyaḥ*  
*samuttiñṭhanti. tena sakhi-lokena saṁpanno mahīyate.*

**Перевод:**

**If he desires to see one of his friends, by his will that person arrives. With him, he is worshipped.**

### 8.2.6

*atha yadi gandha-mālya-loka-kāmo bhavati, saṅkalpādevāsya gandha-*  
*mālye samuttiñṭhataḥ. tena gandhamālyalokena saṁpanno mahīyate.*

**Перевод:**

**If he desires perfumes and garlands, by his will, they arrive. Endowed with perfumes and garlands, he is worshipped.**

### 8.2.7

*atha yady anna-pāna-loka-kāmo bhavati, saṅkalpād evāsya annapāne*  
*samuttiñṭhataḥ. tenānnapānalokena saṁpanno mahīyate.*

**Перевод:**

**If he desires to see food and drink, by his will, they arrive. Endowed with them, he is worshipped.**

### 8.2.8

*atha yadi gīta-vāditra-loka-kāmo bhavati, saṅkalpād evāsya gīta-vāditre*  
*samuttiñṭhataḥ. tena gīta-vāditra--lokena saṁpanno mahīyate.*

**Перевод:**

**If he desires songs and music, by his will, they arrive. Endowed with songs and music, he is worshipped.**

### 8.2.9

*atha yadi strī-loka-kāmo bhavati, saṅkalpād evāsya striyaḥ samu-*  
*ttiñṭhanti. tena strī-lokena saṁpanno mahīyate.*

**Перевод:**

**If he desires women, by his will, they arrive. Endowed with women, he is worshipped.**

### 8.2.10

*yaṁ yam antam abhikāmo bhavati, yaṁ kāmaṁ kāmayate, so 'sya*  
*saṅkalpād eva samuttiñṭhati. tena saṁpanno mahīyate.*

**Перевод:**

**Whatever object he desires, whatever he desires, by his will, that arrives. Endowed with that, he is worshipped.**

### 3

### 8.3.1

*ta ime satyāḥ kāmā anṛtāpidhānāḥ. teñāṁ satyānāṁ satām anṛtam*  
*apidhānam. yo yo hy asyetaḥ praiti, na tam iha darśanāya labhate.*

**Перевод:**

**These auspicious qualities are covered by karma. The existence of these qualities is covered by karma. One cannot see here that person who has departed from here.**

**Комментарий:**

The auspicious qualities (kāmāḥ) such as apahata-pāpma fixed in the Lord (satya) are covered by anṛta. Ṛtam means actions without desire for results, which result in attaining the Lord. Anṛtam are actions which lead to saṁsāra and are opposed to attaining the Lord.

These qualities of the Lord who is within the self are made to disappear by ignorance in the form of karma and thus are not visible. The worshipper cannot see the relative of the worshipper (asya) who has departed from here (itaḥ).

### 8.3.2

*atha ye cāsyeha jīvā ye ca pretā yac cānyad icchan na labhate, sarvaṁ*  
*tad atra gatvā vindate. atra hy asyaite satyāḥ kāmā anṛtāpidhānāḥ.*  
*tad yathā hiraṇya-nidhiṁ nihitam akñetrajñā upary upari sañcaranto na vindeyuḥ, evam evemāḥ sarvāḥ prajā ahar-ahar gacchantya evaṁ brahma-lokaṁ na vindanti; anṛtena hi pratyūḍhāḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**Attaining the Lord, daharākāśa, one attains all those persons living or dead which one desired but did not attain. Here, those qualities of the Lord are covered by karma.**

**People, ignorant of its location, wander about over a treasure of gold placed there, and do not obtain it. Similarly people daily go to the Lord but do not attain him. They are covered by karma.**

**Комментарий:**

Since the liberated devotee has no attachment for material things, fulfilling such desires is only to show the capacity of that person who can fulfill any desire. Previously these qualities were covered by karma, but now, attaining the Lord, those qualities manifest in him.

Just as people, not knowing that a place has treasure, though walking over a hidden treasure of gold, do not obtain it, all people at the time of deep sleep (Ch. 6.1.2), daily go to the Lord, but they do not attain or know him (na vindanti). They are covered by karma (pratyuḍāḥ). Or daily people move about over the Lord always present within, not only during deep sleep, and do not attain him.

### 8.3.3

*sa vā eña ātmā hṛdi. Tasyaitad eva niruktaṁ hṛdy ayam iti. Tasmād dhṛdayam. Ahar ahar vā evaṁvit svargaṁ lokam eti.*

**Перевод:**

**The Lord is in the heart. Thus the word hṛdayam means “he (ayam) is in the heart (hṛdi).”**

**He who knows this attains the Lord daily.**

**Комментарий:**

He who knows this daily till death, who is fixed in meditating on the Lord, daharākāśa, attains the Lord, visible (lokam) as a form of happiness (svarga). Since he meditates on the Lord he attains the Lord and does not attain material Svarga-loka.

### 8.3.4

*atha ya eña saṁprasādo 'smāc charīrāt samutthāya paraṁ*  
*jyotir upasaṁpadya svena rūpeṇābhiniñpadyate,*  
*eña ātmeti hovacaitad amṛtam abhayam etad brahmeti.*  
*tasya ha vā etasya brahmaṇo nāma satyam iti.*

**Перевод:**

**“This jīva, leaving the body, attaining the Lord, manifests his own form.**

**This is the Lord. This Lord is immortal and without fear. This is Brahman.**

**The name of Brahman is satyam.” Thus spoke Prajāpati**

**Комментарий:**

Attainment of the Lord is described. Atha indicates continuation of the subject. Saṁprasāda means the state of deep sleep and related to that, the jīva. This jīva, the worshipper, who daily meditates on the Lord within, leaving this vile material body, attaining the Lord (paramjyotiḥ), fixed in a certain place, manifests his own form.

### 8.3.5

*tāni ha vā etāni trīṇyakñarāṇi sat ti yam iti. tad yat sat, tadamṛtam; atha yat ti, tanmartyam;*  
*atha yad yam, tenobhe yacchati. Yad anenobhe yacchati, tasmāt yam.*  
*Ahar ahar vā evaṁvit svargaṁ lokam eti.*

**Перевод:**

**The word satyam has three syllables: sat ti and yam. Sat is the immortal. Ti is the mortal. Yam is that which regulates both. He is called yam because he regulates the two. He who knows this attains the Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

Amṛtam means the jīvas and martyam means matter. He who knows the Lord who controls jīvas and matter attains the Lord.

### 4

### 8.4.1

*atha ya ātmā sa setur vidhṛtir eñāṁ lokānām asaṁbhedāya. naitaṁ*  
*setum ahorātre tarato na jarā na mṛtyuḥ. na śoko na sukṛtaṁ na duñkṛtaṁ*  
*sarve pāpmāno 'to nivartante. apahatapāpmā hy eña brahma-lokaḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**The Lord is an embankment, a barrier, so that the worlds do not mix. Day and night do not touch the Lord, nor do old age or death, lamentation, pious acts or sinful acts. All sins turn from him since the Lord is devoid of all sin.**

**Комментарий:**

Vidhṛti means a separator. The Lord is a separating barrier so that the worlds do not mix (asaṁmedāya). If the Lord did not support the universe by his aṁśa, all qualities would mix. Earth has fragrance, water as coolness, fire has heat. By the order of the Lord, these qualities are established. Setu means that which binds (sinoti). The Lord containing jīvas and matter within himself, does not allow the objects derived from them to mix their qualities. No sin can touch the Lord since he has the power to counteract them.

### 8.4.2

*tasmād vā etaṁ setuṁ tīrtvā 'ndhassan anandho bhavati; viddhassan*  
*aviddho bhavati; upatāpī san anupatāpī bhavati. Tasmād vā etaṁ*  
*setuṁ tīrtvā api naktam ahar evābhiniñpadyate. Sakṛd vibhāto hy evaiña*  
*brahma-lokaḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**Because the Lord is devoid of all sin, attaining the Lord, the blind becomes free of blindness, the wounded becomes free of wounds and the sick person becomes free of sickness. Attaining the Lord, night becomes day. The Lord is always effulgent.**

**Комментарий:**

Since the Lord is free of sin (tasmāt), attaining (tīrthvā) the Lord (setum), the blind, wounded and person afflicted by fever become endowed with spiritual bodies devoid of faults. Attaining the Lord, darkness becomes light. Devoid of ignorance, night becomes like day. There is no distinction of day and night since the Lord is always effulgent, without being covered.

### 8.4.3

*Tad ya evaitaṁ brahmalokaṁ brahmacaryeṇānuvindanti, teñāmevaiña*  
*brahmalokaḥ, teñāṁ sarveñu lokeñu kāmacāro bhavati.*

**Перевод:**

**Those who know this Lord through brahmacarya attain the Lord. They wander in all worlds as they desire.**

**Комментарий:**

Those who know the Lord by instructions of ācārya and scripture along with practicing brahmacarya—rejection of women as if they were grass--attain the Lord and experience all planets. Those who do not practice brahmacarya do not attain the Lord. He who has the power to support the universe is Parabrahman, the daharākāśa.

### 5

### 8.5.1

*atha yat yajña ityācakñate brahmacaryam eva tat. brahmacaryeṇa hy eva*  
*yo jñātā taṁ vindate.*  
*atha yad iñṭam ity ācakñate, brahmacaryam eva tat. brahmacaryeṇa*  
*hy eveñṭvātmānam anuvindate.*

**Перевод:**

**What is called sacrifice is brahmacarya only. One who knows the Lord by brahmacarya attains the Lord.**

**What is called iñṭa like darśa-purṇamāsā is brahmacarya only. One who sacrifices by brahmacarya attains the Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

Since the activities that will be mentioned such as sacrifice, iñṭa and satrāyaṇa, are accomplished by brahmacarya, brahmacarya is first praised as the means of attaining Brahman.

The Vedic sacrifice is called brahmacarya because it is accomplished by brahmacarya.

Knowledge, which is the result of brahmacarya, is the means to attain the Lord. That knowledge is a form of sacrifice. Thus sacrifice is identified with the attainment of knowledge through brahmacarya.

One who has knowledge of Brahman through brahmacarya attains the Lord. Yo jñātā has similarity to yajña, to indicate that yajña is perfected through brahmacarya.

Having sacrificed (iñṭvā) by brahmacarya, he attains the Lord.

### 8.5.2

*atha yat sātṛāyaṇam ity ācakñate, brahmacaryam eva tat. brahmacaryeṇa*  
*hy eva sata ātmanas trāṇaṁ vindate.*  
*atha yan maunam ity ācakñate, brahmacaryam eva tat. brahmacaryeṇa*  
*hy evātmānam anuvidya manute.*

**Перевод:**

**What is called sātrāyaṇa (soma sacrifice) is brahmacarya. By brahmacarya one protects the ātmā or sat.**

**What is called silence is brahmacarya. By brahmacarya one knows and contemplates the Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

Sat means ātmā. One protects (trāṇa) the ātmā (sat) by brahmacarya, which dispels everything except the Lord. This is similar to sattrāyaṇa.

Mauna means controlling speaking. This has a sound similar to manana. Contemplation (manana) is dependent on hearing about the Lord. This is accomplished by brahmacarya.

### 8.5.3

*atha yad anāśakāyanam ity ācakñate, brahmacaryam eva tat. eña hy ātmā*  
*na naśyati, yaṁ brahmacaryeṇānuvindate.*  
*atha yad araṇyāyanam ity ācakñate, brahmacaryam eva tat. Araś ca ha vai*  
*ṇyaścārṇavau brahmaloke tṛtīyasyām ito divi.*  
*Tad airammadīyaṁ saraḥ; tad aśvatthaḥ soma-savanaḥ; tad aparājitā*  
*pūrbrahmaṇaḥ. Prabhu-vimitaṁ hiraṇmayam.*

**Перевод:**

**What is called “not eating” is brahmacarya. The Lord attained by brahmacarya is not destroyed.**

**What is called “going to the forest (hermit)” is attained by brahmacarya.**

**There are two oceans called ara and nya in the world of the Lord--in the third world.**

**There is a lake called airammadīyam, a pippala tree called soma-savana, and a city called aparājitā. There is a golden hall made by the Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

The path of not eating (anaśakāyaṇa) is the indestructible result (anāśa), the Lord attained by brahmacarya. The third world means the spiritual world, not material Brahmaloka. From bhuva to Brahmaloka are counted as the second world. Airammadīya means “the place where they become intoxicated (mādyante) with amṛta (irayā). Thus it is a lake of nectar. The pippala tree is called soma-savana, because it gives joy like seeing the moon (soma). The city of Brahman is called aparajīta--not attained by persons who do not know Brahman. There is a golden hall made by the Lord, which he accepts as a place for his enjoyment. Though the Lord is all pervading, he is present there in his eternal form. Attaining the Lord means attaining him with this special place.

### 8.5.4

*tat ya evaitāvarañ ca ṇyañcārṇavau brahmaloke*  
*brahmacaryeṇānuvindanti, teñām evaiña brahma-lokaḥ teñāṁ sarveñu*  
*lokeñu kāmacāro bhavati.*

**Перевод:**

**Those who attain ara and nya in the spiritual world by brahmacarya attain the Lord. They wander in the worlds as they desire.**

**Комментарий:**

Brahmacarya is best since attaining the lakes which allow one to attain the Lord depends on brahmacarya.

### 6

### 8.6.1

*atha yā etā hṛdayasya nāḍyaḥ, tāḥ piṅgalasyāṇimnas tiñṭhanti śuklasya*  
*nīlasya pītasya lohitasyeti. asau vā ādityaḥ piṅgalaḥ; eña śukla eña nīla*  
*eña eña pīta eña lohitaḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**The fine nādīs of the heart are brown, white, black, yellow and red. The sun is brown, white, black, yellow and red.**

**Комментарий:**

Going by the nāḍī in the head to attain the Lord is described. The nāḍī are full of subtle rasa from food. The sun is known to have various colors as learned from madhu-vidyā. The rasa takes up these colors from the rays of the sun.

### 8.6.2

*Tad yathā mahā-patha ātata ubhau grāmau gacchatīmañ cāmuñ ca, evam evaitā ādityasya raśmaya ubhau lokau gacchatīmañ cāmuñ ca. amuñmād ādityāt pratāyante; tā āsu nāḍīñu sṛptāḥ. ābhyo nāḍībhyaḥ pratāyante; te 'muñminn āditye sṛptāḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**Just as a long road enter into two villages, to one and the other, the rays of the sun extend to both worlds, this and that. Spreading from the sun the rays enter into the nāḍīs. They spread from the nāḍīs and enter into the sun.**

### 8.6.3

*tad yatraitat suptaḥ samastaḥ saṁprasannaḥ*  
*svapnaṁ na vijānāti, āsu tadā nāḍīñu sṛpto bhavati.*  
*taṁ na kaścana pāpmā spṛñati. tejasā hi tadā saṁpanno bhavati.*

**Перевод:**

**Thus, when a person, having withdrawn his sense, sleeps peacefully and does not see dreams, he enters these nāḍīs. No sin touches him. He is endowed with Brahman.**

**Комментарий:**

When (yatra), having withdrawn his senses (samastaḥ), devoid of troubles arising from external objects (samprasannaḥ), the sleeping person does not see dreams--when he is deep sleep--he enters the nāḍīs to go to Brahman in the heart. In deep sleep, the results of karma do not affect him. No sin touches him. They have no power to affect him. At that time he is with Brahma, who is effulgent (tejasā). He is situated in Brahman and is devoid of using his body and senses.

### 8.6.4

*atha yatraitad abalimānaṁ nīto bhavati, tam abhita āsīnā āhuḥ, jānāsi māṁ*  
*jānāsi mām iti. sa yāvad asmāc charīrād anutkrānto bhavati, tāvaj jānāti.*

**Перевод:**

**When a person becomes weak, relatives sit around him and say, “Do you recognize me? Do you recognize me?” As long as he does not leave the body, he recognizes them.**

**Комментарий:**

Abalimā means weak from disease etc. etat can indicate “being situated in the nāḍīs.”

### 8.6.5

*atha yatraitad asmāc charīrād utkrāmati, athaitair eva raśmibhir ūrdhvam*  
*ākramate. sa om iti vāhodvā mīyate. sa yāvat kñipyen manaḥ,*  
*tāvad ādityaṁ gacchati.*  
*etad vai khalu loka-dvāraṁ viduñāṁ prapadanaṁ nirodho 'viduñām.*

**Перевод:**

**When he leaves the body, the jīva goes upward by those rays described. He chants Om. He goes to the sun at the speed of the mind. The sun is the gateway for going to the spiritual world for those in knowledge. It is closed for the ignorant.**

**Комментарий:**

He goes upwards by the rays which leave the nāḍīs (etaiḥ). That person in knowledge (saḥ) chants Om. Om indicates the Lord as in om tat sad iti nirdeśa brāhmaṇas tridvidhaḥ smṛtaḥ: Brahman is defined in three ways by om tat sat. (BG 13.23) Glorifying the Lord, he remembers (mīyate). At the time of dying he remembers the name of the Lord. Or he is raised up (mīyate) by the rays of the sun. vā indicates emphasis. For those in knowledge the sun is the door for going (prapadanam).

### 8.6.6

*tad eña ślokaḥ -*  
*śatañ caikā ca hṛdayasya nāḍyas tāsāṁ mūrdhānam abhinissṛtaikā.*  
*tayordhvam āyann amṛtatvam eti viñvaṅṅ anyā utkramaṇe bhavanti.*  
*utkramaṇe bhavanti.*

**Перевод:**

**There is a verse concerning this. There are a hundred and one nāḍīs in the heart. One extends to the top of the head. Going upwards by that nāḍī, one attains immortality. All the other nāḍīs going variously are merely for going out of the body.**

**Комментарий:**

Viñvaṇṇ anyā means other nāḍīs going in various directions.

### 7

### 8.7.1

*ya ātmā 'pahatapāpmā vijaro vimṛtyuviśoko vijighatco 'pipāsaḥ*  
*satya-kāmaḥ satya-saṅkalpaḥ, so 'nveñṭavyaḥ sa vijijñāsitavyaḥ. sa*  
*sarvāṁś ca lokān āpnoti sarvaṁś ca kāmān yas tam ātmānam anuvidya*  
*vijānātīti ha prajāpatir uvāca.*

**Перевод:**

**One should seek out and realize the Lord who is free of sin, free of old age, free of death, free of lamentation, free of hunger, free of thirst, who is full satisfied and whose will becomes fact. That person who after knowing the Lord according to scriptural statements meditates on him, attains all worlds and all attractive objects. Prajāpati thus spoke.**

**Комментарий:**

After concluding dahara-vidyā, its aṅga called pratyag-ātma-vidya begins.

Anuvidya means after knowing the meaning of scriptural statements. Vijānāti means meditating.

### 8.7.2

*tad dhobhaye devāsurā anububudhire. te hocuḥ - hanta*  
*tam ātmānam anvicchāmo yam ātmānam anviñya sarvāṁś ca lokān āpnoti*  
*sarvāṁśca kāmān iti.*  
*indro haiva devānām abhipravavrāja; virocano 'surāṇām. tau*  
*hāsaṁvidānāv eva samitpāṇī prajāpati-sakāśam ājagmatuḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**The devatās and demons both heard the words of Prajāpati. They said, “Let us seek that ātmā, seeking whom, one attains all worlds and all desirable objects. Indra of the devatās went to him, and Virocana of the demons also went. The two, carrying wood in their hands, went to Prajāpati without knowledge of the other.**

**Комментарий:**

They heard (anububudhire) Prajāpati’s words through others. Anivicchāmaḥ means “we understand through hearing and contemplation.”

### 8.7.3

*tau ha dvātriṁśataṁ varñāṇi brahmacaryam ūñatuḥ. tau ha prajāpatir uvāca kim icchantāv avāstam iti. tau hocatur ya ātmā 'pahatapāpmā*  
*vijaro vimṛtyurviśoko vijighatso 'pipāsaḥ satyasaṅkalpaḥ so 'nveñṭavyaḥ*  
*sa vijijñāsitavyaḥ sa sarvāṁś ca lokān āpnoti sarvāṁś ca kāmān yas tam ātmānam anuvidya vijānātīti bhagavato vaco vedayante. Tam icchantāv avāstam iti.*

**Перевод:**

**The two undertook brahmacarya for thirty-two years. Prajāpati said to them, “For what purpose have you observed brahmacarya?” They said, “People know of your words: One should seek out and realize the Lord who is free of sin, free of old age, free of death, free of lamentation, free of hunger, free of thirst, who is full satisfied and whose will becomes fact. That person who, after knowing the Lord according to scriptural statements, meditates on him, attains all worlds and all attractive objects. Desiring that knowledge, we have undertaken brahmacarya.”**

### 8.7.4

*tau ha prajāpatir uvāca ya eño 'kñiṇi puruño dṛśyate, eña ātmeti*  
*hovācaitad amṛtam abhayam etad brahmeti.*  
*atha yo 'yaṁ bhagavo 'psi parikhyāyate yaś cāyam ādarśe katama eña iti.*  
*eña u evaiñu sarveñv anteñu parikhyāyata iti sa hovāca.*

**Перевод:**

**Prajāpati said to them, “The person seen in the eye is ātmā.” He said, “He is Brahman, full of knowledge, fully blissful and free of suffering.”**

**They said, “Who is that seen in water or in a mirror?”**

**He said, “He is seen in all these.”**

**Комментарий:**

The person in the eye is not the supreme Lord. This is made clear later by Prajāpati. It refers to the jīva. But he does not teach that the jīva is really situated in the eye. One determines the presence of jīva in the body by the calmness or disturbance in the eye. Since one can determine the presence of the jīva by the state of the eye, the jīva is said to be in the eye.

Just as a person in a house goes out to another house, the jīva goes from one body to another. Amṛta here means having great bliss. Abhaya means not touched by suffering. Brahman means being great because of having unrestricted knowledge.

The two think that the ātmā in the eye is a reflection. Thus they ask about reflections. Is the reflection the same as that ātmā seen in the eye, or is it different. Prajāpati corrects their mistake. That persons in the eye is ātmā (the Lord): he is seen within water and everywhere else. He answered in this way to correct their mistake without harshly demolishing their error. Thinking themselves greatly learned, as heads of the devatā and demons, they might become disheartened and not ask further questions. Thus they would be deprived of knowledge of ātmā.

## 8.

### 8.8.1

*Uda-śarāva ātmānam avekñya yad ātmano na vijānīthas tan me prabratam iti. tau hoda-śarāve 'vekñāñcakrāte. tau ha prajāpatiruvāca - kiṁ paśyatha*  
*iti. tau hocatuḥ, sarvam evedam āvāṁ bhagava ātmānaṁ paśyāva ālomabhya ā nakhebhyaḥ pratirūpam iti.*

**Перевод:**

**Looking at yourself in a plate full of water, tell me if you do not understand about ātmā,**

**yourself. The two looked in the plate full of water. Prajāpati said to them, “What do you see?” They said, “We see ourselves completely as reflections from hair to nails.”**

### 8.8.2

*tau ha prajāpatir uvāca sādhvalaṅkṛtau suvasanau pariñkṛtau*  
*bhūtvodaśarāve 'vekñethām iti. tau ha sādhv alaṅkṛtau suvasanau*  
*pariñkṛtau bhūtvodaśarāve 'vekñāñ cakrāte. tau ha prajāpatir uvāca kiṁ*  
*paśyatha iti.*

**Перевод:**

**Prajāpati said to them, “Decorating yourselves well, wearing fine clothing, being well groomed, look in the plate of water.”**

**They decorated themselves, put on fine clothing, and further groomed themselves. They looked into the plate of water.**

**Prajāpati said, “What do you see?”**

### 8.8.3

*tau hocatuḥ – yathaivedam āvāṁ bhagavaḥ sādhv alaṅkṛtau suvasanau*  
*pariñkṛtau svaḥ evam evemau bhagavaḥ sādhvalaṅkṛtau suvasanau*  
*pariñkṛtāv iti. eña ātmeti hovācaitad amṛtam abhayam etad brahmeti. tau*  
*ha śānta-hṛdayau pravavrajatuḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**They said, “Revered sir! We have become well decorated, well clothed and well groomed. We see these reflections well decorated, well clothed and well groomed.”**

**He said, “This is ātmā. This is full of bliss, devoid of all suffering, full of knowledge.”**

**Satisfied in heart, they left.**

**Комментарий:**

Prajāpati’s intention of having them decorate themselves and looking at their reflections was as follows. Seeing defects in their bodies with long hair and nails from observing brahmacarya for thirty-two years and seeing defects in the decorations reflected, they should understand that their bodies were not ātmā and were devoid of full bliss, full knowledge and absence of all suffering. Though seeing the reflections twice, because of remaining impurities, they did not see the faults and could not dissipate their illusion of thinking that the ātmā was reflected in the water. They did not understand his intention because of their impurities.

He should not say directly, “You are mistaken.” Pointing to the reflection he said “This is the Lord” while holding in his mind the Lord within all beings. In this way the two would feel satisfied. In time when their impurities were dissipated, they would understand the meaning of his words. Wanting to know, they would perhaps come again to him.

### 8.8.4

*tau hānvīkñya prajāpatir uvāca - anupalabhyātmānam ananuvidya*  
*vrajataḥ. yatara etad upaniñado bhaviñyanti devā vā asurā vā, te*  
*parābhaviñyantīti.*  
*sa ha śānta-hṛdaya eva virocano 'surān jagāma. tebhyo haitām upaniñadaṁ provāca - ātmaiveha mahayya ātmā parīcaryaḥ. Ātmānam eveha mahayann ātmānaṁ paricarann ubhau lokāvav āpnotīmañ cāmuñ ceti*

**Перевод:**

**Seeing them, Prajāpati said, “They are going away without knowing or realizing the ātmā.**

**Whoever accepts this teaching whether he is devatā or demon, will be vanquished by saṁsāra.”**

**Satisfied, Virocana went to the demons. He spoke that teaching to them. “Ātmā (the body) is to be worshipped. The body is to be served. Worshipping and serving this body, one will attain both this world and the other world.”**

**Комментарий:**

Prajāpati thought as they were going away, “These two should not get destroyed by having faith in this false instruction.” Hoping they would hear from others just as they had first heard his statement about ātmā, he spoke. The two, without knowing the svarūpa of the ātmā through hearing and contemplation, are leaving. Whether devatā or demon, whoever becomes convinced of this mistaken idea from the teachings of these two will be constantly afflicted by saṁsāra (parābhaviñyanti).

Virocana taught that the body is the ātmā after seeing two times the body reflected in the water, with similarity to the body. He taught that one should serve the body with decorations. The body should be worshipped (mahavyaḥ). One will experience the happiness of all worlds in a material body.

### 8.8.5

*tasmād apy adyehādadānam aśraddadhānam ayajamānam āhuḥ, āsuro*  
*bateti. asurāṇāṁ hy eñopaniñat. pretasya śarīraṁ bhikñayā*  
*vasanenālaṅkāreṇeti saṁskurvanti. etena hy amuṁ lokaṁ jeñyanto*  
*manyante.*

**Перевод:**

**Therefore, even today concerning a person who does not give, who has no faith and who does not perform sacrifices, they say “He is a demon.” It is because this is the teaching of the demons. When the body dies, they decorate the body with ornaments and cloth by begging. They think that they will attain a heavenly planet by this.**

**Комментарий:**

Because he taught this to the demons (tasmāt), even today in this world this tradition continues. They decorate the dead body because they think the body is ātmā. By decorating the corpse they think they will attain a good destination.

### 9

### 8.9.1

*atha hendro 'prāpyaiva devān etad bhayaṁ dadarśa - yathaiva*  
*khalv ayam asmin śarīre sādhv alaṅkṛto sādhv alaṅkṛto bhavati, suvasane*  
*suvasanaḥ, pariñkṛte pariñkṛtaḥ, evam evāyam asminn andhe 'ndho*  
*bhavati, srāme srāmaḥ, parivṛkṇe parivṛkṇaḥ. asyaiva śarīrasya*  
*nāśamanv eña naśyati. Nāham atra bhogyaṁ paśyamīti.*

**Перевод:**

**Before going to the devatās, Indra saw a defect. The reflected image becomes well decorated when the body is well decorated. It is well clothed when the body is well clothed. It is cleansed nicely when the body is cleansed nicely. It is blind when the body is blind. It has a runny nose when the body has a runny nose. It is crippled when the body is crippled.**

**It is destroyed when the body is destroyed. I see no good in this.**

**Комментарий:**

Because Indra had a pure mind, he did not mistake the body for the ātmā. Arriving at his kingdom, but before teaching the devatās, while on the path, he saw a defect (bhayam) in the reflection of the body. Pariñkṛta means cutting nails and hair. Srāmaḥ means runny nose. Parivṛkna means deficient in hands or feet. There is no happiness, freedom from suffering in this.

### 8.9.2

*sa samitpāṇiḥ punar eyāya. taṁ ha prajāpatir uvāca - maghavan*  
*yac chānta-hṛdayaḥ prāvrājīḥ, sārdhaṁ virocanena, kim icchan punar āgam*  
*iti. sa hovāca yathaiva khalv ayaṁ bhagavo 'smin śarīre sādhv alaṅkṛte*  
*sādhv alaṅkṛte bhavati, suvasane suvasanaḥ, pariñkṛte pariñkṛtaḥ, evam evāyam asminn andhe 'ndho bhavati, srāme srāmaḥ, parivṛkṇe parivṛkṇo 'syaiva śarīrasya nāśaman veña naśyati. Nāham atra bhogyaṁ paśyāmīti.*

**Перевод:**

**With wood in his hands, he again went to Prajāpati. Prajāpati said, “O Indra! Since you went along with Virocana with contented heart, why do you again come?”**

**Indra said, “Revered sir! The reflected image becomes well decorated when the body is well decorated. It is well clothed when the body is well clothed. It is cleansed nicely when the body is cleansed nicely. It is blind when the body is blind. It has a runny nose when the body has a runny nose. It is crippled when the body is crippled. It is destroyed when the body is destroyed. I see no good in this.”**

### 8.9.3

*Evam evaiña maghavann iti hovāca, etaṁ tv eva te bhūyo 'nuvyākhyāsyāmi. vasāparāṇi dvātriṁśataṁ varñāṇīti. sa hāparāṇi dvātriṁśataṁ varñāṇy uvāsā tasmai hovāca.*

**Перевод:**

**Prajāpati said, “O Indra! That is so. I will explain it to you again. Live for thirty-two years more as a student.” He lived for thirty-two more years as a student. Prajāpati spoke to him.**

**Комментарий:**

What you say is true. I will again teach you about ātmā without fault. To purify your mind, live as a brahmacārī for thirty-two years. Indra did this. Prajāpati then spoke to him.

### 10

### 8.10.1

*ya eña svapne mahīyamān aścarati, eña ātmeti hovācaitad amṛtam abhayam etat brahmeti. sa ha śānta-hṛdayaḥ pravavrāja. sa hāprāpyaiva devān etad bhayaṁ dadarśa. Tad yady apīdaṁ śarīram andhaṁ bhavati, anandhaḥ sa bhavati; yadi srāmam, asrāmaḥ; naivaiño 'sya doñeṇa duñyati.*

**Перевод:**

**Prajāpati said, “He who acts grandly in a dream is ātmā. He is full of bliss, devoid of suffering and full of knowledge.”**

**Indra was satisfied in his heart and went away. But before going to the devatās he saw a defect. When the body is blind, the body in the dream is not blind. When the body has a runny nose, the dream body does not have a runny nose. When the body has defect, the dream body has no defect.**

**Комментарий:**

In a dream the person is served by women etc. and seems to be enjoying as ātmā. He is not affected by the defects of the body.

### 8.10.2

*na vadhenāsya hanyate, nāsya srāmyeṇa srāmaḥ. ghnanti tv evainaṁ*  
*vicchādayantīva; apriyavetteva bhavati; api roditīva. Nāham atra*  
*bhogyaṁ paśyāmīti.*

**Перевод:**

**When the body is injured, the dream body is not injured. When the body has runny nose, the dream body does not. But people seem to beat him in dreams. They seem to chase him. He experiences suffering and weeps. I do not see any good in this.**

### 8.10.3

*sa samitpāṇiḥ punar eyāya. taṁ ha prajāpatir uvāca - maghavan*  
*yac chānta-hṛdayaḥ prāvrājīḥ, kim icchan puna rāgama iti. sa hovāca*  
*tad yady apīdaṁ bhagavaḥ śarīram andhaṁ bhavaty anandhaḥ sa bhavati,*  
*yadi srāmam asrāmo naivaiño 'sya doñeṇa duñyati.*

**Перевод:**

**With wood in his hands, he again went to Prajāpati. Prajāpati said, “O Indra! Since you went away with contented heart, why do you again come?”**

**Indra said, “Revered sir! When the body is blind, the body in the dream is not blind. When the body has a runny nose, the dream body does not have a runny nose. When the body has defect, the dream body has no defect.”**

### 8.10.4

*na vedhenāsyahanyate nāsya srāmye (me) ṇa srāmaḥ. ghnanti tv evainaṁ*  
*vicchādayantīva. apriyavetteva bhavati. api roditīva. Nāham atra bhogyaṁ*  
*paśyāmīti.*  
*Evam evaiña maghavann iti hovāca. etaṁ tv eva te bhūyo '*  
*nuvyākhyāsyāmi vasāparāṇi dvātriṁśataṁ varñāṇīti. sa hāparāṇi*  
*dvātriṁśataṁ varñāṇy uvāsa. tasmai hovāca.*

**Перевод:**

**“When the body is injured, the dream body is not injured. When the body has runny nose, the dream body does not. But people seem to beat him in dreams. They seem to chase him. He experiences suffering and weeps. I do not see any good in this.**

**Prajāpati said, “O Indra! That is so. I will explain it to you again. Live for thirty-two years more as a student.” He lived for thirty-two more years as a student. Prajāpati spoke to him.**

### 11

### 8.11.1

*Tad yatraitat suptaḥ samastaḥ saṁprasannaḥ svapnaṁ na vijānāti, eña*  
*ātmeti hovāca – etad amṛtam abhayam etad brahmeti. sa ha śānta-hṛdayaḥ*  
*pravavrāja. sa hāprāpyaiva devān etad bhayaṁ dadarśa -nāha*  
*khalv ayam evaṁ saṁpraty ātmānaṁ jānāty ayam aham asmīti; no evemāni*  
*bhūtāni. Vināśam evāpīto bhavati. Nāham atra bhogyaṁ paśyāmīti.*

**Перевод:**

**He said, “When a person, having withdrawn his sense, sleeps peacefully and does not see dreams-- that is ātmā. That is full of bliss, devoid of suffering, full of knowledge.”**

**Indra was satisfied in his heart and went away. But before going to the devatās he saw a defect. This person in dreamless state does not know himself: “I am this.” He does not know other beings. He gets destroyed. I do not see good in this.**

**Комментарий:**

Aha indicates astonishment. Because the person in deep sleep, at that time (samprati) does not have specific knowledge of “I am this person” and is not aware of other beings, he is as if nonexistent or destroyed (vināśam eva) in that state.

### 8.11.2

*sa samitpāṇiḥ punar eyāya. taṁ ha prajāpatir uvāca - maghavan*  
*yac chānta-hṛdayaḥ prāvrājīḥ kim ivecchan punar āgama iti. sa hovāca*  
*nāha khalv ayaṁ bhagava evaṁ saṁpraty ātmānaṁ*  
*jānāty ayam aham asmīti, no evemāni bhūtāni. Vināśam evāpīto bhavati.*  
*nāhamatra bhogyaṁ paśyāmīti.*  
*Evam evaiña maghann iti hovāca, etaṁ tveva te bhūyo 'nuvyākhyāsyāmi no evānyatraitasmāt. vasāparāṇi pañca varñāṇīti. sa hāparāṇi pañca varñāṇy uvāsa. Tāny ekaśataṁ saṁpeduḥ.*  
*etat tat, yad āhuḥ, ekaśataṁ ha vai varñāṇi maghavān prajāpatau brahmacaryam uvāsa. tasmai hovāca.*

**Перевод:**

**With wood in his hands, he again went to Prajāpati. Prajāpati said, “O Indra! Since you went away with contented heart, why do you again come?”**

**Indra said, “Revered sir! This person in dreamless state does not know himself: “I am this.” He does not know other beings. He gets destroyed. I do not see good in this.”**

**Prajāpati said, “O Indra! That is so. I will explain it to you again. There is nothing other than this. Live for five years more as a student.” He lived for fire more years as a student.**

**The total was a hundred and one years. Thus they say that Indra lived for a hundred and one years as a brahmacārī with Prajāpati.**

**Prajāpati spoke to him.**

**Комментарий:**

Prajāpati tested Indra’s qualification for teachings of the pure ātmā, purifying him with a hundred and one years of brahmacarya. Then he taught him. One should teach to a qualified person, purified by long years of brahmacarya.

### 12

### 8.12.1

*maghavan martyaṁ vā idaṁ śarīram āttaṁ mṛtyunā.*  
*Tad asyāmṛtasyāśarīrasyātmano 'dhiñṭhānam. ātto vai saśarīraḥ*  
*priyāpriyābhyām. na ha vai saśarīrasya sataḥ priyāpriyayorapahatir asti.*  
*aśarīraṁ vāva santaṁ na priyāpriye spṛśataḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**O Indra! This body is mortal. It is affected by death. It is the abode of the ātmā, which is immortal and without body. Endowed with a body one is affected by happiness and suffering. For a person with a body there is no destruction of material happiness and suffering. That which is without body is peaceful. It is not touched by happiness and suffering.**

**Комментарий:**

The body has the quality of dying. Not only that, but it has the quality of always being destructible (mṛtyunā āttam). It is pervaded by death. This is stated to show the detestable nature of the body. It is the enjoying place of the ātmā, whose svarūpa is joy, different from the body controlled by karma. The jīva accepts a body for enjoyment, under control of karma. The ātmā is without a body and is full of joy (amṛtasya). Having a body is the opposite.

Liberation (in the spiritual world) is the goal, not simply being without a body. However having no body means no suffering.

Since one must necessarily experience happiness and suffering according to a body produced by karma, if one has no relationship with the body caused by karma, one attains liberation, the puruñārtha. That person is not touched by happiness or suffering.

Vāva means it is well known, or is used for emphasis.

### 8.12.2

*aśarīro vāyuḥ; abhraṁ vidyut stanayitnuḥ aśarīrāṇyetāni. Tad yathaitāni*  
*amuñmādākāśāt samutthāya paraṁ jyotir-upasampadya svena*  
*rūpeṇābhiniñpadyante, evam evaiña saṁprasādo 'smāccharīrāt samutthāya*  
*paraṁ jyotirupasaṁpadya svena rūpeṇābhiniñpadyate.*

**Перевод:**

**Air has no body. A cloud, lightning, and thunder have no bodies. Just as, rising from the sky, assuming their pure states, they manifest their pure forms, the jīva, leaving the body, attaining the Lord, becomes endowed with his own form.**

**Комментарий:**

Vāyu has no head, hands etc. Abhra means a water bearing cloud. The cloud and lightning are different states of water. Things without bodies are not connected with happiness and suffering. Air etc. rise from the sky. They rise to a higher level for performing their functions. The word para means the state of air etc. in their causal aspect. Jyoti indicates the reason for manifesting effects in those states. Approaching their causal substances, air etc. give up their state of being an effect, and adopt forms similar to their causal states. Giving up its seven forms such as avāha and pravaha, air remains as just air. Lightning, giving up its qualities of lightning, remains as light alone. Similarly with cloud and thunder.

In the same way, the jīva by a special path, attains the supreme Lord endowed with his special place. Giving up his condition of saṁsāra, he manifests a form similar to the Lord’s. This form is additional to the jīva but is his own. He does not attain his form as ātmā since he is ātmā eternally.

### 8.12.3

*sa uttamaḥ puruñaḥ. sa tatra paryeti jakñat krīḍan ramamāṇaḥ strībhirvā*  
*yānairvā jñātibhirvā nopajanaṁ smaran idaṁ śarīram. sa yathā prayogya*  
*ācaraṇe yuktaḥ, evam evāyam asmin śarīre prāṇo yuktaḥ.*

**Перевод:**

**The Lord is the supreme person. The jīva follows him, eats, plays enjoys with women or vehicle or relatives, and does not remember his material body which was associated with relatives. As horses are yoked to a chariot and are controlled by a charioteer, the conditioned jīva endowed with prāṇa is yoked to this body.**

**Комментарий:**

Uttamaḥ puruña is the person to be attained (saḥ). The attainer is different from him. The attainer (saḥ) experiences the Lord (tatra) everywhere (literally roams about in him). Or he follows the Lord. Upajanam means the vile body that associated with relatives, or the body produced by interaction of man and woman. Though in liberated state the jīva knows everything, and can experience the body which has been given up, he does not experience the suffering of that body. Since he does not have a material body, he has no attachment to a relationship with that previous body.

Like a horse or oxen yoked (prayogya) to a cart (ācaraṇe) or to a rope, the jīva accompanied by prāṇa is yoked to the ropes of karma in his state of saṁsāra. The example emphasizes the difference between the body and the jīva.

### 8.12.4

*atha yatraitad ākāśam anuviñaṇṇaṁ cakñuḥ, sa cākñuñaḥ puruñaḥ;*  
*darśanāya cakśuḥ. atha yo vededaṁ jighrāṇīti, sa ātmā; gandhāya*  
*ghrāṇam. atha yo vededam abhivyāharāṇīti, sa ātmā; abhivyāhārāya vāk.*  
*yo vededaṁ śṛṇavānīti, sa ātmā; śravaṇāya śrotram.*

**Перевод:**

**When the eye reveals light or form, the jīva uses the eye as his instrument. The eye is his instrument for seeing. He who knows that he is smelling is the ātmā. The nose is his instrument for smelling. He who knows that he is speaking is the ātmā. The voice is his instrument for speaking. He who knows that he is hearing is the ātmā. The ear is his instrument for hearing.**

**Комментарий:**

Ākāśam means light. Or it means form, that which is revealed. The eye reveals light or form. Or the eye functions as the revealer of form. The statements show that the jīva, the knower, is different from the senses of the body and the objects of knowledge.

### 8.12.5

*atha yo vededaṁ manvānīti, sa ātmā; mano 'sya divyaṁ cakñuḥ. sa vā*  
*eña etena divyena cakñuñā manasaitān kāmān paśyan ramate, ya ete*  
*brahmaloke.*

**Перевод:**

**He who knows that he thinks is the ātmā. His mind is only his internal eye. By this internal eye, the mind, he sees and enjoys attractive qualities which are situated in the Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

This jīva (sa eñaḥ) devoid of body and senses caused by karma, the puruña, by natural (divyena) knowledge (cakñuñā), experiences all attractive qualities situated in the daharākāśa (brahma-loke), the supreme Lord.

### 8.12.6

*taṁ vā etaṁ devā ātmānam upāsate. tasmāt teñāṁ sarve ca lokā āttāḥ,*  
*sarve ca kāmā.*  
*sa sarvāṁś ca lokān āpnoti sarvāṁś ca kāmān, yas tam ātmānam anuvidya*  
*vijānātīti ha prajāpatir uvāca prajāpatir uvāca.*

**Перевод:**

**The devatās meditated on the ātmā. They attained all worlds and all desires.**

**He who knows and realizes this ātmā attains all worlds and all desires. Thus Prajāpati spoke.**

**Комментарий:**

Since the ātmā was taught to Indra, the devatās hearing that instruction on ātmā from Indra, meditated on ātmā. Attaining all planets and all desires, they also realized Brahman. The demons however did not achieve this result. Even today, those who meditate as Indra did attain the same results. What is the proof? Prajāpati spoke.

The teachings about the jīva are part of the teachings about Brahman, the Lord. The result of Brahma-vidyā, attaining all planets etc., is here indicated as the result of ātmā-vidyā in order to praise it, as an aṅga of Brahma-vidyā. One should now the svarūpa of the jīva if one desires to attain the Lord.

### 13

### 8.13.1

*śyāmācchabalaṁ prapadye śabalācchayāmaṁ prapadye.*  
*aśve iva romāṇi vidhūya pāpa candra iva rāhor mukhāt pramucya.*  
*dhūtvā śarīram akṛtaṁ kṛtātmā brahma-lokam abhisambhavāmīti abhi-*  
*saṁbhavāmīti.*

**Перевод:**

**Meditating on the dark blue Lord, I meditate on his form endowed with jīvas and matter. Meditating on his form endowed with jīvas and matter, I meditate on his dark blue form.**

**Shedding sins like a horse shaking his mane, and shedding the material body, like the moon freeing himself from Rāhu’s mouth, I, being successful, attain the eternal Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

These mantras are to be chanted by those in knowledge as an aṅga of knowledge. Three types of meditation on the Lord are described: in his svarūpa, being in possession of the jīvas and being in possession of matter. In meditation on the Lord’s svarūpa, one meditates on his attractive form endowed with auspicious qualities like knowledge. His spiritual body endowed with lotus eyes is described. Nīlatoyada-madhyasthā vidyul lekheva bhasvarā: The Lord shines like a flash of lighting within a blue cloud. (Nārāyana-sukta)

Meditating on the dark blue form of the Lord (śyāmāt), I meditate on his form endowed with jīvas and matter (śabalam). Meditating on the Lord’s form endowed with jīvas and matter, I meditate on his dark blue form. The meaning of the statement is that one should constantly meditate on the Lord in these three forms. Being successful (kṛtātmā), I attain (abhisambhavāmi) the eternal (akṛtam) Lord (brahma-lokam).

### 14

### 8.14.1

*ākāśo vai nāma nāmarūpayor nirvahitā, te yad antarā tat brahma*  
*tad amṛtaṁ sa ātmā.*  
*prajāpateḥ sabhāṁ veśma prapadye. yaśo 'haṁ bhavāmi brāhmaṇānāṁ*  
*yaśo rājñāṁ yaśo viśāṁ yaśo 'ham anu prāpatsi. sa hāhaṁ yaśasāṁ yaśaḥ*  
*śvetam adatkam adatkaṁ śvetaṁ lindu mā 'bhigām lindu mā 'bhigām.*

**Перевод:**

**He who is famous as Ākāśa develops names and forms. Brahman who is within those names is blissful. He is ātmā. I meditate on the residence of the Lord. The Lord is the ātmā of the brāhmaṇas, kñatriyas, and vaiśyas. I have attained that Lord. That Lord is the ātmā of the jīvas. May I not be born through semen in a toothless state that destroys sattva.**

**Комментарий:**

Prajāpateḥ means “of the Lord.” Yaśaḥ means ātmā. Aham refers to the Lord who is one’s antaryāmī. The Lord is the ātmā of the brāhmaṇas. I have attained (prāpatsi) that Lord (yaśaḥ). That Lord (aham) is the ātmā (yāśaḥ) of the jīvas (yaśasām).

Śvetam means the white dhātu, semen. It also represents sattva-guṇa. Adatkam means literally “without teeth.” Adatkam also means devourer. May I not attain the womb (lindu), entering through semen. May I not attain birth, toothless and a state that devours sattva.

### 15

### 8.15.1

*taddhaitat brahmā prajāpataya uvāca, prajāpatir manave manuḥ*  
*prajābhyaḥ. Ācārya-kulāt vedam adhītya yathā vidhānaṁ guroḥ*  
*karmātiśeñeṇābhisamāvṛtya kuṭumbe śucau deśe svādhyāyam adhīyāno*  
*dhārmikān vidadhat ātmani sarvendriyāṇi saṁpratiñṭhāpyāhiṁsan sarvā*  
*bhūtāny anyatra tīrthebhyaḥ sa khalv evaṁ vartayan yāvad āyuñaṁ*  
*brahma-lokam abhisaṁpadyate na ca punar āvartate na ca punar āvartate.*

**Перевод:**

**Brahmā spoke this knowledge to Kaśyapa. Kaśyapa spoke it to Manu. Manu spoke it to his sons. After studying the Vedas in the house of the ācārya, completing his duties to the guru according to the rules, leaving his studies, becoming a householder, he should become proficient at studies. He should make his students and children follow dharma. He should fix all his senses in the Lord, practice nonviolence to all beings, except in sacrifices. Living his whole life in this manner, he will attain the Lord and not return.**

**Комментарий:**

Performance of karmas are described as an aṅga of knowledge. The student should study the Vedas with the guru, serving him at all times (karmātiśeñena). He should perform his activities always in accordance to the rules of the ācārya.

Anyatra tīrthebhyaḥ means nonviolence except during sacrifices. This is permitted according to aśuddham iti cen na śabdāt: If one argues that the jīva has some bad karma to experience by being born as a plant because of violence in the sacrifice, it is not so, because Vedic statements allow that violence. (Vedānta-sūtra [3.1.26](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vs/3.1.26))

Na vā u etan mriyase na riñyasi: by this sacrifice you do not die, you are not destroyed. (Yajur Veda) Since violence is unavoidable for householders in acts like picking tulasi etc. that is also included as an exception.

He should live his life till death following this conduct. This applies not only to the householder seeking the Lord, but to a person of any āśrama who seeks the Lord.

Kṛtsna-bhāvāt tu gṛhiṇopasaṁhāraḥ: Because the household āśrama contains rules of all āśramas, it alone is mentioned in the passage concerning realization of Brahman. (Vedānta-sūtra [3.4.48](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vs/3.4.48)) This person does not return as confirmed in Vedānta-sūtra [4.4.22](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vs/4.4.22).

anāvṛttiḥ śabdāt anāvṛttiḥ śabdāt: There is no return from the Lord’s abode, because of scriptural statements. The liberated person has no cause of desiring to return to the control of prakṛti. The desire to return to this world is impossible since the person experiences the Lord who is the very form of the highest, infinite bliss. Nor does the Lord desire the devotee’s return to this world since he considers attaining the devotee to be the greatest acquisition. Returning to this world cannot be caused by karma, since karma has been completely annihilated. The repetition indicates that the discussion of Brahma-vidyā with its aṅgas has been completed.

### 8.15.2

*āpyāyantu mamāṅgāni vāk prāṇaścakñuḥ śrotramatho balami - ndriyāṇi*  
*ca sarvāṇi sarvaṁ brahmau (hmo) paniñadaṁ mā 'haṁ brahma*  
*nirākuryāṁ mā mā brahma nirākarot anirākaraṇamastu anirākaraṇaṁ*  
*me 'stu tadātmani nirate ya upaniñatsu dharmāste mayi santu te mayi*  
*santu.*

**Перевод:**

**Om! O para brahma, Paramātmā! By your mercy may all my limbs, power of speech, life airs, eyes, ears--all my action senses and knowledge gathering senses-- all my bodily and mental powers and strength, attain nourishment. All the Upaniñads are non-different from Brahman itself, from you.**

**May I never reject the Lord described in the Upaniñads; may the Lord never reject me.**

**May my relationship with you remain unbreakable. May your relationship with me remain unbreakable.**

**May all the principles enunciated in the Upaniñads remain in me, who am absorbed in the Lord.**

**May they always remain within me.**

**O Paramātmā! May my threefold miseries be destroyed.**
